# vocabulary-model — each word described by its readings (2026-10-15 → 2026-10-18)

The vocabulary was a set of base sets plus marker subsets (`particles` and `particles_ambiguous`, `suffix_acronyms` and `suffix_acronyms_ambiguous`, `suffix_words` and `honorific_tails`, `titles` and `given_name_titles`, `conjunctions` and `conjunctions_ambiguous`), and the pairs did not work as a model. "Ambiguous" meant three different things by field (may be a given name, may be a surname, may be an initial), and two of the markers were not ambiguities at all. The direction flipped between layers: config/ marked the never-given particles while `Lexicon` marks the may-be-given ones. Every marked entry was written twice and the checks on the pairs were uneven (a `Lexicon` raise for some, an import-time assert for others, nothing for `conjunctions_ambiguous`). A clash between categories (`do` as a particle and as a credential) was recorded nowhere, visible only because two sets happened to overlap, and nothing told an author whether an overlap they created was intended (`titles ∩ particles`, the title/suffix duals) or forbidden (acronyms ∩ words). Language packs need to say which language a word belongs to, and sets cannot.

## Standing

- The vocabulary redesign comes before adding many packs: it is designed for 3.0, and what is safe is pulled forward in four phases, (1) the data format and a compiler, snapshot-neutral, (2) derived ambiguity with the gate on every reading kind, (3) pack semantics, (4) the rules menu, order and shapes, and the output model — 2026-10-15/16.
- Each word is described by its readings (word → reading → properties), each kind belonging to one category that decides the compiled field it feeds; detailed kinds are recorded even where the kinds of a category behave alike, generation and credential being separate kinds among the suffix category's six (`generation`, `credential`, `honorific`, `professional_honorific`, `status`, `courtesy`) — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-18 #737.
- A language file holds the words the parser might meet in names written in that language, immigrant particles included, not the words of its etymology, and a word sits in the file of every language whose names it occurs in, a Latin transliteration in its source language's file; `mul.toml` holds the words shared on purpose instead (the generations `i` to `v`, `2` and `jr` with `junior` and `jnr`, `&`, the European titles below, and the degrees and salutations every country's data writes alike), a word joining it where it harms no language and would otherwise be duplicated into every language's file — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-18 #737; 2026-10-19 #749; 2026-10-19 #750, title kinds; 2026-11-02 #1005 (Derek); 2026-11-03 #1005, the strict-gap batches (Derek).
- The default loads every language and keeps working for names of any language. Choosing a language narrows it since 3.0's phase 3 (#753): a pack settles only the clashes where its language has a vocabulary reading (`locales.VI` excepted: its fragment removes readings of Vietnamese syllables no vi.toml reading can settle, 2026-11-05 #1108), a strict choice reads only the chosen languages' files, `mul.toml` and native-script words' readings as a plain choice of the same languages keeps them (name readings included), and a pack's preferred reading can outrank one a shape rule derives — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-30 #753, file-strict; 2026-11-02 #1005 (Derek); 2026-11-07 #1148.
- `e`, `y` and `i` stay in the default; choosing a language settles their one-case fork for that language's own reading, `may_be_initial` not holding on a chosen file's conjunction, as `locales.PT` and `locales.PT_BR` do for `e` — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b; 2026-10-30 #753, phase 3g.
- A patronymic field came in 3.0, as a view that names its words without emptying `family`, and the particle kinds already draw the `clan`/`patronymic` line for it: a patronymic is followed by a parent's name, a clan prefix by a family name the lineage shares — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-19 #749; 2026-10-28 #754 (Derek); landed as rules/R9, 2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4c.
- A shipped pack that is only a choice of languages declares no `DEVIATES`, its surface the words its choice changes; `ga` turns on `nic`, `mhic`, `mic` and `uí` as clan particles, `gd` `nic`, and `pt-BR` `sb` as Sobrinho's spelling, while the single letters `f` and `n` are excluded, the pack included, the letter abbreviating a surname more often than the agnomen in the filings that write it — 2026-10-30 #753, phase 3h.
- The Breton article `ar` is a reading of `br.toml` written `default = false`, read by a choice of `br` that no pack makes, and `M.` (Monsieur) is excluded, a French choice included, the letter an initial in every name data source measured; fr `père` is read by default (decisions/generational-words), so no French pack ships — 2026-11-03 #658.
- `es` turns on `doña`, the courtesy title that leads a Spanish name with no complement and is a family name too, the other titles phase 3g turned on being read by default since 2026-11-06 #1115 (below); `don`, `dona` and `dom` are excluded, the packs included, names opening with each as a name more often than as the title; `maestro`, a family name whose form of address is written `Mtro.`, is excluded too, and so is `h`, the hijo an initial is written as; `sr` keeps a generation reading beside its salutation in both files, so a choice leaves it the dual position decides; no connective continues a title run, the shape unwritten in name data, and the office titles that take a complement stay out — 2026-10-30 #753, phase 3g.
- `es` turns on `maestra` too, the form of address written out, `maestro` staying excluded; `profesor` and `profesora`, which phase 3g turned on, are read by default since 2026-11-03 (decisions/salutation-titles) — 2026-11-03 #980.
- The titles phase 3f, phase 3g and #1108 wrote `default = false` for neutrality rather than for a collision are read by default, 27 words, 36 readings, the nine doctorates being written in cs.toml and sk.toml both: es `lic`, `licenciado`, `licenciada`, `lcdo`, `lcda`, `ingeniero`, `ingeniera`, `arq`, `mtro`, `mtra`, `doctora`; pt `doutor`, `doutora`, `professora`, `profa`; cs and sk `judr`, `mudr`, `phdr`, `rndr`, `mvdr`, `mddr`, `paeddr`, `pharmdr`, `thdr`; vi `ths`, `đbqh`, `dbqh`. A choice still turns on the titles a name or initials spell: es `maestra` and `doña`, cs `mga` and `bca`, `pan`, `mgr`'s degree, vi `gs`, `ts` and `pgs` — 2026-11-06 #1115.
- Of the romanized titles a pack's phase lists, one whose native spelling is a default title turns on unless, counted in persons and pooled across every population measured, names open with it more often than it opens one as the title; a spelling outside the phase's list (`Shreemati`, `Srimati`) stays open until a phase writes it as a reading: `hi` turns on `smt`, `shrimati` and `shriyut`, `bn` `smt`, `shrimati` and `adhyapak` (`shriyut` with no bearer anywhere, `adhyapak` with family-name bearers only, every one where the title claim, acting at the head, does not reach), while `shri`, opening given names in every population measured, stays excluded, and `sushri`, an Odia given name, is a `default = false` salutation in hi.toml with the Odia name declared in or.toml, so the plain `hi` choice, made for data that holds those names, reads it as a title only inside a title phrase and a strict one as the title (decisions/indic-honorifics, 2026-11-03 #345/#870) — 2026-10-30 #753, phase 3e.
- A language pack is a choice of languages on `Locale.languages` selecting which readings compile, a tag choosing its own file and its ancestors', and a reading index, loaded on its first lookup, carries the languages to run time; strict is file-strict, the chosen files, `mul.toml` and native-script words' readings only, `und.toml` not among them, so it needs no 3i; third-party language files are deferred — 2026-10-27 #753 (Derek); 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3a (Derek); 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b; 2026-10-30 #753, file-strict; 2026-11-02 #1005 (Derek); 2026-11-07 #1148.
- A strict choice is `parser_for(..., strict=True)` over the whole choice, filtering the base's readings as a plain choice does, the caller's own edits applying after it, and a chained call keeping its base's strictness unless it passes `strict`, an explicit `False` on a strict base raising and an explicit `True` making a plain base's choice strict; a strict call whose packs choose no language raises; a strict choice accepts a tag whose file holds no reading, which a plain one refuses as changing nothing; a bare choice has no constructor, no `locales.choose`; and the words strict ungates are derived against the plain choice of the same tags and held to a reviewed set by a test — 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j; 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-c.
- A choice is matched without regard to case, a tag whose file does not exist selects its ancestors' (`pt-PT` selects `pt`), `mul` and `und` are no language to choose, and a vocabulary reading turned on also prefers (a name reading never does, decision 15), so `de`'s generation `i` drops `ca`'s and `pl`'s conjunction under a `de` choice; the `Lexicon` records its choice, so that a choice chained through `base=` unions with the base's, and a caller's own edits apply after the choice, plain or strict, in the order written, a pack's fragment joining before the caller's lexicon — 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b; 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface (Derek); 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-c.
- A reading right in its own language and wrong only as a default, because another language written in the same script collides with it, is written `default = false` in its file, never excluded, so that after the packs an exclusion means wrong everywhere: cs `st` since phase 2h, and cs `ml`, hr, sk and sl `ml` and `st` since 3c (fr `père`, converted with them, is read by default since 2026-11-03, a collision inside French), the rest in their packs' PRs — 2026-10-27 #753 (Derek); 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3c.
- A name reading written `default = false` derives no marker in the default and counts for no check guarding the default's compile; a particle whose word's only `given` readings are off is refused until a pack needs one — 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3a.
- A caller configures vocabulary by reading, `Lexicon` deriving the sets and gates the build derives, and the compiled sets become internal in 3.0 — 2026-10-28 #754, the `Lexicon` surface (Derek).
- The configure-by-reading API: `add(*words, kind, **properties)` and `remove(*words, kind=None)`, the views reading a word's kind from the lexicon, `readings_of` returning a public `LexiconReading` and a repr counting readings by kind, a five-part state (base, ordered edit log, choice, lists, masks) compared structurally, a clash a later choice exposes raised at `parser_for` naming the choice, the choice recorded on the `Lexicon` with chained choices unioning, the default's base lazy, built in 4i-a to 4i-d with no default parse moving; since 3.1 a 2.x pickle is refused, naming the re-pickle under 3.0, and an old spelling refused naming docs/migrate.rst's table of kinds — 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface (Derek); 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-a; 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-b; 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-c; 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-d; 2026-11-08 #1003.
- A whole kind is taken out with `remove(kind=..., every=True)`, every word's readings of it going, those a later choice turns on included, and `words(kind=..., **properties)` lists the words holding a reading of a kind or category — 2026-11-02 #1007, a kind as a whole (Derek).
- Configured by reading, an `add` of a title reading that a `given` reading keeps a title only inside a phrase warns, naming `remove(w, kind="given")`, save one that only updates a held title reading's properties — 2026-11-01 #754, the title warning (Derek).
- A `Locale` may carry CLDR-shaped render patterns, display and sorting, a later addition whose shape is agreed; how a call reaches one is open — 2026-10-28 #754, render patterns.
- 3.0's output model keeps the seven roles and adds every new field as a view, read from `Token.reading`, the reading each token took, or for `surname2` from rules/O6's tag: `generation`, `credentials` and, since 2026-11-03, `honorifics` partitioning `suffix` (decisions/R8), `patronymic`, and `surname2` where rules/O6 read two family names; `middle` and `surnames` keep their meaning, a compound given name being a rule a caller or pack turns on; the default `str()` renders a name in the order it was read in with its written separators in a normalized form, through `render(spec: str | None = None)`, `None` being that default; a language file's `[rules]` compile into its `Locale`'s policy patch — 2026-10-28 #754 (Derek).
- `surname2` is read from O6's mark as `Token.reading` publishes it, `Reading.second_surname`, a field not compared, and not from a published tag, which would make an O6 parse unequal to its 2.x pickle (decisions/R10) — 2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4d.
- Gender is recorded only where a word gives a definitive clue: a feminine form or one half of a fixed pair, never an unmarked noun — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-19 #750, title kinds.
- Title-phrase modifiers count as titles only beside a head title, at the head of the name only and never in a comma part (`Wing Lau` → given 'Wing', `John Smith, Vice` → title 'Vice'), save a modifier that may be a given name, which the head's test reads in every part (`Lau, Wing Kai` → given 'Wing'), rules/H9 — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-19 #750, title kinds; 2026-10-23 #752; 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2i; 2026-10-26 #752, phase 2j, `wing`.
- Name readings are hand-declared, and only on a word some language file claims as vocabulary or refuses, so the vocabulary carries no surname list (the segmentation lists, Korean and the ZH pack's, are outside the per-word model) — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-19.
- Bearer counts are structured provenance (source and count), never used to compute a reading — 2026-10-15/16.
- Phase 2 put vocabulary, declared name and shape readings in one pool decided by one gate of four steps, floor, form, structure and count, asked at the sites that decide, four clashes and two floors (the 2026-10-23 entry's item 4), and derived `may_be_given` and `may_be_name` from declared name readings (a count-free one carrying a `note`, `ld` left ungated, `may_be_initial` kept) — 2026-10-23 #752. It shipped piecemeal, 2a to 2m, a credential's capitals landing through the pool — 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2g; 2026-10-26 #752, phase 2m. A configured `Lexicon` compiles its pool from its base's rows and its log since 3.0 — 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-b. The reading taken surfaces as `Token.reading` since 3.0 — 2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4a. The reading not taken surfaces as `Ambiguity.alternative` since 2.7 (decisions/A1) — 2026-10-26 #752, phase 2m.
- Every reading the parse took or a report names from the vocabulary names the shipped files holding it, `Reading.languages`, looked up at assemble in the readings of the parse's `Lexicon` (the reading index's rows for the default) by the word and the kind, a name by the field it lands in; the files whose reading is on in the `Lexicon` the parse used, so a choice names the files it reads and not those it drops, and none for a shape, position, a caller's own reading, or a word no file holds in that category — 2026-10-29 #754, 4a's follow-up; 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-d.
- Every numeral rule asks one predicate, `_vocab.reads_as_generation`, the vocabulary's own numerals read inside it; a numeral of two or more letters is the generation wherever the trailing run holds it, while a one-letter one before a credential stays the name word decisions/S10's #721 entry made it — 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2f.
- A credential written split across a space is one word, a member of the ambiguous class that the count decides, and the Ph./D. pair starts S8's run from where it stands (decisions/S12) — 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2e.
- A listed credential that is also a name, written in capitals in a name of mixed case, is settled as the credential by its writing: it reports nothing and starts the credential's run where no name word stands behind it with no family comma (2026-10-26, Derek), and keeps its other readings, the clash settled at the sites that choose a reading rather than by retagging the word (decisions/S6) — 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2g, #785.
- A member of the ambiguous class alone in brackets or quotes after the name is the suffix where its writing settles it, with a period inside it or in capitals in a name of mixed case, the case being the name's own words' (rules/S13, decisions/S13) — 2026-10-26 #752, phase 2l.
- Phase 2's title batches go in order of lone-head breakage in PSC, `buddha`, `siddha` and `gyani` in the default gate and `guru` left to a `ne` pack — 2026-10-24 #752.
- `Dean Smith` reads given `Dean`. The contested-head gate covers `dean`, `justice` and `major`, and a title that is also a given name gets a declared given reading one word at a time, in measured batches — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-23 #752; 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2i.
- Order and shape are separate: a language supplies only a default order, and the shapes (where titles, suffixes, particles and commas sit) belong to each order and live in the core; vocabulary readings are to settle a clash and name readings only to keep the gate on — 2026-10-15/16. Each order's shapes are listed as the arrangements it is written in, each pinned to what it reads, a reading a person might not give marked with the document that accepts it or the open issue that holds it — 2026-10-30 #754, sub-phase 4h.
- `cased` replaces `capitalization_exceptions` and, with `same_as`, a canonical spelling, and a reading's `native` spelling is for surfacing; `acronym` is a reading property replacing the acronym/word split, one suffix kind per word; `expands` records the meaning an entry was added for; `addresses` takes `family` and `given`, `full` and `any` being refused until a later phase — 2026-10-15/16; 2026-10-18 #737.
- The vocabulary is per-word TOML in `nameparser/vocab/<tag>.toml`, one file per BCP 47 tag plus `und` and `mul`, at `schema = 1` (a compiler refuses a newer one), every reading a table and no `language` key; `tools/vocab/build.py` compiles it into a committed `nameparser/config/_compiled.py`, the parser never reads the TOML, and parsing it at import was declined — 2026-10-18 #737.
- The marker sets are explicit properties on readings (`may_be_initial`, `attached`, `addresses`, `after_given`, `cased`), and `after_given` stands on a salutation reading only (decisions/H8) — 2026-10-18 #737; 2026-10-24 #835.
- The two markers that record a clash with a name are derived from name readings: an acronym suffix reading beside a `given` or `family` reading is an ambiguous acronym, a particle reading beside a `given` reading a particle that may be given, wherever in the files each reading sits. The build refuses `may_be_name` and `may_be_given = true`, naming the reading to write; a particle whose word has no `given` reading still states `may_be_given = false`, never defaulted; a name reading may stand without a count, with a `note` saying why; and a count kept as evidence against marking such a word goes in the clashing reading's `note` (`ld`). `may_be_initial` is not derived — 2026-10-25 #752, A CLASH WITH A NAME IS A NAME READING. A third marker is derived the same way: a suffix word reading beside a `given` or `family` reading is a suffix word that may be a name (`SUFFIX_WORDS_AMBIGUOUS`), and an `agnomen` reading needs a name reading on its word — 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2h.
- Exclusions are data: an `excluded` key refuses a kind, a category or a behavioral property, in the file of the language whose reading it refuses, and the build refuses the excluded thing in any file, quoting the reason — 2026-10-18 #737.
- A per-word fact that moves into the TOML is replaced in decisions/ by a query that finds it (decisions/README.md, edit kind 4) — 2026-10-18 #737.
- The script is read from the word, not the file: a script subtag is only for a difference the text cannot show — 2026-10-18 #737.
- The names reserved for a later use are build errors, not inert data; locale-pack vocabulary and the Korean surname list stay in Python — 2026-10-18 #737. `prefer` and `strict` are refused for good as data, a strength being the caller's — 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j. `bare_only`, `leading_only`, `trailing_only` and `separators` stay reserved, unbuilt, for 3.x, their build message naming the release — 2026-11-05 #1079; `default = false` records a reading that compiles to nothing, for a pack to turn on, and no exclusion refuses it (Spanish `h`, German `i` for the dotted regnal ordinal, Czech `st`) — 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2h. `attaches` names the title kinds, or the title words, a title reading stands before in a title phrase, compiled into the phrases it makes — 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2i; 2026-10-26 #752, phase 2i (Derek).
- A title reading beside a `given` reading, on a word with no particle reading, derives `TITLES_AMBIGUOUS` instead of `TITLES`: a title only inside a title phrase (rules/H9); a salutation's given-name counts kept as evidence for H8 sit in its `note`, marking nothing — 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2i.
- Every language file is in build-checked order (kind, then the `same_as` path from its root head, then the codepoints of the text), the check naming the entry to move, running last, and refusing a `same_as` loop of any length; `und.toml` is exempt — 2026-10-19 #747; 2026-10-21 #762.
- The particles take eight kinds beside the generic one, `expands` carrying a particle's gloss and `note` its vetting, and the particle keep-outs are exclusions — 2026-10-19 #749.
- The titles take ten kinds beside the generic one, `academic` and `occupation` divided by function rather than subject, and the title keep-outs are exclusions — 2026-10-19 #750, title kinds; 2026-10-19 #750, the rest of the titles.
- `wm` is no title: William's abbreviation reads as the given name it is (`Wm Smith` → given 'Wm'), and its title reading is refused in en.toml — 2026-10-22 #789. The dotted `Wm.` opening the given part reads given too, by the reading below — 2026-10-26 #789.
- A given name written short is a `given` reading whose `same_as` is the full name, normalized and naming no entry, a `family` reading taking none; the word compiles into `GIVEN_NAME_ABBREVIATIONS` (`Lexicon.given_name_abbreviations`), which outranks rules/H2's shape and nothing else (decisions/H2). The traditional English abbreviations in en.toml (`chas`, `wm`), the Filipino `ma` and `jo` in fil.toml, Muhammad written short in mul.toml (`mohd`, `mhd`) and id.toml (`muh`, `moh`, `moch`), `ach` in id.toml and `fco` in es.toml carry one, the last seven since decisions/H6's 2026-11-06 #1115 entry — 2026-10-23 #752; 2026-10-26 #789, phase 2k.
- A particle or salutation reading with no attested use in a person's name is refused rather than kept, its counts as name readings: `bon` and `dí` (und.toml) and `heer` (nl.toml) are no particles, and the plural salutations `herren` (de.toml) and `heren` (nl.toml) no titles, a plural never standing before one person's name — 2026-10-27 #766.
- Abbreviations and full words spelled alike across European languages (`dr`, `prof`, `doctor`, `professor`, `docent`) are in `mul.toml`; a credential goes to a file only on evidence that names in that language carry it, save the degrees written after names in every country's data (`phd`, `msc`, `mba`, `mph`, `bsc`, and `ms` beside its salutation), which are in `mul.toml` with the salutations `mr`, `mrs`, `ms`, `miss`, `mister` and `madam`, `miss` and `mister` with their family readings, a word moving whole, `md` staying in en.toml as Muhammad's abbreviation; `ing` stays in `mul.toml` with its name readings — 2026-10-19 #750, the rest of the titles; 2026-10-20 #751, the credentials; 2026-11-02 #1005 (Derek); 2026-11-03 #1005, the mul.toml audit (Derek); 2026-11-03 #1038 (Derek).
- `und.toml` holds only `bon` and `dí`, of unrecorded origin, their particle readings refused (below) and their name readings kept; every other word, the credentials, conjunctions, bound given names and birth markers included, is in a language file or in `mul.toml` — 2026-10-19 #749; 2026-10-20 #751, the conjunctions.

Not standing: the plans the classifications carried out, phase 1's mechanical conversion of the unclassified categories into `und.toml` (2026-10-18 #737) and the shared readings left in place until their categories were classified (2026-10-19 #749), both done by 2026-10-20 #751, the conjunctions; and #747's reason for exempting `und.toml`, its entries keeping the Python lists' order for its vetting comments to point along (2026-10-19 #747), overtaken once it held two entries and no such comment.

Open: #753 (phase 3, language packs; the 16 words strict ungates, each read for a name reading in the chosen file), #754 (phase 4, order, shapes and the output model; the `Lexicon` surface's build, 4i-a to 4i-d).

## Log

- 2026-10-21 #762 (Derek) — `und.toml` STAYS EXEMPT FROM CHECK 10 BECAUSE IT HOLDS NO LANGUAGE'S WORDS, the ground nameparser/vocab/README.md gives. The 2026-10-19 #747 entry exempted it so its entries could keep the Python lists' order that its vetting comments pointed along; #749 to #751 moved every word out but `bon` and `dí` and its per-entry comments with them, so that reason no longer holds, and tools/vocab/build.py's `UNORDERED` comment now gives this one. No compiled set or check moves.

- **2026-10-15/16 — the model (Derek)** (decision 6's strict variant, dropping other languages' name readings only, superseded by 2026-10-30 #753, file-strict). Decided over a brainstorm whose notes are gitignored; the durable decisions are these.
  1. The vocabulary redesign comes before adding many packs; it is designed for 3.0 and whatever is safe is pulled forward.
  2. Each word is described by its READINGS (word → reading → properties), replacing the base-plus-marker pairs.
  3. Detailed kinds are recorded during the migration even where every kind of a category behaves alike at first; generation and credential are separate kinds (#326).
  4. A language file means "the words the parser might encounter in names in that language" — what occurs in names and records written in it, including particles common there through immigration — not words of that language's etymology.
  5. The default loads every language and keeps working for names of any language, as today; choosing a language narrows it.
  6. Packs prefer and strict packs restrict: a pack settles only the clashes where its language has a VOCABULARY reading, and a strict variant also drops other languages' name-level readings. (Excepted for one pack, 2026-11-05 #1108, below: `locales.VI`'s fragment removes readings of Vietnamese syllables where vi.toml holds none.)
  7. `e`/`y`/`i` stay in the default, at least to start; choosing a language settles the one-case fork.
  8. A patronymic field belongs in 3.0.
  9. Gender is recorded only where a word gives a definitive clue.
  10. Title-phrase modifiers count as titles only next to a head title (parked, not filed). (Unparked and narrowed to the head of the name, 2026-10-23 #752, below.)
  11. Name readings are hand-declared, and only for words some language file claims as vocabulary, so nobody maintains surname lists. (Widened 2026-10-19, below: or refuses.)
  12. Bearer counts are structured provenance (source + count), never used to compute a reading: two data sets deciding one reading would make the decision unclear. A new census flags changed names for review.
  13. `Dean Smith` reads given `Dean`, as today.
  14. Order and shape are separate: a language supplies only a default order; the shapes (where titles, suffixes, particles and commas sit) belong to each order and live in the core.
  15. Vocabulary readings settle a clash; name readings only keep the gate on. (Bent for one pack, 2026-11-05 #1108, below: `locales.VI`'s fragment removes the particle, connective and credential readings of Vietnamese syllables, the settling no reading in vi.toml can do.)
  16. The contested-head rule covers only words outside `titles` today (`dean`, `justice`, `major`); a title that is also a given name today is decided one word at a time, like the graf class (decisions/salutation-titles).
  17. `cased` and `same_as` replace a "canonical" spelling, and `cased` replaces `capitalization_exceptions`; both default to the word, and a reading's `native` spelling is for surfacing.
  18. `acronym` is a reading property, replacing the `suffix_acronyms`/`suffix_words` split; `expands` records the meaning an entry was added for, not every meaning.
  19. `addresses_given` generalizes to `addresses = family | given | full | any`.
  20. A pack's preferred reading can outrank a reading derived by a SHAPE rule (period abbreviation, initials, capitals), not only a vocabulary reading. (Applied in the default too, 2026-10-23 #752, below: a declared `given` reading outranks the leading-abbreviation title's shape.)

  Phasing, fixed then: (1) the data format and a compiler to today's sets, snapshot-neutral; (2) derived ambiguity, with the gate extended to every reading kind — the step that changes behavior, with its own design; (3) pack semantics, needing 1 and 2; (4) the rules menu, order and shapes, and the output model.

- **2026-10-18 #737 — phase 1 shipped: per-word TOML compiled to today's sets (approved by Derek, 2026-10-18, all eight of the spec's recommendations).** (`may_be_name` and `may_be_given = true` as written flags, `may_be_given` stated on every particle, and a property exclusion of `may_be_given` superseded by 2026-10-25 #752, which derives the two markers as this entry's flags bullet planned.) No parse, render or public name moves; it is a re-encoding plus the build checks the encoding makes possible.
  - **The format.** `nameparser/vocab/<tag>.toml`, one file per BCP 47 tag in canonical case (`pt-BR`, `zh-Hant`), plus `und` for words not yet assigned a language and `mul` for words shared on purpose (the Roman numerals). `schema = 1` is the only other top-level key: a new kind or property is additive and does not bump it, a change in what a key means does, and a compiler refuses a newer schema. Each `[[word]]` has a `text`, already in `_normalize` form, and zero or more readings keyed by KIND, each a TABLE (`generation = {}`, never `= true`) so a property can be added to any reading later. The file name is the language; there is no `language` key to drift from it.
  - **Kinds as keys, with generic category keys.** Each kind belongs to one category, and the category decides which compiled field a reading feeds. A category key is itself a kind (`title = {}`, a title not yet classified), so classifying a word later is a data edit that moves no compiled set. Kind names are unique across categories. Phase 1 classifies the SUFFIX category fully — `generation`, `credential`, `honorific`, `professional_honorific`, `status`, `courtesy` — and converts every other category mechanically into `und.toml` with its generic kind and today's flags, so the TOML is the single source from the first PR. One suffix kind per word replaces the import-time assert that the acronym and word sets are disjoint.
  - **Flags now, derivation in phase 2.** The marker sets became explicit properties — `may_be_given` on particles, `may_be_name` on acronym credentials, `may_be_initial` on conjunctions, `attached = { side = "after" }` for the glued peel, `addresses = "given"`, `cased` — rather than being derived from declared name readings, which would have meant declaring a given reading for `te`, `tho` and `du` that no count supports. (Corrected 2026-10-25, #752: each has a US SSA given-name count, `te` 10, `tho` 52 and `du` 27 births summed over all years, so the premise was wrong when written; the figures are on their given readings.) Measured for the later swap: deriving both ambiguity markers from name readings reproduces them exactly if the readings are declared on exactly the flagged words, because no word sits in one marker set while needing to be absent from the other (`do` is in both; `mc` and `vd`, particles and credentials, in neither, consistent with #454). Recompute: `L = Lexicon.default(); L.particles_ambiguous & (L.suffix_acronyms - L.suffix_acronyms_ambiguous)` and `L.suffix_acronyms_ambiguous & (L.particles - L.particles_ambiguous)`, both empty.
  - **`may_be_given` is stated on every particle, never defaulted.** Found while converting, not in the spec: the old encoding stored the NEVER-GIVEN particles and derived the rest, so a new particle was ambiguous unless argued never-given — #293's safe-by-default and decisions/vocabulary-collisions C-i's "under uncertainty, ambiguous". A flag with a default would have picked one side of that silently (a bare `particle = {}` reading as never-given is the unsafe side), so the build refuses a particle reading without it.
  - **Exclusions are data.** `excluded` refuses a kind or category at word level, or a behavioral property at reading level (`honorific = { excluded = { attached = "…" } }`), with a reason. It lives in the file of the language whose reading it refuses, and the build refuses the excluded thing in ANY file, quoting the reason, so a contributor adding a refused reading meets the record of why. An excluded kind refuses itself whatever its properties, and every other kind compiling to the same set, so a generic `suffix = {}` cannot put back a refused `generation` (found in the PR review); the name kinds, which compile to nothing, refuse only themselves. A refused property is refused any value other than the one it takes unwritten on a reading of the excluding kind (false for the `may_be_*` flags, true for a credential's `acronym`, so a particle can still state `may_be_given = false` beside an exclusion of it), on any reading of its category — and `cased`, compiled once per word, on every reading of the word. The pilot carried the generational-words refusals, rai/cha/mai and the glued-peel refusals; the README states the exception that lets those sentences here become queries.
  - **Script is read from the word, not the file**, reversing the notes' premise that Serbian Latin and Cyrillic need separate files: `sr.toml` holds `mlađi` and `млађи` side by side. A script subtag is for what the text cannot tell apart, which in practice is Simplified and Traditional Han.
  - **A build step, not a parse at import.** tools/vocab/build.py compiles the files into a committed nameparser/config/_compiled.py, one sorted entry per line, and the public constants stay where they were as `NAME = _compiled.NAME` assignments with their docstrings (autodoc's attribute scan needs an assignment) and the deprecated-alias bridge. tests/v2/test_vocab.py holds the committed module to a fresh compile and runs the checks with warnings as errors, the snapshot.jsonl pattern (mechanisms.md#COMPILED-DATA-COMMITTED). The TOML ships as package data for tools and later phases, and the parser never reads it.
  - **The comments moved with their words.** The suffix words' per-word facts became fields (`note`, `issue`, `same_as`, `expands`, `excluded`); the class-level parts of the vetting notes were checked against the decisions entries that already hold them, and what had no home went to decisions/W2's entry of this date. In the mechanically converted categories the block comments stand as TOML comments above the entry they stood above, in source order, and inline comments became reading `note`s; classifying those categories is the later work that turns them into fields.
  - **Measurement (2026-10-18, Python 3.11.17).** `Lexicon.default()` and all thirteen raw constants equal the pre-change tree's, compared against values pickled from master at 6803a261; `tools/differential/snapshot.py` reports "matches the snapshot". Negative control: dropping `may_be_name` from `ma` moves 120 snapshot entries and makes `Lexicon.default()` unequal, and editing the TOML without rebuilding fails the staleness test with the refresh command. `import nameparser` measured 48.5–48.8 ms median against master's 49.4 ms (25 fresh processes each, two runs), so the generated module costs nothing visible; the spike measured parsing the TOML at import at about +18 ms on a 48 ms import, and the TOML grows with descriptive data the compiled module never carries.
  - **Declined, with evidence.** PARSING THE TOML AT IMPORT: +18 ms net on every import (spike, 2026-10-18), and it could not be made lazy on 2.x without making the config modules and the shim's `CONSTANTS` lazy too. DERIVING THE MARKERS NOW: see the flags bullet. A `peelable` BOOLEAN: `attached` is the general property from the start, a table so its other side and separators can be added. A `language` KEY in the file: a second source of truth for the file name.
  - **Phases 2–4 are rejected by the compiler, not accepted as inert data**: `addresses = "full"`/`"any"`, `attached` on a side other than after, and the names later phases will use (`default`, `prefer`, `strict`, `bare_only`, `leading_only`, `trailing_only`, `attaches`, `separators`) are build errors naming their phase, so nothing reads as working that does not. Locale-pack vocabulary and the Korean surname list stay in Python: surnames are a segmentation list, outside the per-word model, and packs move in phase 3.
  - **Recompute the composition** rather than quoting it: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind generation --count` (readings, one per file a word is in), `--kind suffix`, `--excluded generation`, `--issue 661`.

- **2026-10-19 — decision 11 widened to words a file refuses (Derek).** A name reading may also sit on a word whose vocabulary reading some file refuses: the reading is the evidence the refusal rests on, as `fils`'s `family` reading in fr.toml backs its excluded `generation`. The build already allowed this (its soft warning fires on a name reading only where the word has neither a vocabulary reading nor an exclusion), so the decision now matches the shipped data; #758's review found the mismatch.

- **2026-10-19 #749 — the particles classified by language and kind.** (Check 5's agreement on `may_be_given` and the unexamined-word convention keyed on `may_be_given = true` superseded by 2026-10-25 #752, which derives the marker from a `given` reading and keys the convention on that reading.) No compiled set moves: `tools/vocab/build.py --check` reports the committed module current, and `tools/differential/snapshot.py` matches. Each particle reading left `und.toml` for the file of every language whose names it occurs in (decision 4), so one word may sit in several (`de` in five); the readings it shares with other categories (`do`, `mc` and `vd` as credentials, `abu` and the Arabic أبو/ابو as bound given names, `freiherr` and `st` as titles) stay where they were until those categories are classified. Eight particle kinds join the generic one: `preposition`, `article`, `patronymic`, `teknonymic`, `clan`, `rank`, `saint` and `alias`, defined in nameparser/vocab/README.md. The `clan`/`patronymic` line is drawn for decision 8's patronymic field: a patronymic is followed by a parent's name, a clan prefix by a family name the lineage shares, so the Irish Ó and Ní and the Gaelic Mac are clans although each began as "descendant/son/daughter of".
  - **One word, a kind per language, one flag.** A particle may take a different kind in each file (`de` is a preposition in French and an article in Dutch), so check 5 now compares the readings of a CATEGORY rather than of a kind: two kinds of one word disagreeing on `may_be_given` would otherwise have compiled silently to the false. The suffix category is unaffected, check 4 allowing it one kind per word.
  - **`expands` carries the meaning, `note` the vetting.** A particle's gloss in its language ("of the, fem. pl.") is its `expands`, so `note` keeps the meaning the comments gave it: a `may_be_given = true` word whose particle readings carry no `note` in any file, or whose note says so, has not been examined (the "bare entry" convention of the old module comments). A word's note sits on one of its files, not repeated in each, so the test is per word: `mac` is examined though only en.toml holds its note. Descriptive `gender` is recorded on the markers that name the bearer's sex (decision 9), among them `bin`/`binti`, `ibn`/`bint`, `ó`/`ní` and `freiherr`/`freiherrin` (`uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind particle` shows each).
  - **The keep-outs became exclusions**, in the file of the language whose reading they refuse: `ben` and `ال` in ar.toml, `בר` in he.toml, `nic` and unaccented `o` in ga.toml, `bt` and `bte` in ms.toml (`uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --excluded particle`). decisions/vocabulary-exclusions' particle block keeps its class-level bullets and points at the query for the rest.
  - **Two words keep the generic kind in `und.toml`**: `bon` and `dí`, in the prefix list since the earliest history (2011 and 2012) with no record of which names they come from, and with no particle use found. Both are borne as names (the counts are on their entries); removing them moves a compiled set, so it is left to its own change.
  - **Recompute** rather than quoting: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind particle --count` (readings, one per file a word is in), `--kind clan`, `--file nl --kind particle`.

- **2026-10-19 #747 — a build-checked word order (check 10).** No compiled set moves: the 41 language files that were out of order on cc030b9c were sorted (an entry moving with any comment above it), `tools/vocab/build.py --check` reports the committed module current, and `tools/differential/snapshot.py` matches. The order, stated in nameparser/vocab/README.md's Word order section, is kind in `CATEGORY`'s order, then the word's `same_as` path from its root head, then text, so the spellings of one word sit together under it before #750 moves the titles out of `und.toml`.
  - **Checked, not rewritten.** `tomllib` only reads, and a comment above an entry belongs to it, which no TOML reader can see; a rewriter would have to round-trip the files' comments. No checked file holds one on 2026-10-19 (every per-entry comment is in `und.toml`), but a word leaving `und.toml` before its category is classified carries its vetting comment with it. So the build refuses a file out of order, naming the entry to move (the first outside a longest run already in order, so a word appended at the end is named rather than its neighbours) and its place against that run, so following the messages takes the fewest moves (#767's second review found the place first given beside a neighbour itself out of place, which took up to 22 moves where 10 would do).
  - **Run last.** Check 10 runs after every other check, so a contributor adding a refused word to a language file meets the exclusion's reason (check 6) rather than an order error, the precedence check 6 is placed first for; the first version ran it at the end of loading, ahead of check 6, and the review of #767 found an excluded word appended to en.toml reported as out of order.
  - **The same_as path, not the direct target, and the file's own path.** Keyed by the direct target, a spelling of a spelling sorts apart from the word it spells: ar.toml's `ed-` (same_as `el-`, itself same_as `al-`) would follow `ul-`. The path keeps each spelling under its own head inside the root's group, and it follows only the file's own entries, so a word that is a head in one file and a spelling in another does not make one file's order depend on the other (#767's review found the first version, which followed a map taking each word's first file, accepting `jnr` before `jr` in en.toml once de.toml made `jr` a spelling).
  - **No same_as loop, over every same_as written.** Check 7 refuses only a same_as naming its own word. Check 10 refuses any longer loop, in every file, `und.toml` included, over every same_as of every reading: no check holds a word to one head across files or kinds, and the first version, which walked one head per word, passed a loop running through a second file's or a second kind's same_as.
  - **Codepoint order of the normalized text.** Defined for every script and every machine, and no one language's collation; the cost is that within a script a precomposed accented letter follows `z` (`aḍ-` after `az-`), and that a traditional and a simplified spelling with no `same_as` between them sort apart (`課長` and `课长` did, until #790 linked the pairs in `zh.toml`).
  - **A refusal sorts under the kind it refuses.** Forty-six language-file entries on 20b595b2 (`senior`, `fils`, the Slavic `ml` and `st`) hold no vocabulary reading, only an exclusion and sometimes the name reading that is its evidence; they sort beside the words they were refused as. (Corrected 2026-10-19 in #750: the figure was dated "2026-10-19", which #750's title refusals made ambiguous the same day, and the next bullet quoted a und.toml comment that #750 moved out.)
  - **`und.toml` is exempt.** Its entries keep the Python lists' order, which its vetting comments still point along (a neighbour "above" or "below"); each category's classification moves its words out, into files that are checked.

- **2026-10-19 #750 — title kinds, and the salutations and given-addressing titles classified (part 1 of #750; kinds approved by Derek in chat, 2026-10-19).** No compiled set moves: `tools/vocab/build.py --check` reports the committed module current, and `tools/differential/snapshot.py` matches. Ten title kinds join the generic one, defined in nameparser/vocab/README.md: `salutation`, `style`, `academic`, `religious`, `nobility`, `military`, `office`, `occupation`, `kinship` and `modifier`. Two lines are drawn on purpose. `academic` and `occupation` divide by FUNCTION, not subject: a form of address (`Dr`, the Egyptian `مهندس`) against a job described before a name (`chemist`, `novelist`), so the descriptive words, the collision-prone half of the old bulk import, sit in one kind any later gate can name. `modifier` is decision 10's parked class (`vice`, `grand`, `his`, `1st`), recorded as data now and behaving as every title does until that rule is designed. A household kind was proposed and folded into `occupation` (it would have held about five words).
  - **The hard cases, decided with the kinds.** `prince`, `princess`, `sir` and `dame` are `nobility`, the last two as chivalric ranks, and `lord`, `lady` and `knight` will be when their kinds are assigned (done in part 2, the same day); `master` is a `salutation`, the style for a boy that is why it addresses by the given name; `father`, `mother`, `brother` and `sister` are `religious`, the use a title makes of them, while `aunt` and `uncle` are `kinship`; the sheikh cluster is a `style`, used for rulers, elders and scholars alike; `chief` will be `nobility` and `deputy` an `office`, not modifiers, since each stands alone as a title ("Chief Obafemi Awolowo"); the Bengali মোঃ and মোসাঃ are salutations, keeping the note that মোঃ abbreviates a name.
  - **Gender only on a marked word** (decision 9): a feminine form or one half of a fixed pair (`mr` and `mrs`, `king` and `queen`), and `mx` as `"n"`; an unmarked noun (`actor`, `pope`, `doctor`) records none.
  - **Files.** Each reading went to the file of every language whose names it occurs in, a Latin transliteration to its source language's file as #749 put the Latin `bin` in ar.toml (`swami` in hi.toml and bn.toml, the sheikh cluster in ar.toml, `cheikh` in fr.toml too). Six files are new: ak, et, id, ro, sw and vi. The few abbreviations shared across European languages (`dr`, `prof`) will go to `mul.toml` (done in part 2, the same day) with the rest of their kind; no salutation qualified, each language writing its own.
  - **The title keep-outs became exclusions**, in the file of the language whose reading they refuse, with their counts as name readings where counts were taken (malam, bay, shehu): `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --excluded title`. The per-word counts in decisions/salutation-titles moved onto the `note` of each word's title reading, and the prose points at the query. The reasons the given-addressing comments gave for leaving `lord`, `lady`, `venerable`, `rabbi` and `imam` out of GIVEN_NAME_TITLES are notes on those words in und.toml until their kinds are assigned (they moved with them in part 2, the same day).
  - **Recompute** rather than quoting: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind salutation --count`, `--kind title --file und --count` (the titles still in `und.toml`, all of them generic until part 2 emptied it the same day, leaving one generic title, `wm`, in en.toml; `--kind title` names the whole category, so no query counts the generic kind alone), `--excluded title`.

- **2026-10-19 #750 — the rest of the titles classified (part 2 of #750)** (its `wm` decision superseded by 2026-10-22 #789, its `md` academic reading by #853, decisions/indic-honorifics, and its measurement corrected in place). No compiled set moves: `tools/vocab/build.py --check` reports the committed module current, and `tools/differential/snapshot.py` matches. The 644 titles still generic in `und.toml` on e943665e took their kinds, all but `wm` (below), and left for the files of the languages whose names they occur in, so `und.toml` holds no title. Part 1's hard cases are applied as decided there: `lord`, `lady`, `knight` and `chief` are `nobility`, `deputy` an `office`, and `dr` and `prof` are in mul.toml.
  - **English holds most of them**: the bulk import's occupations, the US military ranks and their abbreviations, the offices, and the modifiers. `modifier` takes what is a title only inside a phrase: the qualifiers of ranks and offices (`vice`, `rear`, `petty`, `field`), the domain words of the descriptive titles (`baseball`, `software`, `tax`), the ordinals, and the court words of #9's list of judges' titles (`designated`, `docket`, `pro`, `edmi`).
  - **Address, not subject, decides `academic`.** Besides the doctors and professors of every script, it takes the English words used to address a professional (`advocate`, `attorney`, `engineer`, `nurse`, and `md` as the medical degree written before a name), the German abbreviations written before a name (`ra`, Rechtsanwalt; `wp`, Wirtschaftsprüfer; `pd` and `priv.-doz`; `dipl.-ing`), and the Devanagari `पंडित` and `पं`, a scholar's title, as part 1's refusal of the Latin `pandit` already keyed it.
  - **`wm` keeps the generic kind**, in en.toml: it is William's abbreviation, made a title for #68 so that `Wm. Scott Magargee III` would not read given `Wm.`, and no title kind describes it. Removing it moves a parse, though only of the bare spelling: `Wm Smith` reads title `Wm` with the word listed and given `Wm` without it, while the period-abbreviation shape rule takes `Wm.` either way (measured 2026-10-19 with `Lexicon.default().remove(titles={'wm'})`). So it is left to its own change. (Corrected 2026-10-24, #789: the measurement was wrong on its date. It took the off-switch parse at the head of the name only; a trailing `Wm.`, rules/H5's since 2.3.0, and a `Wm.` before a family comma, rules/H6's since 2.5.0, read vocabulary and move with the bare spelling.)
  - **mul.toml** takes `docent`, `doctor` and `professor` beside `dr` and `prof`: full words spelled alike in several European languages, listed once rather than in a file per language.
  - **A transliteration goes to its source language**, as in part 1: the Islamic titles to ar.toml (`imam`, `mufti`, `mahdi`, `murshid`, `hajji`, `vizier`), or to fa.toml where the Latin spelling is the Persian form (`ayatollah`, `akhoond`, `mullah`, `pir`); `rabbi` to he.toml and `rebbe` to yi.toml; `maharajah`, `maharani` and `subedar` to hi.toml; `giani` and `gyani` to pa.toml; `tsar` and `tsarina` to ru.toml; `effendi` to tr.toml; `vardapet` to hy.toml. The Buddhist, Jain and Zoroastrian titles (`arhat`, `bodhisattva`, `buddha`, `siddha`, `tirthankar`, `saoshyant`) are in en.toml: no source-language name is written with these Latin spellings, which are English loanwords. Four files are new: bin (Edo, `edohen` and `ekegbian`), ff (Fula, `lamido`), mi (Māori, `rangatira`) and yi.
  - **Gender** follows part 1's rule. The man's title of a noble pair is marked (`count`, `baron`, `graf`) as `king` is, and so is a `-man` word whose `-woman` pair the list holds (`businessman`); a descriptive noun beside its `-ess` form marks only the feminine (`actress`, `heiress`). `sultan` records none, Ottoman usage having given it to imperial women too (its note in tr.toml).
  - **Comments became fields.** The #269, #343 and #344 block comments over the native-script titles are notes on one entry of each group (the Hebrew quote-boundary probe on `ד"ר`, the Bengali doctor split on `ড`); `duke`'s one-line keep-out is an exclusion in en.toml with its counts; the notes part 1 left on `lord`, `lady`, `venerable`, `rabbi` and `imam` moved with them. The readings `und.toml` shares with a moved title, all credentials (`cpl`, `cpo`, `cpt`, `csm`, `lt`, `md`, `ra`, `sa`, `sgm`, `vc`, and `ms` from part 1), sit in a section of their own, as #749's did.
  - **Recompute** rather than quoting: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind title --file und --count`, `--file en --kind modifier --count`, `--file mul`.

- **2026-10-20 #751 — the credentials given their languages (part 1 of #751; its rule that no credential is in `mul.toml` superseded by 2026-11-02 #1005).** No compiled set moves: `tools/vocab/build.py --check` reports the committed module current, and `tools/differential/snapshot.py` matches. The 605 credential readings in `und.toml` on a0edfeda left it, so `und.toml` holds no suffix reading, and the three credential refusals (`rai`, `cha`, `mai`) went with them to en.toml.
  - **English holds all but seven.** 505 of the 605 came from one import (af5bdab, 2019, #93), the United States post-nominal letters of two Wikipedia lists, and most of the rest are British and Commonwealth honours and degrees, from the list's first versions and #7; these, and the degrees below, are in en.toml. Recompute the provenance with `git blame -w 6803a261 -- nameparser/config/suffixes.py`, the last commit before the TOML.
  - **German names hold seven, alone.** #18 (3e14ea20) added the German parliamentary memberships (`mdb`, `mdl`, `mda`, `mdbb`, `mdep`, `mdhb`) and `stb` (Steuerberater) for German names, German by what they mean; they are in de.toml alone, with `expands`. The degrees #18 wrote AFTER a name are not there (`dipl.-ing`, written before one, is a de.toml title since #750). Its test data writes BA, BSc, M.A., M.Sc., M.Eng., B. Eng., MBA and LL.M. beside one name, John Meyer, and #18 itself calls them international degrees rather than German names; 3e14ea20 added `ba`, `bsc` and `meng` from those rows only because the list lacked them, `ma`, `msc`, `mba` and `llm` being listed already. So those seven credentials are in en.toml alone (B. Eng. is in no file). A word goes to a file on evidence that names in that language carry it, and that is why no credential went to mul.toml either: the Bologna degrees are written after names in most European languages, but nothing in hand shows which.
  - **Comments became fields.** und.toml's block over the ambiguous credentials was class-level — the criterion is decisions/suffix-acronym-collisions' and decisions/vocabulary-collisions' C-i, the reading rules/S4 and S6, the bracket escape rules/S1 — and is gone, its per-word facts already notes on `ms`, `sa`, `se`, `om`, `mc`, `vd`, `meng` and `lac`; `ba` gained the note that earned its marking. The block over the case masks became `excluded = { cased = … }` on `meng`, `edd`, `lac` and `ded`, each with the rendering a mask would cost (decisions/vocabulary-exclusions' CAPITALIZATION_EXCEPTIONS block keeps the class-level part), and the credential half of `abd`'s comment became `expands` and a `note` on its en.toml reading.
  - **`mc` sorts as a credential now.** An entry sorts under its earliest kind and `credential` precedes `clan`, so en.toml's `mc` left `mac`'s side for the credential block, its `same_as` kept.
  - **Recompute** rather than quoting: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind credential --file en --count`, `--kind credential --file de`, `--kind suffix --file und --count`, `--excluded cased`.

- **2026-10-20 #751 — the conjunctions, bound given names and birth markers given their languages (part 2 of #751).** No compiled set moves: `tools/vocab/build.py --check` reports the committed module current, and `tools/differential/snapshot.py` matches. The 44 entries still in `und.toml` on 85ac4167 other than `bon` and `dí` left it, so `und.toml` holds only those two particles of unrecorded origin, and the comment at its top says why (#766 asks whether they stay at all).
  - **Each went where its own record put it.** The conjunctions follow the #269 and #397 issues and the names their comments gave: `and`, `of` and `the` to en.toml, `et` to fr.toml, `und` to de.toml, `y` to es.toml, `e` to pt.toml and it.toml, `i` to ca.toml and pl.toml, `и` to ru.toml and bg.toml, `і`, `й` and `та` to uk.toml, `και` to el.toml, `و` to ar.toml, and `&` to mul.toml, a symbol no language owns. The bound given names, Latin transliterations and Arabic script alike, went to ar.toml, as #749 and #750 sent a transliteration to its source language; `abu`, `أبو` and `ابو` join the teknonymic readings already there. The birth markers follow the old module docstring (#274): `née`, `né` and the unaccented `nee` to fr.toml, which the docstring calls French, and to en.toml, English writing them as French does; `geb` and `geborene` to de.toml and `geboren` to nl.toml, which the docstring calls Dutch; `rozená` to cs.toml; `født` to da.toml and nb.toml, the Nynorsk `fødd` to nn.toml and `född` to sv.toml; the five Russian spellings to ru.toml; `z domu` to pl.toml (#434); `旧姓` to ja.toml (#309). Two files are new: ca and nn.
  - **Comments became fields.** The block over the single-letter connectives (#383, #479, #397) was class-level, held by decisions/P8 and rules/P3, and is gone; its per-word facts are notes on `e` and `i`, and its keep-outs, `y` and the Cyrillic `и`, `і` and `й`, are `excluded = { may_be_initial = … }` (decisions/vocabulary-exclusions' conjunctions_ambiguous block keeps the class-level part). The #269 blocks over the Cyrillic, Greek and Arabic connectives and the Arabic-script bound given names became an `issue` on each and notes on `і`, `و` and `عبد`; `abd`'s comment became its note in ar.toml, beside the credential note part 1 left in en.toml. Spellings carry `same_as` where one word has several (`abdel` and `abdal` of `abdul`, `abou` of `abu`, `й` of `і`, `nee` of `née`, the е spellings of the ё ones), and gender is recorded where the form marks it (`née` and `né`, `geborene`, `rozená`, the Russian participles).
  - **Two keep-outs became data that had been prose.** The Scandinavian abbreviation `f.`, which collides with the initial F., is an exclusion in da.toml. `roz`, removed from the birth markers on 2026-08-26, is an exclusion in cs.toml with its reason, measured again: with it listed, `Rosalind Roz Smith` reads maiden 'Smith' and takes Rosalind for the family, where the 2026-08-26 record says it left no family name at all.
  - **Recompute** rather than quoting: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --file und`, `--kind conjunction`, `--kind bound_given --file ar --count`, `--kind birth_marker`, `--excluded may_be_initial`, `--excluded birth_marker`.

- **2026-10-22 #789 (Derek) — `wm` LEAVES THE TITLES.** SUPERSEDES: the `wm` bullet of 2026-10-19 #750, the rest of the titles. #68 asked that `Wm. Scott Magargee III` not read given name `Wm.`, and `wm` was listed as a title for it in 0.5.5. The request was mistaken: `Wm` is William written short, and a name it opens is a man named William, which a reader of the name would not hesitate over. The case came back from the FEC donor list (`tools/differential/local_corpora.py`, fec-individuals), where bare `WM` opened the given part of 69 names (re-measured 2026-10-24 on 08d130ed), every one read as a title with the given name taken from the next word or left empty. The title reading is refused in en.toml with that reason, so a contributor who adds it back meets it as a build error.
  - **The dotted spelling is split by position, not by spelling.** rules/H2's shape reads an unlisted period-marked abbreviation opening the given part as a title, so `Wm. Smith` keeps title 'Wm.' and `Smith, Wm.` too; making `Wm.`, `Chas.`, `Geo.`, `Ma.` or `Jo.` a given name there is a reading that outranks a shape rule, phase 2's question (#752, D14(b) of its spec), and tests/v2/cases.py pins today's `Wm. Smith` so that change shows. Where VOCABULARY reads the word, the dotted spelling moves with the bare one: a trailing `Wm.` was rules/H5's title (`John Smith Wm.` → family 'Wm.', `Smith, John Wm.` → middle 'Wm.', the readings of 1.4.0 through 2.2.0), and a `Wm.` opening the part before a family comma was rules/H6's (`Wm. Smith, John` → family 'Wm. Smith', the reading of 1.4.0 through 2.4.0). The 2026-10-19 bullet said removal moves only the bare spelling, and is corrected in place.
  - **Measured** against 08d130ed (`snapshot.py --local` with the base recorded by `--tree`): no committed corpus holds the token `wm`, so the snapshot and the CLDR, GND and Wikidata agreement views could not move and did not; psc-individuals' field agreements do not move either. fec-individuals moves 77 of 1,893,964 names: 69 with bare `WM` opening the given part, 3 with bare `WM` behind another title in it, 3 with a trailing `WM.` and 2 with `WM.` before the family comma. ctgov-officials moves 1 of 352,104, `Wm` behind a title before a family comma.

- **2026-10-24 #835 — `after_given`, the salutations that may end a given part.** A behavioral title property, allowed on a `salutation` reading only (a build check, since an office or rank word is borne as a middle name), compiles into `AFTER_GIVEN_TITLES` and `Lexicon.after_given_titles`, read by rules/H8. A salutation borne as a name refuses it with an exclusion carrying its counts. The rule is keyed on a property rather than on the kind, which would make a kind compile into a behavior where every other behavior is a property; the vetting and the measurement are decisions/H8's. Adding the field changes `Lexicon`'s pickle layout, as 2.1 and 2.3 did.
  - **Recompute** rather than quoting: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind salutation`, `--excluded after_given`.

- **2026-10-23 #752 (Derek) — PHASE 2'S DESIGN IS AGREED: READINGS FROM THREE SOURCES IN ONE POOL, THE TWO NAME MARKERS DERIVED, AND ONE GATE OF FOUR STEPS (FLOOR, FORM, STRUCTURE, COUNT) ASKED AT THE SITES THAT DECIDE TODAY.** Derek agreed every recommendation of the design but the one on `;` (2026-10-23); that one and two points the re-measurement raised he decided on 2026-10-24 (the 2026-10-24 #752 entry: the `;` change, the title order and #867/#869's words). The design overview is docs/design/vocabulary-model-rfc.md's Phase 2 section; this entry is the record. Measurements are dated snapshots: each was taken with a spike, a throwaway patch of switches over master `638685b4` (2026-10-24, Python 3.11.17; an earlier round on `48ed448d`), which is not kept, so a figure here is evidence for the decision and each sub-phase PR re-measures on its own base. The measures, main one first: the PSC register's fielded names (`uv run python tools/differential/local_agreement.py psc-individuals`, 1,039,171 records scored, 1,004,775 agreeing on every field on `638685b4`), counted as records fixed, broken and changed; the committed corpora (`snapshot.py`); the CLDR and GND agreement records (`cldr_agreement.py`); the local snapshots of ctgov-officials, tse-donors and fec-individuals (`snapshot.py --local`); and spelling consistency, how many persons' two spellings go from reading differently to reading alike and back (mechanisms.md#PAIRED-SPELLINGS-PER-CHANGE). Local-corpus figures are counts and token shapes: `Aa` a word in Title case, `AA` in capitals, `A.` a dotted letter.
  1. **A name reading may be declared without a count, with a required `note` saying why.** Deriving the markers needs a `given` reading on 38 of the 40 may-be-given particles and a `given` or `family` reading on 7 of the 44 may-be-name credentials (`ba`, `do`, `ed`, `jd`, `lac`, `ma`, `meng`), and 12 of those particles carry no vetting note at all (`aan`, `aen`, `bat`, `da`, `dal`, `den`, `santa`, `tho`, `thoe`, `vande`, `vander`, `vel`). The note keeps the vocab README's unexamined test working. Recompute: `uv run python -c "import tomllib,glob; from nameparser import Lexicon as X; L=X.default(); n={w['text'] for f in glob.glob('nameparser/vocab/*.toml') for w in tomllib.load(open(f,'rb'))['word'] if 'given' in w or 'family' in w}; print(sorted(L.suffix_acronyms_ambiguous-n), len(L.particles_ambiguous-n))"`.
  2. **`ld` keeps no name reading; its count moves into the credential reading's `note`, and it stays ungated.** Its `given` reading records the evidence for NOT marking it (45 SSA births against 11,703 NPPES providers writing the credential, #797); a name reading that would turn the gate on contradicts decision 15's "name readings only keep the gate on" when the evidence is against the gate. Literal derivation would have moved 0 committed entries, 3 ctgov and 5 FEC readings, nothing in PSC.
  3. **A particle with no given reading still states `may_be_given = false`, and `may_be_given = true` is refused with a message naming the `given` reading to write instead**, so a new particle cannot fall to the unsafe side silently (#293's safe-by-default; decisions/vocabulary-collisions C-i).
  4. **"One gate for six rules" is four clashes and two floors.** The title run's floor (rules/H3) and bound given names (rules/P5) reach every member of their category and read only a count; no second reading is weighed, so they are step 1, unchanged. The acronym gate's nine decision sites stay nine, each keeping the order it reads its cues in (C4 the count before the case, S6 the case before the count); what becomes one is the predicate each asks.
  5. **Joined credentials land: a word joined by `-` or `/` whose every chunk is listed and none ambiguous reads as a credential, and an unlisted word of capitals so joined, with a chunk of two or more letters, joins the ambiguous class after a comma, gated by C6's case contrast.** The stricter chunk test is because a hyphen also joins names (`Smith-Jones`). Together they moved 1,782 distinct ctgov readings (1,254 and 759, overlapping on 231), nothing in PSC, the committed corpora or the agreement records; FEC is one case, so the contrast never admits a word there.
  6. **A split credential merges only where its first chunk has two or more letters, and the merged piece is a member of the ambiguous class, decided by C4's count and S4's words to spare; it starts S8's run (#827), landing after the numeral predicate answers the numeral before a credential.** FEC decided the opening letter: it writes a middle initial before a trailing salutation (`SMITH, JOHN A. MS.`), and `A. MS.` folds to the listed `ams` (merging any pair ending its part, but for two single letters, moved 5,122 FEC names, `A. MS.` 2,246 of them). A two-letter opening chunk needs the count: the 30 FEC merges it made all stood behind a one-word family (`SAM E.` → `same`), against 351 of 352 ctgov merges behind two or more name words. Accepted cost: `M. D.` and `M D` stay unmerged. Left to the split-credential PR: rules/S8 refuses a member of the ambiguous class as a run start, so how a merged piece can be both is that PR's to state (today's `Ph. D.` merge is unambiguous, and S8's #827 row covers it). (Carried out, and the run start stated, by the 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2e entry below, decisions/S12. Corrected there too: `M. Sc.` opens with a single letter as well and stays unmerged, so the tally's figure of officials still reversing left it out.)
  7. **A word taken into the credential's run by position alone (`John Smith PhD Jones`) gets no new ambiguity kind.** It is not a clash between two readings; the surfaced reading's source tells it from a word with a declared name reading (item 13). This answers #785's fourth question; its first three (case drops the report and starts the run, the one-case row keeps its report) land through the pool (carried out by phase 2g, the 2026-10-25 entry below), since retagging the word was measured to erase its other readings (`Doe, John DO` became family `DO Doe` once `do` stopped being an ambiguous acronym, its particle reading meeting P6).
  8. (narrowed by the 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2f entry: in the given part after a family comma a one-letter numeral the vocabulary lists stays the suffix, and a one-letter numeral before a credential stays a name word) **Every numeral rule asks one predicate, answering for `i` to `x` the way S10's trailing fork does (shape, the count for one letter, an initial before it, the period, the case), with three readings stated:** a lone letter ending the given part ahead of a further comma is the initial (#819; `Smith, John PSM I, Jr.` the accepted cost), a lone letter in a given part after a family comma stays the initial, and a Title-case numeral in a name of mixed case leans to the name. It answers #784 (`John Smith VI MD` reads family `VI` where `III` starts the run) and #774.
  9. **Generation readings are gated by their kind's cue, and a reading with none is `default = false`, compiled to nothing by default and turned on by a pack in phase 3.** `fils` is gated by case and the count. The Brazilian agnomens `Filho`, `Filha`, `Neto`, `Neta`, `Netto`, `Sobrinho` and `Sobrinha` become gated generation readings in `pt.toml` (`pt-BR` where only Brazil writes one), with the count as their only cue: they are written in the case of the name around them. In tse-donors, 405 filed/registry pairs differ only by a trailing agnomen; master gives both spellings the same last family word in 1, and a gate emulated as an ambiguous acronym, so carrying S6's Title-case lean beside the count, in all 405, keeping every PSC surname (`Ana Neto`) and FEC given name (`SMITH, NETO`) the plain reading broke, by the count and the lean. The count-only gate the design chooses was not measured: it is to be measured before 2h lands. They are not part of the surname: a surname is what a family shares, and an agnomen marks the bearer against a relative of the same name, as `Júnior` does. Spanish `H.` (*hijo*), German regnal `I.` and Czech `st.` are `default = false`. When 2h lands it replaces decisions/generational-words' Standing line that the agnomens stay family names.
  10. **Decision 10 is unparked at the head of the name only: a modifier is a title only beside a head title there, never in a comma part,** after `resident` and `rt` move to `occupation` and `pro` to `academic`. At the head it fixed 177 PSC records and broke 7; in a comma part it reverses names whose part after the comma is the person's role (247 of 367 ctgov comma-part moves, the part being exactly `Resident` 120 times and `resident` 50, counted as the family taking master's given name).
  11. **A contested title's phrase partner is a modifier or a style before it, `of` after it, or a head title after it that the word `attaches` to;** a salutation is never one, and nor is a title before the word that is neither modifier nor style (#853). `attaches` is a property of a title reading naming the title kinds it qualifies, `chief` attaching to `office` keeping `Chief Justice`.
  12. **#853 lands first, as one rule: a title word with a declared given reading, standing after another title that is not its partner, is the given name. Given readings are then declared word by word, each batch its own measured PR;** `lama`, `lord`, `sir`, `lady`, `hon`, `baron` and `professor` stay titles, since PSC's filers type them in the title box; `title-or-name` is reported only where the rule decided against a declared name reading. Measured with nine words out of `titles` (`sheikh`, `shaikh`, `wing`, `prince`, `princess`, `sultan`, `sultana`, `mahdi`, `king`): the word after another title fixed 644 PSC records and broke none; alone at the head it fixed 152 and set 24 aside (title-box filings the scorer stops scoring once the word is no longer listed). That run took the words out of `titles`; the #853 rule is to be measured on its own before it lands. The recommended order, by forename against title-box filings in PSC, was `sultan`, `mahdi`, `sultana`, `wing`, `sheikh`/`shaikh`, `princess`, `prince`, `king`; it and two additions were re-decided in the 2026-10-24 #752 entry. `md` was settled outside phase 2 by #860.
  13. **The reading not taken surfaces as `Ambiguity.alternative` in a 2.x minor, with the pickle guard widened to accept a field added later and every narrow-guard skew still refused; `Token.reading` waits for 3.0.** The parser holds tags, not readings, so a token's reading needs a per-word reading index compiled for parse time, the first parse-time use of the TOML's descriptive data, and phase 1's import cost has to be re-taken.
  14. **Abbreviated given names outrank the leading-abbreviation title (#789's second half): `given` readings with `same_as` the full name, the English set, `ma` and `jo` in `fil.toml`, and the dotted `wm.`.** `ma` and `jo` fixed 86 PSC records (`Mrs Ma. Liza …` filings) and broke none; the English set moved 2 FEC names and nothing else. It changes rules/H2's own example, `Smith, Ma. John` → given `Ma.`. This is decision 20 applied in the default; decision 15, that name readings only keep the gate on, is about a clash between vocabulary readings that a pack settles, not a name reading against a shape reading. The first half, bare `wm` out of the titles, is Derek's 2026-10-22 split (the 2026-10-22 #789 entry), shipped in #873.
  15. **The reserved names: `attaches` and `default` gain their meaning in phase 2; `bare_only`, `leading_only`, `trailing_only` and `separators` stay reserved,** their messages naming the phase that needs them: the gate's slots carry position, rules/P7 settles `bare_only` by shape, and `separators` belongs with `attached`'s other side in phase 4.
  16. **The bracket escape reads the derived clash (#810): a bracketed member of the ambiguous class escapes the nickname reading where its writing settles it, with interior periods, or in capitals in a name of mixed case, the case asked of the whole name.** `(Ed)` stays a nickname in every case, and so does content standing inside the name rather than after it (`Jean 'JD' Smith`, whose `JD` an escape asking the word's own case moved to the middle name). Recorded in rules/S1 when 2l lands.
  17. **PSC's fielded names are the main measure for every phase-2 behavior PR, which reports fixed, broken and changed records beside the committed snapshot,** since it is the one corpus whose filers separated the fields; spelling consistency is read beside it, never instead.

  The rules: rules/S8 carries #827's row, which S8's present wording already covers. Every other behavior settled here changes a rule's statement (rules/H2's own example, rules/S1's Except for the ambiguous class, the numeral wording of rules/S8 and rules/S10, and the front of the name), so its rules amendment lands with its sub-phase PR under the same-PR amendment rule rather than as `deviates:` rows now.

  Declined, with evidence:
  - THE RFC'S PARTNER WORDING, "after another title word or a modifier, before a phrase link or before a head title": with `dean`, `justice` and `major` given readings it broke 1,314 PSC records (`Mr Dean Smith` → title `Mr Dean`).
  - ANY TITLE AFTER THE WORD AS A PARTNER: 99 of the 106 FEC names it moved were a given name and its trailing salutation read as a phrase (`SMITH, DEAN MR.`), so only a head the word `attaches` to is kept (item 11).
  - LISTING `vi` TO `x` AS SUFFIX WORDS: 152 FEC moves, about 130 wrong (`SMITH, JOHN X`, a middle initial read as a generation), and S10's report silenced on every spelling.
  - RETAGGING A CAPITALS CREDENTIAL AS UNAMBIGUOUS (#785): it erased the word's other readings (item 7).
  - THE AGNOMENS AS PLAIN GENERATION READINGS: 32 PSC records broken, `Ana Neto` among them.
  - MERGING ANY ADJACENT PAIR INTO A CREDENTIAL: 5,890 FEC moves, the middle initial before a salutation (item 6).
  - DECISION 10 IN A COMMA PART: it reverses the names whose comma part is the role (item 10).

- **2026-10-24 #752 (Derek) — THE `;` CHANGE IS A 2.x CHANGE OUTSIDE PHASE 2, AND THE TITLE BATCHES GO IN ORDER OF LONE-HEAD BREAKAGE, WITH `buddha`, `siddha` AND `gyani` IN THE DEFAULT GATE AND `guru` LEFT TO A `ne` PACK.**
  - **`;`.** Derek decided to investigate adding `;` to the default suffix delimiters and making a trailing delimiter count, as a 2.x change outside phase 2. It shipped as #871: of the 494 ctgov names holding a `;`, 443 moved, 438 of them a name read reversed (its given name taken from after the comma) now read with its given name from before it, and nothing moved in PSC, FEC or tse.
  - **The order.** Derek: "use lone-head breakage". Ranked from the nine-word measurement without `md` (the 2026-10-23 #752 entry's item 12), fewest title-box filings set aside first, ties by more fixed: `wing` (25 fixed, 0 set aside); `mahdi` with `bard` (19, 0; `bard` 1, 0); `buddha`, `siddha`, `gyani`; `king` (12, 0; its cost is the royals the committed corpora hold, `King Henry`, which PSC does not); `sultana` (8, 0); `sultan` (19, 1); `shaikh` (7, 1); `princess` (10, 4); `prince` (25, 6); `sheikh` (27, 12). `sheikh` and `shaikh` are separate batches. It replaces the order by forename against title-box filings recorded in the 2026-10-23 #752 entry's item 12.
  - **#867 and #869's words.** `bard` (an occupation title from the 2014 list; the Norwegian given name `Bård`) joins `mahdi`: with both out of `titles`, PSC 79 fixed, 0 broken; FEC 20 names read right; committed 0. Derek agreed that `buddha`, `siddha` and `gyani` go in the default gate, which nepal-candidates measured at 120 romanized/Devanagari pairs brought to agree (172 names open with the four words) at no committed, PSC or FEC cost. `guru` goes to a `ne` pack in phase 3, beside #870's `श्री`: its title reading is #346's renunciate class, its Devanagari twin `गुरु` must move with it (44 nepal pairs go apart when only the Latin spelling moves), and the committed corpora's `Guru Nanak` and `Guru Gobind Singh` read it as a title.

- **2026-10-24 #872 — THE RE-MEASUREMENT ON `638685b4` AND THE PRS THAT MERGED AFTER IT ADD THE PAIRED-INITIALS PREDICATE TO THE 2026-10-23 #752 ENTRY'S SPLIT CREDENTIALS, CHANGING NO AGREED DECISION.**
  - **Paired initials.** #872 made the split-credential judgment for the joined spelling (`A.B.`, two dotted single letters in a script with capitals, outside the slot right after a comma), so the split merge asks #872's `is_paired_initials` for that case once its inline script condition is lifted into a predicate that classify and the split merge share. C5's comma reading keeps the bare predicate: `John Smith, พ.บ.` reads given `พ.บ.` today, and moving it would change that.
  - Marking `sa` possibly a name, #857's first proposal and measured in the same re-measurement, was declined for its removal: decisions/C8's 2026-10-25 #857 entry.
