- **2026-10-25 #752 — A CLASH WITH A NAME IS A NAME READING: phase 2a derives the two name-clash markers, carrying out items 1 to 3 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry.** SUPERSEDES: in 2026-10-18 #737, the flags bullet, the stated-on-every-particle bullet and the Exclusions bullet's `may_be_given = false` beside an exclusion of it (only behavioral properties may be excluded, and `may_be_given` is now a stated one); in 2026-10-19 #749, the `may_be_given` agreement and the unexamined-word convention keyed on `may_be_given = true`, re-keyed on the given reading. No compiled set moves: `tools/vocab/build.py` rebuilds `nameparser/config/_compiled.py` byte for byte. `SUFFIX_ACRONYMS_AMBIGUOUS` is now the words with an acronym suffix reading and a `given` or `family` reading, and the particles outside `NON_GIVEN_NAME_PARTICLES` the words with a particle reading and a `given` reading, each wherever in the files the two readings sit (build check 11). The model's reason (decision 15, name readings keep the gate on) is unchanged; what changes is that the reading, not a flag beside it, is what the build reads, so a clash cannot be marked without the evidence that it is one, nor carry evidence and stay unmarked.
  - **The readings written.** Every flagged word that lacked one got a name reading: the seven credentials `ba`, `do`, `ed`, `jd`, `lac`, `ma` and `meng`, and the 38 flagged particles other than `bon` and `dí`, `do` among them, so 44 words and 49 readings (a credential with both a Census and an SSA count got both). The counts are `tools/name_usage.py`'s, taken 2026-10-25 and recorded as each reading's `as_of`.
  - **A name reading may be declared without a count, with a required `note` saying why.** Derivation needs a `given` reading on every flagged particle, and some are borne as given names in no source the tool reads; refusing a count-less reading would have forced each of those to the never-given side, against decisions/vocabulary-collisions C-i's "under uncertainty, ambiguous". Eight particle words have no count in any source (`aen`, `bat`, `binte`, `freiherr`, `freiherrin`, `thoe`, `ua`, `vande`); each keeps its may-be-given reading on decisions/vocabulary-collisions C-i's default or, for `binte`, beside `binti` (#604), with a required `note` saying so. The build refuses a count-less name reading with no `note`. The unexamined-word test of nameparser/vocab/README.md keeps its shape, keyed on the given reading: a word whose particle readings carry no `note` is unexamined, whatever counts its given reading carries, since those were taken for the derivation and not read against the particle position.
  - **`ld` keeps its count in its credential reading's `note` and has no name reading, so it stays unmarked.** Its `given` reading recorded the evidence for NOT marking it (45 SSA births against 11,703 NPPES providers), which derivation would have read as a mark. The count moved into the credential reading's `note`, so a name reading that keeps the gate off, contradicting decision 15, never exists. Declined: deriving it literally, which moves 3 ClinicalTrials.gov and 5 FEC names, measured 2026-10-25 by writing the given reading back on this change; psc-individuals holds no record writing it, so it cannot move there.
  - **A particle with no given reading still states `may_be_given = false`, required,** so a new particle never reaches the never-given side unremarked, the reason the property had no default in phase 1; `may_be_given = true` and `may_be_name` (either value) are refused with a message naming the reading to write, and `false` beside a `given` reading is refused as contradicting it. Check 9 now asks for a `given` reading on a bound given name that is also a particle. `may_be_given` is a STATED property in the README's table: held to the readings, compiled to nothing by itself.
  - **`may_be_initial` stays a property.** Its clash is with the initial, a shape every cased single letter has, and which letters a tradition writes both ways is a judgment about the letter, not a name anyone bears.
  - **Measured (2026-10-25, against 632685c8):** `tools/differential/snapshot.py` matches; `cldr_agreement.py` matches every record; `snapshot.py --local` for ctgov-officials, fec-individuals and tse-donors, recorded from the base with `--tree`, matches; `local_agreement.py psc-individuals` matches the record. Negative control: dropping `jd`'s given reading takes it out of `SUFFIX_ACRONYMS_AMBIGUOUS`, and dropping `van`'s, with `may_be_given = false` written, puts it in `NON_GIVEN_NAME_PARTICLES` (tests/v2/test_vocab.py).
  - **Recompute** rather than quoting: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind given --count`; the markers' sizes, `uv run python -c "from nameparser.config import suffixes, particles; print(len(suffixes.SUFFIX_ACRONYMS_AMBIGUOUS), len(particles.PARTICLES - particles.NON_GIVEN_NAME_PARTICLES))"`.

- **2026-10-25 #752 — one predicate per question (neutral).** Three questions that #752's later changes widen had their answers copied between sites, and each now has a shared predicate in `_pipeline/_vocab.py` for the widening to land in (mechanisms.md#ONE-PREDICATE-PER-QUESTION). Two are whole: every site asks them. The numeral's is not yet: the bullet below names the sites found still recognizing a numeral on their own. No reading moves.
  - **`split_pair(first, second)`**, the split credential (`Ph.` `D.`, decisions/phd-merge): group's merge, `_comma._pieces` and `is_wholly_suffix` each held their own copy of the two-pattern test. The test runs per adjacent pair, so each caller asks `SPLIT_PAIR_OPENS`, the predicate's own first-word pattern, C-level before the call, and a name holding no `Ph.` pays no frame. The split-credential change widens both together (item 6 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry above).
  - **`reads_as_generation(text, preceding, one_case)`**, the numeral: `is_trailing_numeral_suffix` renamed, the predicate every numeral rule is to ask (item 8 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry above, #784, #774, #819). Today it is the fork's SHAPE half alone. rules/C12's evidence asks a broader question beside its call: whether the word ending the part before the comma is any suffix word, by the suffix-word tag (`John Smith Jr., AD`, `John Smith Esq., AD`), or reads as a generation by the predicate (`John Smith VI, AD`); the tag stays at the call site because `Jr.` and `Esq.` are no numerals. The listed numerals belong in the predicate too, or each numeral rule tests the vocabulary itself again, the hand copy #656's first C12 draft was (it asked the suffix-word tag and missed `VI`). The shape test already takes the listed `ii` to `iv`, so reading the vocabulary inside it adds only the one-letter `i` and `v`, and that moves readings, which this PR may not: answered yes on a grid of 3,927 readings (17 heads, 11 numerals, 7 tails, three orders; the second review, 2026-10-25 on 30dc7aac), `i` and `v` moved 375 readings at the peel's fork, 348 of them in their fields (`John J. V` from middle 'J.', family 'V' to family 'J.', suffix 'V', with a new report), and 144 reports at `reads_as_a_reported_numeral` with no field moving (`John J. V MD` gains a `suffix-or-name`), the ones those sites meet carrying the `initial` tag (`I`, `V`, and their one-case spellings; a lower-case `v` in a name of mixed case carries none, and the peel takes it as a suffix word). Adding `ii` to `iv` alone moved nothing. That half is left to the change that has every numeral rule ask the predicate. Until it lands (it did: the 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2f entry below, which asks the predicate at every site listed here, the peel taking `ii` to `iv` by the suffix test, which the predicate answers alike), these sites recognize a numeral on their own, each keeping the answer it had (found 2026-10-25 by two reviews and a grep of `_pipeline/` for the numeral pattern, the suffix-word tag and the suffix predicates; a list found by search, not a proof that no other site exists): the predicate's own missing `i` and `v`, above; rules/S8's run start asks the vocabulary alone (`John Smith III MD` → suffix 'III MD', `John Smith VI MD` → family 'VI', #784); rules/C1's listing form asks the suffix-word tag (`John Smith III, M.J.` keeps suffix 'III' where `John Smith VI, M.J.` and `John Smith V, M.J.` read family 'John Smith VI' and 'John Smith V', #774); the peel takes `ii` to `iv` through `is_suffix_piece` before its fork asks the predicate; rules/C9's walk over the given part after a family comma asks `is_suffix_lenient`, the vocabulary alone (`Smith, John V` → suffix 'V', `Smith, John VI` and `Smith, John X` → middle, where `John Smith VI` reads suffix 'VI'); and after a suffix word in that part a listed one-letter numeral is taken by its tags (`_pieces._numeral_behind_the_initial_veto`), so `Smith, John PhD V` reads suffix 'PhD V' with no report where `Smith, John PhD VI` reports `suffix-or-name`.
  - **`is_cased_paired_initials(text)`**, rules/S3's paired initials in a script with capitals: classify asked the script half inline (#872, decisions/S3). This is the lift the 2026-10-24 #872 entry above names, for classify and the split merge to share. One overlap is left to the split-credential PR to state: item 6 refuses a split credential whose first chunk is a single letter, so for a split `A. B.` that length test answers before this predicate is asked. (Stated by the phase 2e entry below, decisions/S12: the length test refuses every other opening letter, and two dotted single letters are asked of this predicate, merging only in a script without capitals.) rules/C5's comma reading keeps the bare `is_paired_initials`, since after a comma a caseless pair reads as a name too (`John Smith, พ.บ.` → given 'พ.บ.'), and moving C5 onto this predicate would change that reading.
  - **Measured neutral** against the branch, first on 632685c8 and again on 2dd0414b once phase 2a had merged: a grid of 20,364 texts (22 heads covering no comma, a whole name, one word and nothing before a comma, a nickname-only head, a maiden clause, a family comma with a title-particle head and a quoted clause before the pair; 33 spellings including `MA`, `M.D.`, `X.Y.Z.`, `XYZ`, `Ph. D.` and its case variants, `V`, `VI`, `III`, `XI`, `M.J.`, `K.C.` and `พ.บ.`; each text also in lower and upper case), under all three orders, read identically (fields, ambiguity kinds and details, token roles and tags, `initials()`, `capitalized()`), and the same harness reported 45,871, 18, 3,974 and 2,986 differing readings with `split_pair`, C12's call to the numeral predicate, the cased half and the peel's call to it each knocked out. The old and new predicates gave the same answer on 19,769 direct calls. The committed snapshot, every CLDR, GND, Wikidata and conventions agreement record, psc-individuals' agreement record and the ctgov-officials, fec-individuals and tse-donors local snapshots (each recorded from the base with `--tree`) all match, on both bases. psc-individuals' record was itself stale against 632685c8 (3 regressions, 5 progress, 5 changed before this change), so it was re-recorded from each base first.
  - **Frames**, counted with `sys.setprofile` on both trees: `_CALL_BASELINE`'s reference name and `Smith, John` cost what they did (305 and 158). A word matching `SPLIT_PAIR_OPENS` (`Ph`, `ph.`) with a word after it pays one frame at each site that tests it, whether or not a `D.` follows (`John Smith Ph. D.` 233 → 234, `Smith, John Ph. D.` 239 → 241; the review counted `ph ph ph ph ph ph` 282 → 286), so the cost grows only with the number of such words. C12's evidence keeps its tag test ahead of the call, so it costs what it did (`John Smith Jr., AD` 372). That is a rare shape that no guard counts.
- **2026-10-25 #752, phase 2f — EVERY NUMERAL RULE ASKS ONE PREDICATE.** (Its expectation that the split-credential sub-phase keep `John Smith V Ph. D.` as suffix 'V Ph. D.' superseded by the phase 2e entry below, decisions/S12, which reads it as `John Smith V PhD` reads, family 'V'.) Carries out item 8 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry above (#784, #774, #819), and the list of sites in the 2026-10-25 one-predicate entry. `_vocab.reads_as_generation(text, tags, preceding, one_case)` answers for every spelling from `i` to `x` as S10's trailing fork does: the shape on the word without its trailing periods; a single letter written with its period an initial (rules/T5); a numeral the vocabulary lists in two or more letters (`ii` to `iv`, read from the token's `vocab:suffix` tag, not copied at a call site) the generation whatever stands around it; for the rest, an initial in front a name word, and a Title-case numeral in a name of mixed case declined, which `numeral_leans_to_the_name` tells apart so the decline is reported. `preceding` is None where a slot asks no initial in front, the given part after a family comma, where the predicate reads a one-letter numeral by the vocabulary. The count for one letter stays the caller's, which alone knows where the name's words are.
  - **The sites.** SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-11-05 (#1080, #1093): before a family comma the count is over the name words, a piece made wholly of particles or connectives counting as none, save a generational connective beside the numeral that P13 did not link (#1097, decisions/P13), not over every piece (decisions/S10's 2026-11-05 #1080 and #1093 entries). The trailing peel asks it of a numeral anywhere in the run, behind the suffix test, so `John Smith VI MD` reads suffix 'VI MD' as `III MD` does (#784); rules/S8's run start asks it of a numeral of two or more letters (`John Smith VI Jones` → suffix 'VI Jones'); rules/C12's evidence asks it with the token's tags, its broader suffix-word test still beside the call; C1's listing form asks it of a numeral ending the family part, with S10's count over that part's pieces (`John Smith V, M.J.` and `John Smith VI, M.J.` keep the suffix, #774); the given part's walk asks it of a numeral of two or more letters (`Smith, John VI` → suffix 'VI') and of the bare one-letter numeral its last-piece test reads, and a one-letter numeral behind a suffix word in a part of suffix words asks it too; the maiden take and `tail_reading` reach it through the peel, `trailing_candidates` with no case (its superset); the report on a numeral a post-nominal follows asks it, with the Title-case decline. A numeral of two or more letters read by its shape alone reports `suffix-or-name` wherever it is taken, the run start and the given part included.
  - **The three readings agreed on 2026-10-23.** #819: a lone letter ending the given part ahead of a further comma is the middle initial, the credential run in front stopping short of it (decisions/C9's 2026-10-25 #819 entry; `Smith, John PSM I, Jr.` the accepted cost). FEC's constraint: in that part a one-letter numeral is the generation only where listed, so `SMITH, JOHN X` keeps middle 'X'. A Title-case numeral in a mixed-case name leans to the name (`Nguyen Van Vi` → family 'Van Vi', reported); a numeral the vocabulary lists in two or more letters is not leaned (`John Smith Iii` keeps suffix 'Iii'), being unambiguous vocabulary as an unambiguous credential is.
  - **One reading kept rather than widened: a one-letter numeral before a credential stays a name word** (decisions/S10's #721 entry, `John Smith V PhD` → family 'V', reported). Reading it with the count instead moved 6 tse-donors names, every one wrong: a lone `V` or `I` behind a surname or a particle before `JR` or `JUNIOR` (shape `Aa A Aa de V JUNIOR` read family 'de', suffix 'V JUNIOR'), where the letter abbreviates a surname; and 3 committed names (`John Smith V PhD`, `Jane Doe nee Smith V PhD`, `Jane Doe nee Smith V MA`). A split credential behind such a numeral (`John Smith V Ph. D.`) is left to the split-credential sub-phase (item 6), which must keep its suffix 'V Ph. D.' by its own rule.
  - **Measured** (2026-10-25, against master 2af8da8e, each local baseline re-recorded from that checkout with `--tree` immediately before its check, since the shared cache's `snapshot.jsonl` is overwritten by any session measuring in parallel): the committed snapshot moves 6 names, every one intended: the three GND regnal rows (`Georg VI.`, `Konstantin VII.`, `Bonifatius VIII.`, family → suffix), `Smith VI, AD` (family 'Smith VI' → 'Smith', suffix 'VI'), and #774's `John Smith V, M.J.` and `John Smith VI, M.J.`. GND agreement goes from 618 to 621 of 801 on family and 779 to 782 on suffix, three progress rows each, and every other agreement record matches. ctgov-officials moves 1 reading (a job title ending in a level before a comma, `Aa Aa I, A` → suffix 'I', reported); fec-individuals 22, each a numeral ending the given part or the family part (`A, A VI` → suffix 'VI'; `A VI, A` → suffix 'VI'; `A A V, A` → suffix 'V'), about 14 read as generations and about 8 a middle name or a fragment read as one, all reported; tse-donors none. psc-individuals (1,039,171 records scored): 3 fixed, all Vietnamese names ending in the surname `Vi` that the Title-case lean keeps a name; 1 broken, a Title-case `Viii` written as the bearer's generation, which the lean reads as a name (the lean's cost, rules/S10); 1 changed, a record whose title box the scorer sets aside. Spike figures above (listing `vi` to `x`: 152 FEC moves) are the 2026-10-23 entry's and were not re-taken.
  - **Recompute:** `uv run python tools/differential/snapshot.py` on the parent of this change's merge with its code, and for each local corpus `snapshot.py --local <source> --record --tree <base checkout>` then `snapshot.py --local <source>` from this tree, back to back; `uv run python tools/differential/local_agreement.py psc-individuals` the same way, `--list N` printing the moved records locally.
- **2026-10-25 #752 — phase 2c: a credential joined by a hyphen or a slash reads as one where every chunk counts as a suffix as written**, carrying out the listed half of item 5 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry. `John Smith, MD/PhD` reads suffix 'MD/PhD' where it read given 'MD/PhD', family 'John Smith'; a bare member of the ambiguous class is no chunk the test counts (`John Smith, MA/PhD` keeps its reading). The record, with what was measured and what was left unread, is decisions/S3's entry of this date (rules/S3).
- **2026-10-25 #752 — phase 2d: an unlisted word of capitals joined by a hyphen or a slash is the caps shape**, carrying out the unlisted half of item 5 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry (#797's half A). `John Smith, PU-PH` reads suffix 'PU-PH', reported, where it read given 'PU-PH', family 'John Smith'; C6's case contrast gates it after a comma as it gates the unjoined word. The record is decisions/S9's entry of this date (rules/S9, rules/C6).
- **2026-10-25 #752, phase 2e — A CREDENTIAL SPLIT ACROSS A SPACE IS ONE WORD, A MEMBER OF THE AMBIGUOUS CLASS THE COUNT DECIDES**, carrying out item 6 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry and #827's reading (A). `John Smith, Pharm D` reads suffix 'Pharm D' where it read given 'Pharm', middle 'D', family 'John Smith'; `John Smith Ph. D. Jones` reads suffix 'Ph. D. Jones' as `John Smith PhD Jones` does; `SMITH, SAM E.` keeps given 'SAM', the count declining the pair. The record, with what was measured, is decisions/S12's entry of this date (rules/S12).

- **2026-10-25 #752, phase 2h (its "the kind says what the writing means" superseded by decisions/S11's 2026-11-03 (Derek), #1039 entry: a reading's `title_case_is_name` says it) — GENERATION READINGS ARE GATED BY KIND, AND `default = false` HAS ITS MEANING.** Carries out item 9 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry above and its reserved-names item. SUPERSEDES: in 2026-10-18 #737, `default` as a reserved name. Three changes, each one the design's:
  - A suffix word reading beside a name reading derives `SUFFIX_WORDS_AMBIGUOUS` (`Lexicon.suffix_words_ambiguous`), gated as an ambiguous acronym is, by the words to spare, at the same sites; the kind says what the writing means. A new kind, `agnomen`, records the Brazilian agnomens (`filho`, `filha`, `neto`, `neta`, `sobrinho`, `sobrinha` in `pt.toml`, `netto` in `pt-BR.toml`), whose writing never leans and which `AGNOMENS` (`Lexicon.agnomens`) lists; build check 11 refuses an agnomen with no name reading, since one would take the family name from every two-word name it ends. `fils` takes a `generation` reading beside its family reading, its exclusion gone. No shipped suffix word had a name reading before, so nothing else moves. The rule is rules/S11 and the record, with what was measured and what was accepted, decisions/S11.
  - `default = false` is a behavioral property of every category but `name`: the reading is held to the per-entry checks and compiles into no set and no marker, and checks 4, 5, 6 and 9 do not weigh it, so it may stand beside a live reading of the word in another file and beside another language's exclusion of it. `default = true` is refused, being every reading's unwritten value. The first three are the readings with no cue: Spanish `h` (*hijo*) and the German regnal ordinal on `i`, each written with the period that makes a one-letter word an initial (rules/T5), and Czech `st` (*starší*), whose `cs.toml` exclusion it replaces. No reading moves.
  - The other reserved names keep their reservation and name the phase that needs them: `attaches` phase 2 (its front-of-the-name work), `prefer` and `strict` phase 3, and `bare_only`, `leading_only`, `trailing_only` and `separators` phase 4, the gate's slots carrying position and shape settling a bare letter (rules/P7).
  Adding the two fields changes `Lexicon`'s pickle layout, as 2.6 did.
- **2026-10-25 #752, phase 2g — CAPITALS SETTLE A LISTED CREDENTIAL**, carrying out item 7 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry above (#785's first three questions). In a name written in more than one case a listed member of the ambiguous class written in capitals reports nothing where it read as the credential (`John Smith MA`, `John Smith, MA`, `Doe, John DO`), and it starts the credential's run as its dotted spelling does (`John Smith MA Jones` → suffix 'MA Jones'). The word keeps its tags, so the clash settled is the one with the name reading alone: `Doe, John DO` keeps family 'Doe', the particle reading still refusing the run start, and `Jane Doe nee MA` keeps its maiden name and its report. A name in one case keeps its report (`JOHN SMITH MA`). The record, with what was measured and the accepted cost, is decisions/S6's entry of this date (rules/S6, S8).
- **2026-10-25 #752, phase 2i — A TITLE THAT MAY BE A GIVEN NAME IS DERIVED, AND `attaches` HAS ITS MEANING.** Carries out items 10 to 12 and the `attaches` half of item 15 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry; the rule and its measurement are decisions/H9's entry of this date (rules/H9). What changes in the vocabulary:
  - A title reading beside a `given` reading derives a fourth marker, `TITLES_AMBIGUOUS` (`Lexicon.titles_ambiguous`), and the word compiles into it INSTEAD of `TITLES`, so a caller who lists it as a title has it read as one everywhere, the 1.4 recipe (`c.titles.add("dean")`). A title that is also a particle keeps its given reading for the particle's marker and stays in `TITLES` (`st`, `freiherr`). A `family` reading alone beside a title marks nothing, as before.
  - Thirteen salutations carried a `given` reading recorded on 2026-10-24 as H8's evidence (#835), which the derivation would have read as the clash (`Mr Smith` → given 'Mr'): `bwana`, `goodman`, `gospodin`, `herr`, `master`, `mister`, `mr`, `señora`, `señorita`, `signora`, `sra`, `श्री` and `মোঃ`. Their counts move into the salutation reading's `note`, the README's convention for counts kept as evidence against marking (item 2's `ld`). No compiled set moves for them. Recompute: `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --kind salutation`.
  - `attaches`, a behavioral title property, is a non-empty list of title kinds, held to one value per word across files (check 5), and compiles into `TITLE_PHRASES` (`Lexicon.title_phrases`): the two-word phrases the word makes with every title word of those kinds. `chief` attaches to office and military, `major` to military. Two more sets compile from the kinds: `TITLE_MODIFIERS`, the words every title reading of which is a modifier, and `TITLE_STYLES`.
  - Reclassified, as item 10 asked and as the build found: `resident` and `rt` to occupation, `pro` to academic, `d'affaires` and `member` to office (decisions/H9).
  - `dean`, `justice` and `major` gain an office or military reading beside a dated `given` one; `justice`'s exclusion (#606) is replaced by the reading. The batches of 2j declare the rest.
  Adding the four fields changes `Lexicon`'s pickle layout, as phase 2h did.

A list entry that is no kind must be a word with a title reading (a build check, its mutation in tests/v2/test_vocab.py). `major` attaches to `general` and `sergeant` and `sgt` to `major`. `grace` (style) and `marshal` (military, its #606 exclusion replaced) carry dated `given` readings. The measurement and Derek's answers are decisions/H9's entry of this date.

- **2026-10-27 #766 — FIVE UNATTESTED READINGS REFUSED: `bon`, `dí` and `heer` are no particles, `herren` and `heren` no titles.** Each reading is replaced by an exclusion quoting the evidence, and each word keeps or gains its name readings with dated counts (`heer`: Census 2010 surname 2,007, Wikidata family 113, beside its SSA given count; `herren` and `heren` re-counted unchanged). None had a recorded source: `bon` and `dí` came with the 2011 and 2012 prefix lists and #749 found no particle use; `heer`'s only source was #18's `Johan de heer Van Kampen`, two real names run together; `herren` and `heren` came unvetted with #99's honorifics, and a plural addresses several people. `PARTICLES` loses three words and `TITLES` two, `heren` leaving `AFTER_GIVEN_TITLES` with it. As name words they read as any other: `Marie Bon Durand` → middle 'Bon', family 'Durand' (was family 'Bon Durand'); `Herren Hans` → given 'Herren' (was title 'Herren'); `Doe, Jan Heren` → middle 'Heren', 1.4.0's reading, where 2.6.0 read title 'Heren' (decisions/H8); and a leading `Bon` or `Heer` no longer reports `particle-or-given`. Measured 2026-10-27 against 6ae585f3 with `tools/differential/measure.py`: no committed corpus name moves (`tests/v2/test_snapshot.py`, the CLDR and GND records). The local corpora moved 79 entries (ctgov-officials 14, fec-individuals 9, jrc-names 30, psc-individuals 20, tse-donors 6; nepal-candidates, tw-donors and ukec-individuals none), of four shapes: a surname of a real particle and the word (`de Heer`, `le Bon`, `van Bon`), whose base is now the word, so `initials()` no longer counts the particle and case repair writes the particle as it writes any other; the word between the given name and the surname, now a middle name, where psc-individuals' filers typed it (4 records now agree with their boxes, none newly disagree); a leading `Bon` or `Heer` given name losing its `particle-or-given` report; and `Herren` or `Heren` opening a name, now the given name. The accepted cost is a compound surname written with `Bon` as its own first word (`Bon Valsassina`, the stage name `Bon Jovi`), now middle 'Bon', as any double surname reads by position (`Policy(two_family_names=True)` is the opt-in). jrc-names reads 5 more persons' spellings alike. Recompute: `uv run python tools/differential/measure.py --base <a checkout of 6ae585f3>`, and the refusals with `uv run python tools/vocab/query.py --excluded particle` and `--excluded salutation`. `und.toml` keeps both words, so it is not empty.
- **2026-10-27 #752, phase 2j, `sheikh` and its spellings (Derek) — THE NINTH AND LAST TITLE BATCH: `sheikh`, `sheik`, `shekh`, `shaik` and `cheikh` have dated `given` readings**, so each is a title only inside a title phrase; `shaykh` stays a title. `Sheikh Ahmed Khan`, `Mr Sheikh Ahmed` and `Cheikh Anta Diop` read the given name; a ruler written with the bare title does too (`Sheikh Mohammed bin Rashid`), the cost Derek accepted on 2026-10-27. With it every word item 12's measurement named has its reading. The measurement is decisions/H9's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-27 #752, phase 2j, `prince` and `princess` (Derek) — THE EIGHTH TITLE BATCH: both have a dated `given` reading**, so each is a title only inside a title phrase. `Prince Fielder`, `Mr Prince Smith` and `Smith, Princess` read the given name; a royal written with the bare title does too (`Prince Harry`), the class accepted for `king`, `sultan` and `shaikh`, accepted by Derek on 2026-10-27. `His Royal Highness Prince Harry` and `Prince of Wales` keep the title. The measurement is decisions/H9's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-26 #752, phase 2j, `shaikh` (Derek) — THE SEVENTH TITLE BATCH: `shaikh` has a dated `given` reading**, so the style is a title only inside a title phrase. `Shaikh Ahmed Khan` and `Mr Shaikh Ahmed` read given 'Shaikh'; a ruler written with the bare title does too (`Shaikh Hamad bin Isa`), the class accepted for `king` and `sultan`, the larger share of rulers Derek accepted on 2026-10-27. `sheikh` keeps its own batch. The measurement is decisions/H9's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-26 #752, phase 2j, `sultana` and `sultan` (Derek) — THE FIFTH AND SIXTH TITLE BATCHES, IN ONE PR: both have dated `given` readings**, `sultan`'s on its tr.toml entry, so each is a title only inside a title phrase. `Sultana Begum` and `Sultan Khan` read the given name; a sultan written with the bare title does too (`Sultan Hassanal Bolkiah`), the cost accepted for `king`. Derek combined the two batches on 2026-10-26. The measurement is decisions/H9's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-26 #752, phase 2j, `king` (Derek) — THE FOURTH TITLE BATCH: `king` has a dated `given` reading**, US SSA 37,890 births and Census rank 34 as a surname, so it is a title only inside a title phrase. `King Curtis` and `Mr King Smith` read given 'King'; a monarch written with the bare title does too (`King Henry`), the cost Derek accepted on 2026-10-26. `pope` replaces `king` as the worked example of a title that is also a surname wherever the rules and tests used it. The measurement is decisions/H9's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-26 #752, phase 2j, `buddha`, `siddha` and `gyani` (#869) — THE THIRD TITLE BATCH: each has a declared `given` reading**, `siddha`'s and `gyani`'s without a count, which their notes explain: the first halves of Nepali compound given names, which no source outside nepal-candidates counts. `Buddha Bahadur Tamang` reads given 'Buddha', middle 'Bahadur'; `guru` stays a title for a `ne` pack (the 2026-10-24 #752 (Derek) entry). The measurement is decisions/H9's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-26 #752, phase 2j, `mahdi` and `bard` (#867) — THE SECOND TITLE BATCH: both words have a dated `given` reading.** `Mahdi Karimi`, `Mr Mahdi Karimi`, `Karimi, Mahdi` and `Bard Larsen` read the given name, where 1.4.0 through 2.6.0 read a title; no listed phrase holds either word, so nothing is reported. The measurement is decisions/H9's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-26 #752, phase 2j, `wing` — THE FIRST TITLE BATCH: `wing` HAS A DATED `given` READING.** The order is the 2026-10-24 #752 (Derek) entry's. Decision 10 had already read `wing` as a name at the head of the name, so the reading reaches the parts decision 10 left as listed, after a comma and ending the name: `Lau, Wing Kai` reads given 'Wing', and `John Smith Wing.` family 'Wing.'. The rule and the measurement are decisions/H9's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-26 (Derek), #785 — PHASE 2g NARROWED: a capitals member with a name word behind it, and no family comma, does not start the credential's run**, the name word being evidence of initials; `Maria Ana MA Silva`, `Jean Pierre MA DUPONT` and `Mary Jane MA King` keep the readings 1.4.0 through 2.6.0 gave, and `John Smith MA Jones` keeping family 'Jones' is the cost. The record is decisions/S6's entry of this date.
- **2026-10-26 #789, #752, phase 2k — A GIVEN NAME WRITTEN SHORT IS A `given` READING WITH `same_as`, AND IT OUTRANKS rules/H2's SHAPE.** Carries out item 14 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry above and the second half of #789; the first half, bare `wm` out of the titles, is the 2026-10-22 #789 entry. SUPERSEDES: in the README's property table, `same_as` as a property of every category but `name`. What changes in the vocabulary:
  - A `given` reading may carry `same_as`, the full given name the word abbreviates (`chas` is `charles`). On a name reading it names a name, not an entry, so check 7 asks only that it be normalized and not the word itself, and a `family` reading is refused one. The reading compiles the word into a new set, `GIVEN_NAME_ABBREVIATIONS` (`Lexicon.given_name_abbreviations`, re-exported from `nameparser.config.given_names`), so an entry holding only such a reading is not the inert name reading the build warns of. No `Constants` attribute reaches it, and the shim passes it whole: a v1 caller who lists the word as a title has it read as one, the leading peel asking the title vocabulary before the shape.
  - The set's one reader is rules/H2's shape test, which declines a word in it, so the vocabulary reading outranks the shape-derived one, decision 20 applied in the default. It is not a derived marker: no second vocabulary reading is weighed, only the shape's. `ma`, which already had given, family and credential readings in en.toml, takes its abbreviation reading in fil.toml, and the credential's ambiguous marker is unchanged, its name readings already deriving it. `jo` had no reading before, and its `given` reading derives nothing.
  - The words, with their counts on each entry: `benj`, `chas`, `edw`, `geo`, `jas`, `jno`, `richd`, `robt`, `saml`, `thos` and `wm` in en.toml (the last keeping its refused title, whose reason now names this reading), `jo` and `ma` in fil.toml. What moved, the vetting and the grid are decisions/H2's entry of this date. Recompute the list: `uv run python -c "from nameparser import Lexicon; print(sorted(Lexicon.default().given_name_abbreviations))"`.
  Adding the field changes `Lexicon`'s pickle layout, as phases 2h and 2i did.
- **2026-10-26 #752, phase 2l — THE BRACKET ESCAPE READS THE DERIVED CLASH**, carrying out item 16 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry above (#810). A member of the ambiguous class, the class 2a derives from name readings, alone in brackets or quotes after the name is the suffix where its writing settles it (`John Smith (MA)`, `John Smith (M.A)` → suffix), read in one pass, and stays the nickname otherwise (`John Smith (Ed)`, `JOHN SMITH (MA)`, `Jeffrey (JD) Bricken`). It is recorded as rules/S13, a rule of its own rather than the amendment to S1 item 16 planned, the position it reads being evidence S1's signal does not read; the writing is S3's and S6's evidence, which S13 cites. The record, the measurements and what was declined are decisions/S13's 2026-10-26 #810 entry.
- **2026-10-26 #752, phase 2m — THE READING NOT TAKEN SURFACES AS `Ambiguity.alternative`, WITHOUT ITS LANGUAGES**, carrying out item 13 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry above and item 7's source with it: a word the credential's run took by position alone reports its name reading's source as `position`, a fourth source beside the three the item named, and a word with a declared name reading reports `declared`. The pickle guard was widened as item 13 asked, every skew the narrow guard refused still refused. The reading's languages were left out: the per-word index costs about 0.35 ms of an 18.2 ms import (decisions/A1) and describes the default vocabulary, not a pack's or a caller's lexicon, so they wait for 3.0 with `Token.reading`. The record, the measurements and what was declined are decisions/A1's 2026-10-26 #752 entry.

- **2026-10-27 #753 (Derek) (its item 10's clause making ZH's surname list and the RU and TR_AZ endings rule data for `[rules]` superseded by 2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4f; item 2's definition of strict, item 4, and 3i in item 11, superseded by 2026-10-30 #753, file-strict) — PHASE 3'S DESIGN IS AGREED: A CHOICE OF LANGUAGES SELECTS THE POOL, ONE MECHANISM FOR TURNING READINGS ON AND PREFER; NAME READINGS IN A PACK KEEP THE GATE ON; A READING INDEX CARRIES THE LANGUAGES TO RUN TIME; STRICT WAITS.** Derek accepted every item as written in chat on 2026-10-28, items 1, 2, 4, 5, 6 and 8 first, then 3, 7 and 9 to 12. One design inside item 11 stays open: 3g's connective, which accepting the work order did not decide. The design overview is docs/design/vocabulary-model-rfc.md's Phase 3 section. Decisions 15 and 20 are kept as written; decision 6's strict variant is reshaped to a property of the whole choice and deferred (items 1 and 4). Phase 3 is 3.0 work since the 2026-10-28 re-plan (item 12). The inputs are #753's comments of 2026-10-27: the inventory of 411 kept-out candidate readings with their verdicts, and the probe of foreign titles and function-word collisions. Measurements were taken 2026-10-27 on master `cfe8bf92` (Python 3.11.17) with throwaway Lexicon edits standing in for a pack, so each is evidence for the proposal and each sub-phase re-measures on its own base. Local-corpus figures are counts.
  1. **A pack is a choice of languages carried by `Locale`.** `Locale` gains `languages`, a set of BCP 47 tags; `parser_for` unions the sets of the packs it is given, so the choice is order-independent while policy patches still fold in order; strict is an argument of the whole choice (`parser_for(locales.NE, locales.HI, strict=True)`), since a strict pack beside a preferring one has no meaning, reshaping decision 6's "strict variant". The shipped language packs are `Locale` constants with an empty lexicon fragment (`locales.NE`, `locales.PT_BR`); what a fragment holds once 3.0 configures vocabulary by reading is the 2026-10-28 #754 entry on the `Lexicon` surface. The dependency this rested on, `Locale` and `Policy` keeping their 2.x shape once the facade is gone, is answered by the 2026-10-28 3.0 freeze: both keep it, with additions. Weighed against: `Parser(languages=…)` beside `Locale`, two ways to choose one thing, and a `Policy` field, `Policy` being behavior and vocabulary being `Lexicon`'s.
  2. **One mechanism.** The chosen languages select which readings the compiler compiles, the default being that compiler with every language and no choice. Turning on, prefer and strict are derived from the file each reading sits in, and nothing is written per pack beyond its language's readings: a chosen language's `default = false` readings join; prefer drops other languages' vocabulary readings of a word on which a chosen language holds one, never `mul.toml`'s (`Haddad, Abd`, suffix `Abd` today, reads given `Abd` with the en credential gone); strict drops other languages' name readings and the markers they derive. A clash a property records with a SHAPE reading is settled for the chosen language's own reading, decision 20 applied: `may_be_initial` does not hold on it, so a `pt` choice reads `ANA SILVA E SOUZA` family `SILVA E SOUZA` where the default reads middle `SILVA E` with a report. This answers decisions/3-0-reevaluations' 2026-09-13 bullet that a pack cannot remove `e`, and its 2026-08-17 bullet's "overrides rather than additions": a pack overrides by selection, never by a removal list. That removes base entries all the same, so mechanisms.md#LOCALE-PACKS-PURE-DATA's add-only contract is amended, in 3b's PR, which builds the selection (items 11 and 12), not by this decision. The shape-clash rule follows from decision 7, which decision 20 permits; it reaches every reading `may_be_initial` stands on (pt and it `e`, ca and pl `i`, the Italian one unjudged), and it forecloses an `nl` pack marking `y` (the 2026-08-18 bullet there), which waits with `fru` below. New beside decision 6: `mul.toml`'s exemption from prefer. Left open rather than built: a property a language's own reading should take only under its pack, Swedish `fru` and Icelandic `frú` as after-given salutations (rules/H8), refused in the default for the Cameroonian surname, the two readings in the inventory needing it.
  3. **`default = false` is allowed on `given` and `family` readings, and turned on they derive markers like any name reading, never settling (decision 15 unchanged).** Measured on nepal-candidates' 126,981 Devanagari and romanized pairs, comparing every word's role across the two spellings: 123,440 read alike in the default; `given` readings on `श्री`, `guru` and `गुरु`, which make each a title only inside a title phrase (rules/H9), give 123,636 (198 fixed, 2 broken, both pairs whose romanization opens with a listed title); a `family` reading on `dc` as well, making the credential ambiguous, gives 123,639, the count still leaving `Ram Bahadur DC` a suffix; dropping `dc`'s credential, which settling by a name reading would do, gives 123,658, 19 more. Settling is not taken: the 19 pairs are the accepted price of decision 15, whose `do` case it protects. `bk`, `kc`, `gc` and `bc` read as the family in every shape tried (spoken order, a family comma, `FAMILY_FIRST`, dotted and Title case), so a reading would move nothing and none is added. Recompute: the readings moved into or out of `Lexicon` fields by hand, the pairs compared by `tuple(t.role for t in parser.parse(s).tokens)`.
  4. **Strict waits, out of the first packs, until name readings say whose names they are.** A name reading sits in the file whose source counted it: 129 of the 227 name readings are in en.toml (2026-10-28, `c8357e74`), `do`'s Vietnamese Đỗ readings among them, vi.toml holding no `do`, so strict built on the files drops them under a `vi` choice, and simulated, `Minh Do` reads suffix `Do` and `Do Van Minh` family `Do Van Minh`, decision 15's harm. A census count belongs to no one language either. The candidates needing strict wait with it, `nl`'s prepositions among them, which need more than strict: turned on as possibly-given particles, `in` and `to` read `Park In Hwan` family `In Hwan` and `Nguyen To Nga` family `To Nga` (the marker acts at the head only), and check 11 accepts `may_be_given = false` in place of the Korean and Vietnamese readings. Recompute: count `given` and `family` readings per file with tools/vocab/build.py's `load`; simulate by removing `do` from `suffix_acronyms_ambiguous` and `particles_ambiguous`. Open: none of 3a–3h says whose names they are. Moving readings between files would not, a word being borne in several languages (`do` Vietnamese and Korean, `kim` Korean and Scandinavian), so the RFC points at an attribute on each name reading naming its bearers' languages, compiling to nothing until strict reads it, the first provenance anything would compute a reading from. It cannot be filled by script: 188 of the 227 count a source under a `us_` key and 21 none, per-country evidence is note prose, 40 of the notes naming nam_dict, and a box count says where a word was typed, not whose name it is (`do`'s ORCID family box is led by Brazil, 7,227 against Vietnam's 876, none of Brazil's `do` alone against Vietnam's 618; by share Vietnam leads, 1.90% to 0.81%; 2026-10-28, master `c8357e74`). The attribute names languages, so no country-to-language table is read at run time (item 6). A sub-phase of its own, 3i, after 3a, independent of the output model, moving no default parse; strict after it.
  5. **The languages reach run time as a reading index.** A second generated module, one row per reading (text, tag, kind, behavioral properties), loaded for every caller with `Lexicon.default()`, since phase 4's `Reading.languages` (4a) is filled from it on every parse, rather than on first access to `reading.languages`, which would need a `Reading`, which holds no word, to carry one as a hidden key; the compiler moves from tools/vocab/build.py into the package so build and pack share one implementation, and a test holds that compiling the index with no choice reproduces `_compiled.py`. It is the per-word index item 13 of the 2026-10-23 #752 entry says `Token.reading` needs. A choice is applied as a delta against the default's compile, so it composes with a caller's `base=` lexicon and a `Locale`'s own fragment. Measured: 2,140 readings over 1,797 words, 106 in more than one file; the index about 93 KB of source, about 0.45 ms to import with its bytecode cached against about 20 ms for a cached `import nameparser` (2026-10-28, `c8357e74`; about 11 ms without the cache, the 2026-10-27 figure, taken under `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`), once per process for every caller; the compile 9 ms, once per choice and only for a caller who makes one; loading the TOML 39 ms. Weighed against: per-language deltas compiled at build time, which do not compose (under prefer `{pt, en}` `do` keeps its particle and its credential, while Portuguese's delta drops the credential and English's the particle); every combination precompiled, combinatorial; the TOML read at run time, slower and either re-running the build's checks or skipping them.
  6. **A chosen tag selects its file and its ancestors', never its descendants, and no macrolanguage table is read.** `pt-BR` selects `pt-BR.toml` and `pt.toml`; `pt` only `pt.toml`, so a caller in Portugal does not turn on the Brazilian agnomens (#843). The two files share no word today. Malay and Indonesian, which share `puan`, `putra` and `putri`, are chosen together by a caller who has both. The TOML names `prefer` and `strict` stay refused, the message saying a strength is the caller's choice and never data.
  7. **A pack is measured three ways.** The default does not move (the committed snapshot, and the index invariant of item 5); a vocabulary pack's deviation surface, the words whose compiled readings its choice changes, is derived from the index, and tests/v2/test_locales.py's non-interference gate requires the default's reading for every corpus name without such a word, the policy and segmentation packs (JA, RU, TR_AZ, ZH) keeping their written `DEVIATES`; and `snapshot.py`, `--local` with `--base` included, takes a choice and prints what it moves by field, beside each pack's own ground truth (nepal-candidates' script pairs for `ne`, the CLDR agreement per pack, PSC's nationality slice). This answers decisions/3-0-reevaluations' 2026-09-13 bullet that the harness cannot see a pack.
  8. **Third-party language files are deferred past phase 3.** A file joining the pool at run time would skip the checks that run across files (check 6's exclusions, check 11's particle statement), the ones that refuse a third-party `nl` `in` written with neither a `given` reading nor `may_be_given = false`. `Locale(lexicon=…)`, or its successor, stays the way to add vocabulary, that successor being configuration by reading (the 2026-10-28 #754 entry on the `Lexicon` surface).
  9. **Every exclusion a pack turns on becomes a `default = false` reading,** the shape cs `st` already has, so that once the packs land an `excluded` key means wrong everywhere: the eight whose reason opens "pack-shaped" in 3c (cs `ml`, hr, sk and sl `st` and `ml`, fr `père`), and the rest in the PR of the pack that turns each on (`pan`, `roz`, `nic`, `shri`, `sig`, `jaun`, `bt`, `bay`, `sb`); `shri` stays excluded, no pack turning it on (the 2026-10-30 phase 3e entry). Recompute: `grep -h 'pack-shaped' nameparser/vocab/*.toml | wc -l`.
  10. **What stays out of phase 3.** ZH's surname list and the RU and TR_AZ endings are rule data for phase 4's `[rules]` (its 4f), and a Python pack gains `languages` only where a measurement shows what it moves; the candidates that wait on a position rule (Turkish `Bey` and `Hanım`, Hungarian `úr`, Thai `นาย`, French `M.`) are phase 4's; same-language clashes (`kumari`, `sayyid`) take phase 2j's route in the default, since no pack settles one.
  11. **The work order,** shape first and one PR each, measured on its own base: 3a the index and shared compiler with name readings allowed `default = false` (neutral); 3b the choice, tag selection, turning on, prefer, the shape clash, the derived deviation surface, `snapshot.py`'s choice and the amended contract (neutral); 3c the pack-shaped exclusions converted (neutral); then the packs, `ne` (3d), `hi` and `bn`'s Latin forms (3e), `cs`, `sk`, `sl` and `hr` (3f), `es` and `pt` with a connective that counts only inside a title run (3g), and `ga`/`gd` and `pt-BR` (3h); 3i, the bearers' languages of each name reading (item 4), after 3a, ahead of strict. 3g's connective is a rule with its own design, open, not decided with the work order: with `profesora` and `ministro` added as titles and no rule, `Profesora de Historia Ana López` reads family `de Historia Ana López` (the rules/P1 fold) and `Ministro da Fazenda Carlos Souza` given `da`, while `Doctora de la Vega` must keep family `de la Vega`, the two separated by whether a name stands behind the phrase; it must reach only readings a pack turns on.
  12. **Phase 3 is 3.0 work, opt-in, the default's parses unmoved, with phase 4's shape questions settled first** (the 2026-10-28 re-plan: 2.8 is the last 2.x feature release, and 3.0 ships phases 3 and 4 with the facade's removal). The output model decides what a pack can express (a patronymic field, a second surname, #326's credentials apart from generations, #704's `middle`). The output model was agreed on 2026-10-28 (the 2026-10-28 #754 entry). 3a and 3c are independent of it, and so are 3b's selection semantics, its API's dependency answered (item 1); 3f and 3h need 4b's `generation` view, 3g needs 4d's `surname2` and 4g's compound given name, and 4f, which compiles `[rules]` into a `Locale`'s policy patch, lands with or after 3b; `ne` needs none. One standing text is amended with 3b, mechanisms.md#LOCALE-PACKS-PURE-DATA's contract that a pack is add-only and declares its deviations in its docstring (a pack adds, and selects among the default's readings by language, a vocabulary pack's deviations derived); decisions/3-0-reevaluations' Standing line that removing base vocabulary through a pack is part of 3.0's pack design is carried out.
- **2026-10-28 #754 (Derek) — PHASE 4'S OUTPUT MODEL IS AGREED: THE SEVEN ROLES STAY AND EVERY NEW FIELD IS A VIEW, MOST READ FROM `Token.reading`; A PATRONYMIC NAMES ITS WORDS WITHOUT EMPTYING `family`; THE RENDER DEFAULTS FOLLOW THE ORDER A NAME WAS READ IN.** (its item 4's honorifics in `credentials`, and the third view it left open, superseded by decisions/R8's 2026-11-03 #326 entry) Derek agreed every item in chat on 2026-10-28, items 6 and 4 and the `surnames` line of item 5 first, then the rest. The design overview is docs/design/vocabulary-model-rfc.md's Phase 4 section. Decision 8 (a patronymic field in 3.0) is kept and its stated value revised (item 3); decisions 9 and 14 are kept as written. The core API is frozen for 3.0 except for additions (decisions/3-0-reevaluations, 2026-10-28 #925, THE CORE API), and each item says whether it is one. The inputs are #754 and its comment of 2026-10-23, #326, #704 and #694. Measurements were taken 2026-10-28 on master `c8357e74` (Python 3.11.17) over the fielded local corpora (`orcid-researchers`, `psc-individuals`, `patentsview-inventors`), the committed fields files and the committed corpus's distinct entries (`snapshot.corpus_entries()`, each parsed under its declared order), with throwaway scripts that are not kept, so each figure is evidence for the decision and each sub-phase re-measures on its own base. Local-corpus figures are counts. Recompute the box counts by reading each source's records with `local_corpora.read_records`, counting its boxes by word count and word ending per country or nationality, and the role the parse gives the counted word with `parse()`; the ending tests are the RFC's Phase 4 table's, and other tests give counts a few percent apart.
  1. **`Role` keeps its seven members, and every field asked for is a view.** A role partitions the tokens, and each field asked for (a second surname, a patronymic, generation and credentials, a second given name) subdivides a role that exists, so each is computed from the tokens, a property and a render key, except the second given name, which is the `middle` that exists (item 7), as `family_base` and `given_names` are (rules/R1, rules/R2). `as_dict()`, `comparison_key()` and the seven role properties are unchanged, and every view is an addition. Weighed against: a `Role` member for any of them, a break of the frozen `ParsedName` (`as_dict()` gains a key, `comparison_key()` a component, so a stored key stops matching, and the role its words leave returns less text than in 2.x), with no 2.x feature release left to warn in.
  2. **`Token.reading`, the reading each token took**, a `Reading` (decisions/A1's type), `None` for a token `replace()` builds, with `ADDED_IN` metadata so a 2.x pickle loads, and left out of comparison so `==` keeps its 2.x meaning. `Reading` gains `languages`, the files holding the reading, filled from phase 3's reading index (3a) and empty where no file holds it; `ReadingKind` gains `PATRONYMIC`. The views read it. It carries out the 2026-10-23 #752 entry's item 13 and the 2026-10-26 phase 2m entry's deferral of the languages.
  3. **A `patronymic` view, filled by a word or a rule that says patronymic, with `family` and `middle` unchanged.** The default fills it from the vocabulary's `patronymic` particles (`bin`, `binti`, `ibn`, `bint`, `bar`, `bat` and the native-script forms) with the name they introduce (`Mahathir bin Mohamad` → family `bin Mohamad`, as today, and patronymic `bin Mohamad`); RU's and TR_AZ's rules fill it where a caller turns them on (`Ivan Ivanovich Ivanov` under RU → middle `Ivanovich` and patronymic `Ivanovich`); an Icelandic ending rule fills it under an `is` pack only, since the default cannot tell `-dóttir` and `-son` from Swedish surnames (ORCID's Swedish records end their family box in `-son` or `-dóttir` in 2,184 of 20,000). A matronymic, written the same way, fills it too. SUPERSEDES: the RFC's earlier statement of decision 8's value, that `family` can be empty for a name with a patronymic. Measured: filers put an Icelandic patronymic in the family box (ORCID 1,647 of 2,583 Icelandic records, PatentsView 535 of 722, PSC 101 of 125), and CLDR's `is` file's 3 Icelandic samples put it in `surname` or `surname-core` in all 9 of their renderings (its one foreign sample puts a `-dóttir` word in `surname2`), while Wikidata's `patronymic-is` and `patronymic-my` strata record 108 names with a patronymic and no family name, of which the parse puts the patronymic in `family` 107 times. An East Slavic patronymic stands in the given part: CLDR's 2 `ru` samples put it in `given2` in all 6 renderings; PatentsView's Russian given boxes end in one 8,423 times of 20,000, and the parse reads such a word as the middle 10,902 times of 10,962; 90 of the 112 Russian nationals in PSC who filled the middle-name box wrote one there. A `family` that keeps it and a `patronymic` that names it agree with both kinds of source. Weighed against: emptying `family` where the patronymic is recognized, which moves the field the fielded sources fill and changes `family` for every Icelandic and nasab name, a behavior change of a frozen property; and a neutral parent-names name for the view, which buys nothing the view can detect, since a matronymic and a patronymic are written alike.
  4. **`generation` and `credentials` are views that partition `suffix` (#326)**, not roles, `generation` the tokens read as a generation or an agnomen, `credentials` the rest, the post-nominals that are neither (`Esq.`, the honorifics after a name) falling in `credentials`, CLDR's catch-all, with `Token.reading.kind` telling them apart for a caller who needs it. Measured: of the 1,054 committed corpus entries with a suffix, 33 hold a generation (or agnomen) and a listed credential together, 77 a generation beside any other suffix word, and 64 only listed post-nominals of other kinds (each suffix word classified by the RFC's Phase 4 recipe, joined tokens merged); of the 615 CLDR renderings with either field, the 75 whose suffix holds exactly CLDR's words split into CLDR's `generation` and `credentials` by the vocabulary's reading kind in all 75. Weighed against: two new `Role` members (item 1), and a third view for the honorifics, left open since CLDR has none.
  5. **a `surname2` view, the family's second unit where rules/O6 read the family as two**, marked by a new stable tag where O6 takes its word, empty otherwise; `family` keeps both units as O6 makes it, and a family comma alone marks nothing (`Lloyd Webber, Andrew`). Measured: a two-word family box in ORCID's records from Spain 48.0%, Mexico 64.7%, Colombia 72.8% and Chile 50.5%, against 3.9% from the United States. Left open: a view for the first unit and its name, `surname` standing close to the `surnames` view that exists. Declined: redefining `surnames` as the two units, which would repeat `family`, the family holding both where O6 reads two, and lose what `surnames` does today, joining `middle` and `family` so that a double surname the default splits (`Gabriel García Márquez` → middle `García`) reads whole, the view rules/P2 names for that reading.
  6. **The render defaults follow the order the name was read in, a behavior change of the output rather than a break of the API.** `ParsedName` gains `order` (an addition, `ADDED_IN`, a 2.x pickle rendering as today, left out of comparison, which lets two equal names render differently, a cost accepted 2026-10-28: a 2.x pickle of `김민준` renders `'민준 김'` and a fresh parse `'김민준'`, and the two compare equal); the default `str()` and `render()` spec put the family first where `order` does, and join two words that stand one directly after the other, in the same order, in the original and the rendering with the kind of separator that stood between them there, normalized, decided 2026-10-28 (nothing; one ASCII space for any whitespace, the ideographic space U+3000 included (`山田　太郎` → `'山田 太郎'`, where today gives `'太郎 山田'`); an interpunct or a nakaguro the parse read as a separator alone, a spaced interpunct being a name word, rules/T3; one comma the parse reads as `, ` if ASCII, `، ` if Arabic and alone if full-width, since a full-width comma carries its own width in CJK text, a reason for the comma the rendering keeps and not for the whitespace it normalizes (the Japanese comma U+3001 is no comma to the parse, a parser question outside this design), so `John Smith ,Jr.` renders `'John Smith, Jr.'`; the written whitespace staying in `ParsedName.original` and the token spans), the spec's decoration between the two words' fields standing instead (the nickname's quotes, the `) ` after a maiden name), and any other pair or separator (a delimiter, a consumed marker word, two or more commas, a hyphen, a slash, a zero-width or directional mark) taking the spec's own joiner; `initials()` gives nothing for a word in a script with no initials. A name with a family comma or written given-first keeps today's word order and its separators by the same rule, a suffix comma alone leaving a family-first name family-first (`威廉·莎士比亚` keeps its dot; 20 of the 36 names whose interpunct `str()` drops are read given-first). A family comma is never kept, the rendering reversing its pair (`Smith, John` → `'John Smith'`), and one directly between the family and its suffix is, decided 2026-10-28 (`John Smith, Jr.` → `'John Smith, Jr.'`, today `'John Smith Jr.'`; 214 of the 1,003 committed corpus entries whose suffix follows the family write one there). Accepted with it: `Smith, John, Jr.`, whose suffix the rendering moves away from the given name before it, keeps `'John Smith Jr.'`, so two writings of one name render differently. Measured over the committed corpus's distinct entries, the counts reading the ASCII comma (the RFC gives the full-width and Arabic ones: 36 of the 728 hold a family comma the parse reads and keep today's word order): 728 without a comma read family-first, `str()` renders all 728 differently from their text (`김민준` → `'민준 김'`), 150 of them with the family's last word touching the given name's first, and it drops the U+00B7 interpunct from 36 of the 41 holding one. This carries out decisions/3-0-reevaluations' render-default clean slate. Decided with it on 2026-10-28 (the RFC's Phase 4 Render defaults): `render(spec: str | None = None)`, `None` the order-aware default `str()` passes and a caller's spec rendering exactly as written, so `render("{given} {family}")` keeps its 2.x meaning and a caller passing 2.x's default string keeps given-first output. Decided too: a family-first layout with the title first, the suffix last and the name roles in `order`'s sequence, each with its decoration, a family-comma name keeping a given-first `order`. The signature is a change, not an addition as the freeze defines one (no working call changes meaning, `render(None)` raising today, but the default's introspection and a subclass override typed `spec: str` do), Derek taking it as worth that and waiving its 2.x warning (decisions/3-0-reevaluations, THE RENDER SIGNATURE). The title stays first though 50 of CLDR's 70 surnameFirst renderings with a title put it after the surname, because the default renders only an order the parse reads back, and under a family-first order the parse reads a bare title only at the front (`Wang Dr Xiaoming` → given `Dr`, `Wang Xiaoming Senator` → middle `Senator`); reading a title where CLDR writes it is a parser question, #938, and the default may follow it (#938 declined 2026-10-30, real family-first names writing the title in front: decisions/H5's 2026-10-30 #938 entry). Apart from that signature, no signature changes, but `str()`'s output changes for every name read family-first, every name whose words are written with nothing, an interpunct or a nakaguro between two words the rendering keeps side by side, and every name with one comma directly between its family and its suffix, and no 2.x release can warn about a `str()` result, so it is taken as an improvement for 3.0's release notes, not a break needing a warning.
  7. **`middle` keeps its meaning (#704)**, every word of the given part after the given name, the given name one unit unless a rule joins words into it, as rules/P5 already does (`Abdul Aziz Tariq` → given `Abdul Aziz`); a compound given name is a `[rules]` rule a caller or a pack turns on, CLDR's `given2` is `middle`, and `Kaur` stays in `middle`, as under FAMILY_FIRST does an English name after a Chinese one (`Lim Mei Ling Jessica` → middle `Ling Jessica`), an accepted cost. Measured: in PSC, whose form has a middle-name box, 2,499 of 1,040,708 forename boxes hold two or more words, and of 6,101 Spanish nationals 25 do while 2,621 fill the middle box; ORCID's and PatentsView's given box holds middles too, so it cannot separate the two. The `Maria do Carmo` shape, a particle inside a given box of three or more words with a family box filled, reads the given box's words into the family in 1,812 of 1,923 ORCID records and 1,165 of 1,219 PatentsView records from Brazil, Portugal, Spain, Mexico, Argentina, Colombia, Chile and Italy, which is that rule's case. Weighed against: #704's option (b), one given unit by default, which changes `middle` for every English name of three words.
  8. **A language file's `[rules]` compiles into its shipped `Locale`'s policy patch**, each rule kind a `Policy` field (an addition), so RU and TR_AZ are rebuilt from their files and `Locale` gains no field for rules. With it, **phase 3's open API dependency is answered**: its design (the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, item 1) carries a choice of languages on `Locale.languages`, assuming `Locale` and `Policy` keep their 2.x shape after the facade's removal, and the 2026-10-28 freeze keeps them so. The rest of that design, strict as an argument of the whole choice included, stays phase 3's to decide; its RFC section records the dependency as answered.
  9. **The work order**, one PR each, measured on its own base, the snapshot recording each new view where it is non-empty: 4a `Token.reading` and `ReadingKind.PATRONYMIC` (neutral; `languages` after 3a); 4b `generation` and `credentials` (neutral); 4c `patronymic` (neutral); 4d `surname2` (neutral); 4e the render defaults and `order`; 4f `[rules]` into the policy patch, with or after 3b; 4g the compound given name, after 4f; 4h the shape inventory per order (#469). Interleaved with phase 3 (its sub-phases the 2026-10-27 #753 entry's item 11): 4a beside 3a; 4b to 4d before the packs that need them, `cs` (3f) and `pt-BR` (3h) on 4b, `es` and `pt` (3g) on 4d and 4g; an `is` pack on 3b, 4c and 4f; 3i, the bearers' languages, on 3a alone.
  10. **What stays out of the output model.** Gender (decision 9) waits, `Token.reading` able to carry it later as one more addition. Names of given names only are a rule's question, not a field's: ORCID's empty family box holds 68,006 of 1,831,533 records, 15.9% to 18.7% of those from India, Pakistan, Bangladesh and Indonesia, and the parse fills `family` in 51,367 of the 51,415 with two or more given-box words. #694's two-word surname after a given name (`Tariq Abdul Aziz`) is a parse rule for the default, outside phase 4. Phase 1's four reserved names said to be phase 4's (`separators`, `bare_only`, `leading_only`, `trailing_only`) have no sub-phase yet.
- **2026-10-28 #754, render patterns — A `Locale` MAY CARRY CLDR-SHAPED RENDER PATTERNS (A GIVEN-FIRST AND A SURNAME-FIRST DISPLAY PATTERN AND A SORTING PATTERN), A SHAPE AGREED NOW FOR A FEATURE THAT COMES LATER AS AN ADDITION.** The RFC's Phase 4 section, "Render patterns a pack may carry", holds the shape: the pack field, a table of Phase 4's views against CLDR's person-name fields, and the gap it shows, CLDR's `surname-prefix` and `surname-core` belonging to the first surname where `family_particles` and `family_base` read the whole family, which the per-surname units close. CLDR is a starting point, not the authority: its `pt` sorting pattern files under the first surname and leaves `surname2` out, which is not ABNT's last-surname entry (decisions/R2). Proposed there, not decided: a casefolded key method beside `comparison_key()`, collation left to the caller; and, open for a decision, whether a pattern is reached through a convention recorded on the name or through the parser.
- **2026-10-28 #754, the `Lexicon` surface (Derek) — A CALLER CONFIGURES VOCABULARY BY READING, AND THE COMPILED SETS BECOME INTERNAL.** Derek decided in chat on 2026-10-28, choosing it over keeping a public field per compiled set and over keeping 2.0.0's eleven while hiding the eleven added since. A caller states what a word may be in the language files' terms (a `given` reading on `guru`, no credential reading on `dc`) and `Lexicon` derives the sets and gates as the build does. The reasons: the set mechanics are hard to keep straight even for the maintainer, a reading is what a caller can know about a word, and it is the form a pack contribution takes. Today the same configuration needs rules/H9's set algebra, `Lexicon.default().remove(titles={'guru'}).add(titles_ambiguous={'guru'})` (`Guru Prasad Sharma` → given `Guru`) and `remove(suffix_acronyms={'dc'})` (`Ram Bahadur DC` → family `DC`), while `add(suffix_acronyms_ambiguous={'dc'})` leaves suffix `DC` (2026-10-28, run on this branch's base). A caller's own removal may settle a clash: decision 15 binds the default and the packs, not a caller's data. `dataclasses.fields(Lexicon)` gives 22 public fields on 2026-10-28, eleven since 2.0.0's eleven; the RFC's Phase 4 "Left open" holds the weighing. A break of the frozen `Lexicon`, recorded in decisions/3-0-reevaluations. Open: the method names, and whether a caller's own language file can join the pool (phase 3's item 8 defers third-party files). The 2.x warning the freeze asks for was waived on 2026-10-29, 2.8 being the last 2.x feature release (decisions/3-0-reevaluations, THE LEXICON WARNING).

- **2026-10-29 #753, phase 3a — THE READING INDEX AND THE SHARED COMPILER LAND, AND A NAME READING MAY BE WRITTEN `default = false`; NO PARSE MOVES.** Carries out items 3 and 5 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, and 3a of its item 11.
  - **What landed.** tools/vocab/build.py writes a second generated module, nameparser/config/_index.py, beside _compiled.py: for each word, one row per reading in every file, on by default or not, `(tag, kind, properties)`, the properties those the compiler reads (each category's behavioral ones, and `same_as` on a `given` reading); counts, notes, exclusions and the stated `may_be_given` stay out, being checked at build time and compiled to nothing. The compiler moved into nameparser/_reading_index.py (`compile_readings`, with `CATEGORY`, `BEHAVIORAL` and `GATED_WORDS`), which the build loads by path as it loads _lexicon.py; the build keeps the checks and compiles _compiled.py from the index rows, so the module the parser reads is the index compiled. tests/v2/test_reading_index.py holds the no-choice invariant: `compile_readings(index())`, rendered as the build renders it, is _compiled.py byte for byte. The run-time reader is `readings_of(text)`, every `IndexReading` (text, tag, kind, props) of a word, for `Reading.languages`, which 4a (#942) adds but leaves unfilled and a follow-up after 3a and 4a both merge fills; `IndexReading.kind` is the vocabulary's kind (`credential`, `preposition`), and mapping it to a `ReadingKind` is that follow-up's.
  - **A name reading written `default = false` derives no marker in the default** (item 3, the half before a pack turns it on): the word's other readings compile as they would without it. So it counts for none of the checks guarding the default's compile: an agnomen on by default needs a name reading on by default (check 11, else it reads ungated), and a bound given name that is also a particle, which needs a `given` reading on by default (else Lexicon raises at import), is refused by check 11's particle half below before check 9 reads it. A name reading off beside a suffix word kind no gate reads is still refused, gating nothing when on either.
  - **Left open: a particle whose word's only `given` readings are off is refused.** Check 11 asks such a particle to state `may_be_given = false` exactly where its word has no `given` reading. The default compiles it never-given, a pack turning the given reading on compiles it possibly given, and which of the two the stated property describes is a choice no 3.0 pack needs yet (`nl`'s prepositions wait with strict). The build refuses the shape, naming both ways out, rather than pick a side; the first pack that needs it decides, with its measurement.
  - **The index is written grouped by word, and an `IndexReading` is built only for a word looked up.** On a flat draft of this change, one row per line, grouping the rows at run time took about 0.56 ms; building every row's object takes about 1.4 ms (`index()`, which only a compile over the whole pool calls); each against the import's own cost, so the build writes one line per word and the lookup table is a single `dict()` call. A change to a word's readings is still a one-line diff.
  - **The index loads on the first `readings_of` call, not inside `Lexicon.default()` (decided 2026-10-29, Derek).** SUPERSEDES: item 5's "loaded for every caller with `Lexicon.default()`". Proposed by 3a and chosen over loading it with the default lexicon. What it buys depends on the state of `Reading.languages`. Today, and until the follow-up that fills it after 3a and 4a (#942) both merge, no parse reads the index, so no caller pays the import, parsing or not, where loading it with the default lexicon would cost every parsing caller the import for nothing. Once the follow-up fills `Reading.languages`, the first parse will load it, so every parsing caller will pay it once, as item 5 measured, and only a caller who imports nameparser without parsing is spared. Wherever the follow-up calls `readings_of`, the import moves with the call. (Corrected 2026-10-29: the follow-up calls `languages_of`, which loads the index through the same lookup table, and only a parse that reads a word its own `Lexicon` lists loads it, so `John Smith` does not; "every parsing caller will pay it" was the forecast. The 2026-10-29 #754, 4a's follow-up entry's item 4 has the measurement.)
  - **Measured** 2026-10-29 on `9210f221` plus this change, Python 3.11.17, medians of fresh processes with bytecode cached as an installed wheel has it: importing the index takes 0.56 to 0.68 ms across three runs on a shared machine, about 3% of `import nameparser`'s 18 to 24 ms in the same runs, above the 0.45 ms item 5 measured on a flat draft of 2,140 rows; the first lookup after it, 0.07 ms; compiling the whole index, 2.1 ms; uncached, the import takes about 13 to 18 ms (13 after `import nameparser` in this session, 17 to 19 in review's runs, a figure no wheel install pays). The index holds 2,157 readings over 1,798 words, 106 of them in more than one file, and 3 readings written `default = false`; it is about 95 KB of source. Recompute: `uv run python tools/vocab/build.py`, then with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` unset, time `import nameparser.config._index` after `import nameparser` in fresh processes (the first run writes the bytecode), and count with `from nameparser.config._index import READINGS`.
  - Not touched: `Lexicon` and every other pickled type gain no field, so no pickle layout moves, and 3a forces no public name; the configure-by-reading methods stay open.
- **2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4a — `Token.reading` IS READ AT ASSEMBLE, FROM EACH TOKEN'S FINAL ROLE AND THE TAGS THE DECIDING STAGES LEFT, ITS SUFFIX AND NAME SOURCES BY THE PREDICATES THAT NAME `Ambiguity.alternative`.** It carries out item 2 of the 2026-10-28 #754 entry above, all but `Reading.languages`, which a follow-up adds and fills from 3a's reading index (`_reading_index.readings_of`, the 2026-10-29 #753 entry above), adding the field with its values because an empty one would say that no file holds the reading.
  1. **Where it is filled.** A word's reading is decided in two halves today: its tags say which readings of the word the vocabulary or a shape offers and how its part stands (classify's vocabulary and shape tags, the `joined` mark group and post_rules write, post_rules' unjoined marks, which make `van` in `van Gogh` a name word where `Vincent van Gogh` keeps it a particle), and the stage that gives the word its role says which one was taken. Both halves are final only at assemble, since post_rules still moves words after assign (P1's fold, P6's attachment, the patronymic rotations, `middle_as_family`'s fold), so a reading written at an earlier site would need re-writing at every later move, the stale-mark class `_types._remarked` exists to recompute. Assemble reads the suffix and name readings through `_vocab.suffix_kind` and `_vocab.name_source`, the predicates `suffix_reading` and `name_reading` use for the reading NOT taken, so the two are named by one predicate there. The bound given name's `vocabulary` and the title, connective, particle and initial readings are assemble's own tests, kept out of the shared predicates so that no alternative moves (the first draft put the bound given name in `name_source` and moved `Smith, John Abu`'s from `declared`, which review caught; `_EXPECTED_ALTERNATIVES` holds it now), the initial by classify's `initial` tag where `name_reading` names one by its shape (a one-letter `é` in `John Smith é` reads `name`, the tag never written on it). Neutral, measured 2026-10-29: the committed snapshot is byte-identical, every recorded alternative included.
  2. **What each role reads as.** A title: `title` from the vocabulary where a title tag stands, by its shape where rules/H2's period-abbreviation shape claimed it (`Insp.`, `Esq. Smith`), and by position where a title phrase took a word nothing lists (`Parliament` in `Member of Parliament`); its connective `conjunction`. A suffix: `suffix_kind`'s kind and source, save a word no suffix tag marks and the vocabulary does not list, which stands there by position: a word past a second comma (`Foo` in `John Smith, Jr., Foo`), or a continuation of the entry before it (`Jones` in `John Smith PhD Jones` and in `John Smith III Jones` reads `credential`, `position`), save a credential split across a space, whose second word takes its first word's reading whole (`Ph.` `D.`); a connective reads `conjunction` from the vocabulary, as in a title or a name part, whether it joins two entries (`and` in `John Smith, PhD and MD`) or trails them with nothing to join (`John Smith, Jr. and`), as `John Smith and` does on the name side. A name-role word: `initial` by its shape where classify tagged it one, `conjunction` or `particle` from the vocabulary where it joins, and otherwise `name`, `declared` where a derived marker says so, `vocabulary` for a word the vocabulary lists as a bound given name, whether rules/P5 joined it (`Abdul` in `Abdul Aziz Tariq`) or not (`Abdul` as the middle name in `John Abdul Smith`), save one that is also a particle (`abu`), which is P5's given name only where P5 joins it, in the given part and opening its segment past the titles (`Abu Bakar Smith`, `Smith, Abu Bakar`, `Dr. Abu Bakar Smith`), and otherwise a `particle` where it joins, the chain having taken it (`John Abu Smith`, `Smith, John Abu`, and the given part of `Salim Ali abu Bakar Hassan` under FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST, where P5 joins nothing past the family it opens with), no tag telling the two joins apart, and for a family word the segmentation split off an unspaced name by `Lexicon.surnames` (`김` in `김민준`, where a spaced `김 민준` reads `position`, the order placing it), and `position` for the rest; a particle or connective with nothing to join reads as the name word it stands as (`van` in `van Gogh`, `name`, `declared`), and so does a particle in a nickname, which has no family to join (`"Van" Smith`).
  3. **Not filled**, `None`: a token `ParsedName.replace()` builds, a token `Parser.revise()` harvests, whose role the caller forced, and a token loaded from a pickle written before 3.0 (`ADDED_IN = "3.0.0"`; `tests/v2/data/parsedname_v28.pickle`, made by the released 2.8.0 wheel, loads and equals a fresh parse). `capitalized()` carries each reading through.
  4. **Cost**, measured 2026-10-29 with `tools/perf/call_count.py`: the reference row moved from 305 to 306 frames on Python 3.11 and from 286 to 287 on 3.15, one frame for its suffix `III`. Every `Reading` a token can take is built once at import (280 of them, ten kinds by seven roles by four sources) and shared, where a construction per token would cost two frames a word; an untagged word, the commonest, is read in C-level tests with no frame. `_CALL_BASELINE` (307 and 288) is not moved.
  5. **Left open.** A listed suffix word that the compiled sets do not split by kind reads `post-nominal`, so `Jr`, `Sr` and `Esq.` read alike: the TOML gives `jr` a `generation` reading, but `SUFFIX_WORDS` holds both kinds and classify tags them alike, so only a vocabulary lookup by kind can tell them apart: 3a's reading index, a new `Lexicon` field being set aside (the 2026-10-28 `Lexicon` surface entry makes the compiled sets internal, and `Lexicon`'s own pickle guard refuses an added field). It is the predicate's own answer, which `Ambiguity.alternative` has given since 2.7 (the snapshot's one post-nominal alternative is `Rinpoche`'s), and a kind left unset would have meant a parsed token with no reading or a kind for "not yet known", so the reading taken agrees with it until 4b, whose `generation` view needs the split, reads a listed word's kind from 3a's index; `tests/v2/pipeline/test_assemble.py::test_a_suffix_word_reads_as_its_vocabulary_kind` holds `Jr` and `Sr` as strict expected failures until then. `ReadingKind.PATRONYMIC` exists and no reading takes it until 4c, whose rules write it.
