This amends the 2026-10-28 #754 entry's item 9: 4b needs 3a for that kind.
- **2026-10-29 #754, 4a's follow-up (item 1's "names the default's files whatever `Lexicon` the parse used", and that a reading written `default = false` names none, superseded by 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-d; item 4's lookup memos, `_languages` and its twin `_part_languages`, by 2026-11-05 #1085, which makes them one; the memo over `Reading` and `_forms` stand) — `Reading.languages` IS FILLED AT ASSEMBLE FROM 3a's READING INDEX, FOR THE READING A TOKEN TOOK AND FOR A REPORT'S ALTERNATIVE; NO PARSE MOVES.** Completes 4a (the 2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4a entry) with the field item 2 of the 2026-10-28 #754 entry names. Until it, no parse read the index (the 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3a entry's load bullet); since it, the first parse that reads a word from the vocabulary of its own `Lexicon` loads it (item 4).
  SUPERSEDES: decisions/A1's 2026-10-26 #752 (phase 2m) entry's decline of the reading's `languages`, for 3.0; its reason, that the index describes the default and not the `Lexicon` a parse used, is answered by item 1's semantics rather than removed.
  1. **What it holds.** The BCP 47 tags of the shipped files holding the reading taken, on by default, each once, sorted (a word joined from parts and an alternative over several words sorting the union), read through `_reading_index.languages_of(text, kinds)`, which walks the index rows without building an `IndexReading`. A reading matches the rows of every vocabulary kind of its category: a `title`, `particle` or `conjunction` reading its category's rows; a `name` reading the rows listing the word as a name of the field it lands in, `family` for a family or maiden word and `given` for a given or middle one, with the `bound_given` rows in either (a nickname takes any name rows), and a middle word `middle_as_family` folds into the family by the middle it stands as, the policy choosing the reported field and not the files (`John Ma Smith` under it names `en fil`, as without it), so `Ma` read as a family name (`Long Ma`) names `en` and not `fil`, which lists it only as a given name, and a declared name read into a field no file lists it in names none (`van` as a family name, which `nl` lists as given); and every suffix kind the word's suffix rows, a word having one suffix kind (the build's check 4), so `Jr`, which reads `post-nominal` until 4b, names `en`, the file of its `generation` reading. Only a `vocabulary` or `declared` reading is looked up; a shape, position, an initial or a patronymic names no file. A reading written `default = false` names none (`St` in a caller's `suffix_words`, held off by `cs.toml`), and neither does a word a caller's own `Lexicon` adds that no shipped file holds in that category (`Zzq` as a title), while one a file does hold names it (`Dean` added to `titles` names `en`), nor the Korean surnames that divide unspaced hangul, which `config/surnames.py` keeps outside the per-word files (`config/_ko_surnames.py` since 2026-11-04 #1077): `김` in `김민준` reads `name`, `vocabulary`, with no files. It names the default's files whatever `Lexicon` the parse used, the semantics decisions/A1's 2026-10-26 decline weighed against it: a file holds the reading or not, and which lexicon read it is the caller's own knowledge. Under a choice of languages (3b) a "live" reading becomes one the choice makes, and `languages_of` filtering on `on_by_default` is the place 3b changes.
  2. **How a word is looked up.** By the vocabulary's own form of the word (`_normalize`), then with every period removed, as a credential is matched (`M.A` in `John Smith (M.A)`) and as a word listed whole without them is (`Lt.Gen.` as `ltgen`); a credential split across a space whole, its first word joined to the second (`Ph.` `D.` as `phd`), the second taking the first's reading, past a nickname written between them too (`John Smith Ph. "Bob" D.`), where the second word read `credential`, `position` under 4a alone and now takes the first's `credential`, `vocabulary`: the one `Token.reading` this change moves, no role, tag or alternative moving with it; and a word joining listed words with punctuation by its parts, in order, a suffix's parts divided by any mark but the period first (`M.D./Ph.D.`, `MD;`), then any word's by every mark (`Lt.Gov.`), never at a combining mark, which `\W` would count (`प्रोफे.डॉ.` names `hi` and `mr` through `डॉ`), and only where the parse's own `Lexicon` does not list the word whole (in any form, its periods kept or removed): a caller's own `zzq-dr` title or `mac-van` particle was read whole, so it names none of `dr`'s or `van`'s files. An alternative names the files for any word its report names, each read in the field the alternative names. MEASURED 2026-10-29 on `bd02d68f` plus this change over the snapshot's 6,360 distinct names, the empty one among them (20,804 tokens): 3,935 readings are `vocabulary` or `declared` and 3,750 of them name a file; of the 185 that name none, 157 are Korean surnames split off by the list and 28 are declared names read into a field no file lists them in (`van` 17 as a family name, `le` 3, `san`, `la` and `fils` 2 each, `fils` once more and `von` once). Matching name rows by category rather than by field, as this entry's first draft did, 3,778 named a file, all of the 157 aside. By the normalized and period-free forms alone, without the split and the parts, the first draft counted 265 naming none: the 157, 42 words joining listed words, and 66 words of credentials split across a space, 40 by `split:credential` (`Ph.` `D.`) and 26 by `split:member` (`Pharm D`). Recompute: parse every `name` of `tools/differential/snapshot.jsonl` and count the tokens whose `reading.source` is `vocabulary` or `declared` by whether `reading.languages` is empty; for the second figure, look each up with `languages_of` by its normalized and period-free forms only, and sort the misses by the split tags on the word and the word before it.
  3. **The field.** `Reading.languages: tuple[str, ...] = ()`, `compare=False`, so two readings compare as in 2.x and every recorded alternative is untouched, and `metadata={ADDED_IN: "3.0.0"}`, `Reading` having shipped in 2.7: a `Reading` pickled by 2.7 or 2.8 loads with `()` (`tests/v2/data/parsedname_v28_alternative.pickle`, made by the released 2.8.0 wheel), and a renamed field or a shipped one missing beside it is still refused (`_REFUSED_SKEWS`). The constructor takes any iterable of str and refuses, with `TypeError`, a bare str, a mapping, a value that is not iterable and a non-str tag. The repr names the files where there are any (`Reading('particle' as 'family', 'vocabulary', in es fr it nl pt)`). The snapshot does not record readings, so nothing re-records; it is byte-identical.
  4. **Cost**, measured 2026-10-29, Python 3.11.17. Two memos keep the lookups off the frame count: `_languages`, an `lru_cache(maxsize=4096)` keyed on the word as written, the kind and the field (with `_part_languages`, its twin for the parts, asked only where `_languages` finds nothing, and `_forms`, a word's two normalized forms), and an `lru_cache(maxsize=1024)` over `Reading` for the readings that name files, each a pure function of the shipped files, bounded because the caller's text keys them, and C-level on a hit, so a word read before costs no frame. The one frame left is `_listed_whole`, the lexicon check on a word read by its parts, which looks the word up in the parse's own `Lexicon` and so cannot be memoized on the text: `Lt.Gov. John Smith` and `John Smith, M.D./Ph.D.` count one frame more per parse than on `dce56273`, where the parts were read inside the memo with no lexicon check, while a word no part of which a file holds (`van` as a family name, `김`) pays none. `tools/perf/call_count.py`'s reference row moved from 311.00 on `bd02d68f` to 311.48, all of it the first reference parse of the 50 (335 frames against 311: the index import and the four first lookups; 331 and 311.40 before the review fixes); after it each parse counts 311.0 on both trees. `_CALL_BASELINE` (307) is not moved and the serial benchmark passes. Which parse loads the index is decided by the parse's own `Lexicon`, not the files: any `vocabulary` or `declared` reading looks its word up, so `김민준` and a caller's own title (`Zzq John Smith`) load it though no file holds either, while `John Smith` does not. In fresh processes with bytecode cached, as an installed wheel has it, the first parse to read a listed word (`Dr. Juan de la Vega III`, after a `John Smith`) takes 1.20 to 1.22 ms against 0.22 ms on `bd02d68f`, the medians of 21 processes in each of three runs: about 1.0 ms once per process, the index import 3a measured at 0.56 to 0.68 ms plus the first lookups and readings. The steady parse, over 4,000 names half of them listed-word names, takes 78.6 to 79.0 µs against 78.3 to 79.4 µs on `bd02d68f`, within the runs' noise. A rerun after the review fixes, on a quieter machine, measured the cached first parse at 0.78 ms (median of 15, twice). Without bytecode (`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and an empty `PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX`, as in a container that never writes it) the same first parse takes 16.4 to 16.5 ms, the index compiled from source each process. Recompute: time the two parses in fresh processes and a `timeit` over the 4,000 names in each tree, with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` unset and the index's bytecode written, and the first parse again with it set and an empty cache prefix.
  5. **Not done here: a listed suffix word's kind.** The index lookup this change makes does not settle 4b's `Jr` and `Sr` (`tests/v2/pipeline/test_assemble.py`'s strict expected failures stand): the kind a token takes is named by `_vocab.suffix_kind`, which also names `Ambiguity.alternative`, so reading the kind from the index at assemble alone would split the two, and reading it inside `suffix_kind` moves the alternatives and puts the index on the decision path. 4b decides it.
- **2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b — A CHOICE OF LANGUAGES SELECTS THE POOL: `Locale.languages`, TURNING ON, PREFER AND THE SHAPE CLASH OF `may_be_initial`, WITH A DERIVED DEVIATION SURFACE; A CALLER CHOOSING NO LANGUAGE PARSES AS BEFORE.** Carries out items 1, 2, 6 and 7 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, and 3b of its item 11. No pack ships (3d is the first).
  - **What landed.** `Locale` gains `languages`, a frozenset of BCP 47 tags, empty by default, marked `ADDED_IN = "3.0.0"` so a `Locale` pickled before it loads choosing nothing; it is validated by shape (`[A-Za-z]{2,8}(-[A-Za-z0-9]{1,8})*`, a bare string, bytes or a mapping refused as `Policy`'s fields refuse them), compared and hashed, and shown in the bounded repr (`Locale('pt': languages pt)`). `parser_for` unions the packs' `languages` into one choice and applies it to the base lexicon before any fragment is unioned, so the order of the packs does not change it (item 1). The selection is nameparser/_reading_index.py's: `selected(tags)` gives the files a choice selects, `choose(readings, chosen)` the readings it compiles, `compile_choice(chosen)` their compile, kept per choice, and `changed_words(chosen)` the words whose compiled readings it changes; `parser_for` applies `compile_choice`'s difference from the empty choice's compile as a delta (`_parser._choice_delta`), entries leaving and joining each `Lexicon` field and case masks leaving and set, so it composes with a caller's own `base=` vocabulary (item 5's composition): an entry the choice does not change stays, a caller's own included, and an entry the choice drops leaves whoever put it there, so `parser_for(EN, base=Parser(lexicon=Lexicon.empty().add(particles={'do', 'van'})))` keeps only `van`; that removal is decided with the chained choices (the 2026-10-29 chained-choices entry below). A word the choice drops from a base set leaves that set's markers too, a caller's own mark included: found by review, `Lexicon.default().add(particles_ambiguous={'vd'})` under `en`, whose credential drops nl's preposition `vd`, raised the orphan-marker `ValueError`. An error the `Lexicon` still raises while the choice is applied is wrapped as rules/D2 wraps a patch's, naming the languages chosen, the choice being every pack's at once (`while applying the languages chosen, en, pt: …`). nameparser/_lexicon.py's `_COMPILED_FIELDS` maps each `Lexicon` field to the constant it is read from, and tests/v2/test_parser.py holds that read from the empty choice's compile they are `Lexicon.default()`'s fields. `snapshot.py --languages TAGS` reads the committed corpora under the choice against the committed snapshot, which holds the default's readings while it is current (so a branch that moves the default re-records first), or `--local SOURCE --base PATH` a local one against the base's default readings, and records nothing; `--base` may name the tree being read under a choice, whose moves are the tree's own.
  - **The semantics as built,** each a rule of `choose`: a chosen file's `default = false` readings join, `default` dropped; on a word where a chosen file holds a reading of any category but a name, the other files' readings of those categories leave, save `mul.toml`'s, every language's, and `und.toml`'s, whose language is not known to be another's (und holds only name readings today, so exempting it moves nothing); a chosen file's conjunction reading drops `may_be_initial` (decision 20); name readings never leave and never settle (decision 15). With nothing chosen, `choose` keeps exactly the readings on by default, so the empty choice compiles `_compiled.py` (tests/v2/test_reading_index.py), and `parser_for` with no language skips the choice altogether, building the parser without loading the index (a subprocess test; a parse loads it since the 4a follow-up, for `Reading.languages`, choice or none).
  - **Tag selection (item 6), with three details the item left open:** `Locale` stores each tag in BCP 47's canonical case (`PT` as `pt`, `pt-br` as `pt-BR`, `zh-hant-tw` as `zh-Hant-TW`), so equal choices compare and hash equal, and the index's tags are matched without regard to case, so `pt-br` selects `pt-BR.toml` rather than silently only `pt.toml`; a tag whose own file does not exist selects its ancestors' (`pt-PT` selects `pt`); and a tag selecting no file at all, and `mul` and `und`, which are no language to choose, raise `ValueError` at `parser_for`, wrapped with the pack's code (rules/D2) and naming the tags that have a file.
  - **Turning a reading on also prefers it.** The rules of `choose` apply to every chosen-file reading, on by default or not, as item 2 has them, and its examples did not show what that does to a word whose turned-on reading is the chosen language's only one: `de` turns on its generation `i` (`default = false` in de.toml), which makes `i` a word `de` holds a vocabulary reading of, so `ca`'s and `pl`'s conjunction readings leave and `mul`'s generation stays. Under `de`, 32 committed Catalan names read the `i` as a name word (`Claudi Ametlla i Coll` → family `Coll`, middle `Ametlla i`) and five after a family comma read it as a suffix (`Carod i Rovira, Josep` → suffix `i`, and in a maiden clause `Doe, Jane nee Puig i Soler` → middle `nee Puig Soler`, suffix `i`). Recorded, not changed: it is the rule as agreed; a German caller with Catalan names chooses `ca` too, and the data question, whether de's `i` should be a reading at all, belongs to the pack that would ship it.
  - **Measured** 2026-10-29 on `bd02d68f` plus this change, Python 3.11.17, each tag chosen alone against the default's readings with `snapshot.py --languages TAG`. Committed corpora, entries moved (names held only by `corpus_cldr.jsonl` among them): `pt` 38 (1), `pt-BR` 38 (1), `en` 54 (7), `de` 49 (0), `it` 14 (0), `cs` 11 (2), `es` 3 (1), `ar` 2 (1), `fr` 0. `changed_words` gives the words behind them: `pt` `do`, `e`, `sr`; `pt-BR` the same, pt-BR.toml holding no word that moves; `en` `abd`, `do`, `ra`, `sr`, `vd`; `de` `i`, `ra`; `it` `e`; `cs` `st`; `es` `h`, `sr`; `ar` `abd`; `fr` none. Every move read is one of these words: `pt` reads the one-case `e` as the connective in every comma shape and under all three orders (`ANA SILVA E SOUZA` → family `SILVA E SOUZA`; `SILVA E SOUZA ANA` under `FAMILY_FIRST` → family `SILVA E SOUZA`, given `ANA`), exactly as a lowercase `e` in mixed case reads in the default (`Ana Silva e Souza`), its report gone where no role moves (`SILVA E SOUZA, ANA`), and a mixed-case capital `E` stays the initial the writing makes it; `pt` and `es` read `Sr.` as the salutation, so `J. Smith Sr.` → title `Sr.`; `cs` turns on its generation `st` and the Saint readings leave, so `Jiří Dienstbier st.` → suffix `st.`, reported suffix-or-name since en's `given` reading still gates it, and `Jill St. John` → family `John`; `ar` reads `Haddad, Abd` given `Abd`. Local corpora, entries moved against the same tree's default readings (`snapshot.py --local SOURCE --base . --languages TAG`): `tse-donors` under `pt` 4,824 of 854,531, 4,751 of them names holding a bare `e` (`ABILIO ROMUALDO LIMA E SOUZA` → family `LIMA E SOUZA`), 72 more a `do` (a trailing `DO` read as a name word, `ALESSANDRA ALVES DOS SANTOS DO` → family `DO`) and one an `SR`, counted by the bare word each name holds; `tse-donors` under `en` 14,559, all but one holding `do`, which loses its particle (`ABADIA DO CARMO CARVALHO` → family `CARVALHO`); `ctgov-officials` under `en` 327 of 536,981, 223 of them names holding `Abd`, whose bound given reading leaves for the credential (`A M Abd El Baky` → family `M`), 43 `Sr`, 39 `do`, 18 `vd`, one `Ra`, and three a `do` inside a hyphenated word, which changes only its case repair (`Giao DO-PHAM` → `Do-Pham`); `psc-individuals` under `en` 209 (its 1,040,708 filed names), 131 holding `do` (`Alexandre Martelliti Do Amaral` → family `Amaral`), 38 `Abd`, 35 `Sr`, three `Ra`, de's academic title leaving it (`Mr Ra Jojo Cole` → given `Ra`), one `vd` and one a hyphenated `do` (`Costa-Do-Amaral`, case repair only); `ukec-individuals` under `en` and `nepal-candidates` under `ne` none, ne.toml holding no reading the default leaves off or that clashes. So choosing a language alone costs the readings of every word it holds a vocabulary reading of: `en`, which holds the credentials, is the costliest choice, and a pack is a language whose clashes are few. Each local count is by the first of the changed words a moved name holds as a bare word, a word inside a hyphenated one counted apart; every moved name holds one, so the local corpora show no break of non-interference either. Recompute: the commands above, with `uv run python -c "from nameparser import _reading_index as ri; print(sorted(ri.changed_words(ri.selected(['pt']))))"` for each tag's words.
  - **The no-choice path does not move.** tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes unchanged; `parser_for` with packs choosing no language returns the base lexicon's value; and `tools/differential/measure.py --base` a checkout of `bd02d68f` reports no move in any committed or local corpus check.
  - **The deviation surface is derived (item 7).** tests/v2/test_locales.py's `test_non_interference_each_choice` runs every corpus name under `pt`, `en`, `cs`, `de` and `pt` with `en`, requiring the default's reading for every name holding no word of `changed_words` (matched over-wide: each piece whole, periods removed, cut at non-word characters, and a non-ASCII word anywhere), about 1 s a choice on one worker; its negative control removes `e` from `pt`'s surface and fails on the names it moves. tests/v2/test_reading_index.py holds, for every file tag chosen alone, that a choice changes only words its selected files hold. decisions/deviates-registry records the registry half: a registered vocabulary pack's `DEVIATES` is the derived surface from 3d.
  - (Superseded by 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface, effective with 4i-c on 2026-11-02: a chained choice now unions with the base's.) **A choice chained through `base=` is applied on top of the base's, not unioned with it**, decided in the next entry (option (a) of three weighed there). A `Parser` records no choice, so `parser_for(EN, base=parser_for(PT))` applies `en`'s delta to a lexicon `pt` already changed: `Maria do Carmo` reads family `Carmo` and `John Smith DO` family `DO`, where `parser_for(PT, EN)` keeps both readings (`do Carmo`, `Smith`). Item 1's order-independence holds within one call.
  - (Superseded by 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-d: the field names the files whose reading is on in the parse's own `Lexicon`, which records its choice since 4i-c.) **`Reading.languages` does not follow a choice: it names the files holding the reading on by default, whatever choice built the parser.** SUPERSEDES: the 4a follow-up entry's expectation, in its item 1, that 3b changes `languages_of`'s filter on `on_by_default`. Measured on this branch merged with that follow-up: under `pt`, the one-case `E` of `ANA SILVA E SOUZA` reads `conjunction` with languages `('it', 'pt')`, though prefer dropped `it`'s reading from the pool; under `cs`, the `st.` of `Jiří Dienstbier st.` reads `generation` with languages `()`, though `cs.toml` holds the reading the choice turned on. Kept, for two reasons: the follow-up decided the field names the default's files whatever `Lexicon` the parse used, a caller's own `St` in `suffix_words` naming none for the same `cs` reading; and a parse cannot know its choice, a `Parser` recording none (the chained-choices entry below), so a field that followed the choice needs the `Parser.languages` left open there. The cost is that a reading a choice turns on names no file and a language a choice dropped is still named; tests/v2/test_parser.py's `test_reading_languages_name_the_default_files_under_a_choice` pins both, so a change with `Parser.languages` moves them deliberately.
  - Not touched: `Lexicon` and `Parser` gain no field; `_locale.py` imports nothing new beyond `_types.ADDED_IN` and `_policy`'s string-and-mapping guard; `_parser.py` imports `_reading_index` at call time, and tests/v2/test_layering.py's table says so. 4f's `[rules]` compiled into a `Locale`'s policy patch needs no change to this shape: a pack's policy patch and its `languages` are separate fields. Strict (item 4) is not built.
- **2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b, chained choices (Derek) — SUPERSEDED by 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface (in effect since 4i-c, 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-c) — ONLY ONE CALL'S CHOICE COUNTS: A CHOICE CHAINED THROUGH `base=` APPLIES ON TOP OF THE BASE'S, AND A CHOICE DROPS THE CALLER'S OWN BASE ENTRIES THAT IT REMOVES.** Derek decided in chat on 2026-10-29, choosing option (a) of the three the phase 3b entry above weighed. `parser_for` unions the `languages` of the packs passed to it and applies that one choice as a delta against the default's compile; a `Parser` records no choice, so a base built by an earlier `parser_for` carries its choice only as the lexicon it changed. Two consequences, both pinned by tests in tests/v2/test_parser.py so that a change to them is deliberate:
  1. **Chained choices compose one after the other.** `parser_for(EN, base=parser_for(PT))` reads `Maria do Carmo` family `Carmo` and `John Smith DO` family `DO`: `en` drops `pt`'s particle and `pt` had dropped `en`'s credential. The way to choose both is one call, `parser_for(PT, EN)`, which keeps both readings (`do Carmo`, suffix `DO`).
  2. **A choice drops what it removes from the base, whoever put it there.** `parser_for(EN, base=Parser(lexicon=Lexicon.empty().add(particles={'do', 'van'})))` keeps only `van`: the delta removes `do` from `particles`, the caller's listing included, and leaves every entry it does not touch.
  Weighed against: (b) a `Parser.languages` field recording the choice, so a base's choice unions with the packs' (a new public name, safe for pickles, `Parser`'s guard honoring `ADDED_IN`), deferred rather than declined; (c) a `Lexicon` field recording it, which every older `Lexicon`, `Parser` and pack pickle would refuse, `Lexicon`'s guard not honoring `ADDED_IN` (an objection removed on 2026-10-30, when that guard learned the marker, #960, decisions/A1). Open: (b), with the configure-by-reading method names (the 2026-10-28 #754 `Lexicon` surface entry), and with it whether `Reading.languages` follows the choice (the phase 3b entry above).
- **2026-10-29 #753, phase 3c (its fr `père` superseded by decisions/generational-words' 2026-11-03 #684 entry) — THE EIGHT PACK-SHAPED EXCLUSIONS ARE READINGS OFF BY DEFAULT: cs `ml`, hr, sk and sl `ml` and `st`, fr `père`; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** Carries out the 3c half of item 9 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, and 3c of its item 11.
  - **The split.** Every exclusion whose reason opened "pack-shaped" refused a `generation` reading that is right in its language and wrong as a default only because another language written in Latin script collides with it: `ml.` against the initials `M. L.`, `st.` against Saint, `père` outside French. Each is now a `generation` reading written `default = false` in its own file, with the issue list it had and #753, an `expands` naming the full word, and a `note` giving the collision, the shape cs `st` has had since 2026-10-25 (phase 2h). The other exclusions a pack turns on (`pan`, `roz`, `nic`, `shri`, `sig`, `jaun`, `bt`, `bay`, `sb`) stay exclusions until the PR of the pack that turns each on; every other exclusion is wrong everywhere and stays. Recompute: `grep -l 'pack-shaped' nameparser/vocab/*.toml` lists no file, which tests/v2/test_locales.py's `test_no_exclusion_is_pack_shaped_any_more` holds.
  - **The default does not move.** The build reports `_compiled.py` current, only `_index.py` gaining the eight rows (11 readings written `default = false`, from 3), and tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes with no re-record.
  - **What choosing each language now does,** measured 2026-10-29 on `b27dadd5` plus this change with `snapshot.py --languages TAG`, entries moved (names held only by `corpus_cldr.jsonl` among them): `cs`, `sk`, `sl` and `hr` 14 each (4), where `cs` moved 11 (2) and the other three none before; `fr` 0, no committed name holding `père`. The three new entries under each read `ml.` as the suffix: the rules corpus's `Jiří Dienstbier ml.`, and two CLDR renderings with `ml.` before a comma of credentials (`g. Janez Peter Novak ml., univ. dipl. inž.`). The other eleven are `st`, and each language's turning on its reading also prefers it (the phase 3b entry's turning-on bullet), so en's and fr's title and particle readings of `st` leave: `St Clair` reads given `St`, family `Clair`, where the default reads title `St`. That cost reached `cs` with 3b; it reaches `sk`, `sl` and `hr` here, and settling it, whether a Saint reading should outlast a choice that holds `st` as a generation, is 3f's, which turns those packs on. tests/v2/test_locales.py's `test_a_chosen_language_reads_its_off_generation_and_the_default_does_not` parses every generation reading off by default of more than one letter (`Anna Novak ml.`, with and without the period) under its language and the default; a one-letter word (es `h`, de `i`) is left out, the initial veto taking it written with its period (`Anna Novak h.` reads family `h.` under `es`, while a bare `h` reads suffix `h`).
  - **Open for 3f: a turned-on `ml` reads a lone comma part as the suffix, silently.** `ml` has no name reading, so under a choice it is an ungated suffix word: `Novak, ML` reads suffix `ML` with nothing reported, where the default reads given `ML`, and `Novak ml., Anna` family `Novak`, suffix `ml.`; the gated `st` reports `SUFFIX_OR_NAME`, and fr `Jean Père` reports `GIVEN_OR_FAMILY` (suffix `Père`). The initial pairs `Anna M. L. Novak` and `Anna ML Novak` do not move. Pinned in tests/v2/test_locales.py; whether `ml` takes a name reading or a gate is 3f's. So is the one-letter gap: es `h` and de `i` turned on stay inert written with a period (`Juan Pérez H.` keeps family `H.` under `es`, `Wilhelm Karl I.` family `I.` under `de`), a strict expected failure there.
  - Not touched: no `Lexicon` or `Parser` field, no code. The vocabulary README's example of an exclusion is now `duke`, wrong everywhere, where it was `père`.
- **2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4b — `generation` AND `credentials` LAND AS VIEWS PARTITIONING `suffix`, AND A LISTED SUFFIX WORD'S KIND IS READ FROM THE READING INDEX; NO FIELD OF ANY PARSE MOVES.** Carries out item 4 of the 2026-10-28 #754 output-model entry and closes 4a's `Jr`/`Sr` seam: `_vocab.suffix_kind` asks the reading index for the kind of a word tagged `vocab:suffix-word`, so `Token.reading` and `Ambiguity.alternative` read `Jr` as a generation through the one predicate. What the implementation decided, the measurement and its recipe are in decisions/R8's 2026-10-29 entry, and the rule is rules/R8.
- **2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4c — `patronymic` LANDS AS A VIEW, READ FROM A PATRONYMIC PARTICLE'S RUN AND FROM THE RU AND TR_AZ RULES WHEREVER THEY ARE ON; NO FIELD OF ANY PARSE MOVES.** Carries out item 3 of the 2026-10-28 #754 output-model entry: the particle and the words after it in its field read `patronymic`, a particle's kind read from the reading index at classify as 4b reads a suffix word's, and `ReadingKind.PATRONYMIC`, which 4a added and no reading took (the 2026-10-29 sub-phase 4a entry, item 5), is now taken. What the implementation decided, the measurement and its recipe are in decisions/R9's 2026-10-29 entry, and the rule is rules/R9.
- **2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4d — `surname2` LANDS AS A VIEW, THE FAMILY'S SECOND UNIT WHERE rules/O6 READ TWO; O6'S MARK IS PUBLISHED ON THE READING, NOT AS A STABLE TAG; NO FIELD OF ANY PARSE MOVES.** Carries out item 5 of the 2026-10-28 #754 output-model entry, and amends it and the RFC's freeze table on one point: the "new stable tag" is a private tag assemble drops, the mark reaching the published token as `Reading.second_surname`, a field added with `ADDED_IN` and not compared, since a published tag would make an O6 parse unequal to its 2.x pickle. The last of the views lane's four (4a to 4d). What the implementation decided, the measurement and its recipe are in decisions/R10's 2026-10-29 entry, and the rule is rules/R10.
- **2026-10-29 #754, sub-phase 4f — A LANGUAGE FILE'S `[rules]` TABLE COMPILES INTO ITS SHIPPED PACK'S POLICY PATCH; RU AND TR_AZ ARE BUILD OUTPUT, VALUE-EQUAL TO THE PACKS WRITTEN BY HAND; NO PARSE MOVES.** Carries out item 8 of the 2026-10-28 #754 output-model entry. SUPERSEDES: in the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, item 10's clause "ZH's surname list and the RU and TR_AZ endings are rule data for phase 4's `[rules]` (its 4f)": a `[rules]` table only switches rules on, the endings stay with their rule kinds in the core and ZH's list stays in its pack (items 5 and 7; accepted 2026-10-29, Derek). What the item left to this sub-phase, and how each was decided:
  1. **The schema: each key the `Policy` field of its name, with a value of that field's shape.** `patronymic_rules` takes a non-empty array of `PatronymicRule` values (`"east-slavic"`, `"turkic"`), each once; `name_order` one order constant's name in the file's case (`"family-first"`, `"family-first-given-last"`); `two_family_names` `true`. The keys are `tools/vocab/build.py`'s `RULE_KEYS`, a key's shape `flag`, `choice` or `set`, so 4g's compound given name is a new `Policy` field (with `ADDED_IN`) and one row there and in `_locale._RULE_VALUES`, the converter; tests/v2/test_vocab.py's `test_the_rule_tables_agree_with_policy` holds the two tables to each other, to `PolicyPatch`'s fields and to `Policy()`'s defaults, and runs every accepted name through the converter into a `Policy`. Naming the keys after the fields keeps item 8's "a rule a pack turns on is a `Policy` field" literal: the table is `Policy(...)` written as data. Weighed against: a key per rule (`east_slavic_patronymic = true`), which names a rule twice, once in the file and once in the enum.
  2. **A rule is written only to turn it on.** `name_order = "given-first"` and `two_family_names = false` are refused: a pack's scalar overrides the caller's own in `apply_patch`, so either would switch a caller's rule off whenever the pack is applied. Every other refusal is fail-loud as the item asks, build check 12, each with its row in tests/v2/test_vocab.py's mutation table (sixteen rows): a table in a file no pack reads, a `rules` that is not a table, an empty table or array, an unknown key, an unknown rule or order name, a value of the wrong type (a string for the array, an array or an integer for the scalars, a number in the array), a name listed twice, a default, and a file a pack reads with no table.
  3. **The compiled patch lives in `nameparser/config/_rules.py`**, a third generated module beside `_compiled.py` and `_index.py`, one line per file (its tag and its keys as sorted pairs, a set as a sorted tuple, tuples throughout so the module holds no mutable state); `build.py --check` exits 1 when it is stale, as for the other two, and tests/v2/test_vocab.py's `test_the_committed_rules_are_what_the_toml_compiles_to` fails naming the refresh command. A pack reads it through `nameparser._locale.rules_patch(tag)`, which imports the module at call time and builds the `PolicyPatch`, so the pack modules keep importing `_locale`, `_lexicon` and `_policy` only, and `_locale`'s row in tests/v2/test_layering.py gains `nameparser.config._rules`, the one data module it reads. Weighed against: the build writing `PolicyPatch(...)` source, which would make a config data module import `_policy`; and a converter in each pack, a second copy of the schema per pack.
  4. **Which file a pack reads is a roster in the build, `PACK_RULES`** (`ru` → `RU`, `tr` → `TR_AZ`), since the build never imports the package; tests/v2/test_vocab.py holds every listed pack to `rules_patch` of its tag. **TR_AZ reads `tr.toml`.** There is no `az.toml`, the pack's one rule is the same rule for both languages, and a pack reads one file; an `az.toml` written later holds Azerbaijani vocabulary and needs no table of its own while the rules are the same. The table does not choose the file's vocabulary: RU's and TR_AZ's `languages` stay empty, the rest of item 10 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry (a pack gains `languages` only where a measurement shows what it moves).
  5. **What stays with the rule kind, not the file: the endings**, reversing in part item 10 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, which made them rule data for `[rules]` (the SUPERSEDES line above). The table carries the switch, not the data: `-ovich` and `oğlu` are the implementation of a rule kind, in `_pipeline/_vocab.py`'s patterns where rules/O1 and rules/O2 read them, and a rule kind is core (mechanisms.md#LOCALE-PACKS-PURE-DATA). A file turns a rule on; it never supplies what the rule matches.
  6. **`DEVIATES` stays hand-written, and is held to the rules the patch turns on.** 3b's derived surface is a vocabulary choice's (`changed_words`), and a rule's surface is the rule's own matcher, which a pack cannot import. tests/v2/test_locales.py's `test_a_packs_marker_regexes_are_the_rules_its_patch_turns_on` replaces the hand pairing of four regexes with a pairing derived from each pack's compiled patch, over every `PatronymicRule` member and every pack: turning `turkic` on in `ru.toml` fails it until `ru.py` declares the Turkic surface. Weighed against: moving each rule kind's surface predicate beside its enum member in `_policy.py`, which would remove the copies; it waits for a rule kind whose surface is not a copy of a pattern (Open below).
  7. **ZH and JA keep their hand-written patches**, reversing in part the same clause of item 10 for ZH's surname list, which stays a Python list in its pack rather than becoming `[rules]` data. Both turn on `segment_scripts`, which the table could express as a set of script names, but neither turn-on stands alone: ZH's segmentation reads its surname list, a Python list outside the per-word model, and JA's reads the caller's segmenter, the one code hook, so a `[rules]` table would split each pack's behavior across a file and a module without making either pure data. ZH's moves when its surname list has a home in the vocabulary; JA's stays as it is, its turn-on serving the segmenter, the one code hook a pack keeps.
  - **Measured unchanged** 2026-10-29 on `23aacddc` plus this change, Python 3.11: `locales.RU` and `locales.TR_AZ` compare equal to the `Locale`s written by hand before, their rules `PatronymicRule` members (tests/v2/test_locales.py's `test_rules_packs_equal_their_hand_written_patches`); pickles of both, and of `parser_for(RU)`, `parser_for(TR_AZ)` and `parser_for(RU, TR_AZ)`, made under the 2.8.0 wheel and under `23aacddc`, load in this tree equal to its own and parse alike, and under `PYTHONHASHSEED=0` this tree's pickles of all five are byte-identical to `23aacddc`'s; the full suite, the snapshot and the locale agreement records (`cldr_agreement_locales.json`, `wikidata_native_agreement_locales.json`) do not move. Import, `nameparser` already imported in each case (medians of 25 fresh processes, each `python -c "import time, nameparser; t = time.perf_counter(); import nameparser.locales as L; L.RU; print(time.perf_counter() - t)"`, dropping `; L.RU` for the first figure): `import nameparser.locales` 0.23 ms before and 0.24 after; the first access of `locales.RU` or `locales.TR_AZ` 0.50 ms before and 0.69 after, which the review measured as 0.27 → 0.46 ms and 0.31 → 0.45 ms on other runs, about 0.2 ms each time, the import of `config/_rules.py`; packs still load lazily. A cold `import nameparser` is about 47 ms, unchanged. The default parse runs no new code, `rules_patch` being called once per pack at its import.
  - **Open:** #754, deriving `DEVIATES` from the rules (item 6), with 4g; #753, ZH's turn-on (item 7).
- **2026-10-30 #753, phase 3d — THE FIRST LANGUAGE PACK, `locales.NE`: A CHOICE OF `ne` TURNS ON `given` READINGS OF `श्री`, `guru` AND `गुरु`, SO EACH IS A TITLE ONLY INSIDE A TITLE PHRASE AND ELSEWHERE A NAME WORD PLACED BY POSITION; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** Carries out 3d of item 11 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, the route the RFC's "Name readings in a pack" measured: a name reading turned on derives the marker any name reading derives (`TITLES_AMBIGUOUS`, rules/H9), and decision 15 holds inside the pack.
  1. **The readings are in `ne.toml`, each `given = { default = false, note = … }`.** A chosen tag selects its own file and its ancestors' (the phase 3b entry), and `ne` has none, so `languages=("ne",)` reaches only `ne.toml`; a `given` reading in `hi.toml` or `bn.toml` would be turned on by a Hindi or Bengali choice instead, which is 3e's to weigh. The title readings stay where they were (`श्री` in `hi.toml` and `mr.toml`, `guru` in `bn.toml`, `hi.toml` and `pa.toml`, `गुरु` in `hi.toml`), and a name reading never prefers (`choose`'s "Name readings never leave and never settle anything"), so the choice keeps them and the pair derives the marker. `hi.toml` is untouched: its `श्री` note, which says a `given` reading there would make the title one only inside a title phrase, stays true of the default. A count on each reading is in its `note`, there being no count key for a corpus of pairs.
  2. **`locales.NE` is nothing but its choice** (`Locale(code="ne", lexicon=Lexicon.empty(), languages=frozenset({"ne"}))`), registered in `locales/__init__.py`, and it **declares no `DEVIATES`**: mechanisms.md#LOCALE-PACKS-PURE-DATA says a vocabulary pack's deviations are derived, and a pack module may not import `_reading_index` (layering). tests/v2/test_locales.py's `_deviates` ors the surface derived from `changed_words` (`guru`, `गुरु`, `श्री` for NE, pinned by `test_ne_pack_contents`, through the 3b gate's `_surface_deviates`) into the predicate of EVERY pack with `languages`, so a hand `DEVIATES` needs to declare only what a fragment or a patch beside a choice changes, the choice's words no longer needing a hand copy (nothing refuses one that restates them) (`test_a_pack_with_a_choice_and_a_patch_declares_only_the_patch`); the registry contract refuses a `DEVIATES` on a pack that is nothing but its choice and requires one of every other pack. `snapshot.py --languages` and `alike --languages` read the choice alone, which is the whole of NE but not of a pack with a fragment or a patch beside its choice; both say so where they are described. NE's rotators cover each word in each script, at the head, behind a listed title and after a family comma. This is the pattern 3e to 3h copy where a pack is a choice alone.
  3. **`local_corpora.py alike` takes `--languages`**, as `snapshot.py` has since 3b: the flags under the choice, against the default's flags (the tree's own, or `--base`'s), so the RFC's "Measuring a pack" pair measure is one command. A choice is read by `_parse(languages)`, which `surname` and `_read` now ask, and which joins `_ALIKE_CODE`, so the change re-keys every kept `alike` base once.
  - **The default does not move.** `build.py` reports `_compiled.py` current, only `_index.py` gaining the three rows; tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes with no re-record.
  - **What `ne` moves,** measured 2026-10-30 on `40675146` plus this change, Python 3.11. Committed corpora (`snapshot.py --languages ne`): 6 entries, 4 of them held only by `corpus_cldr.jsonl`. Those four are CLDR's `hi`, `kok` and `mr` renderings opening with the salutation (`श्री ललित कुमार शर्मा एम॰डी॰`, CLDR title `श्री`), where `श्री` leaves the title for a name word placed by position: given in that one, family in the other three (`श्री शर्मा ललित कुमार, एम॰डी॰`, a family-first rendering, → family `श्री शर्मा ललित कुमार`), one of which, an `mr` rendering whose title was `श्री. एम.`, keeps `एम.`. The choice reads Hindi, Konkani and Marathi names as Nepali, the cost the user docs state. The other two are `corpus_issues.jsonl`'s `Guru Gobind Singh` and `Guru Nanak`, read given `Guru` where the Sikh Gurus' `Guru` is the title; decisions/indic-honorifics' accepted cost (`Guru Dutt` reads title `Guru`) is lifted under the choice and paid the other way. CLDR agreement per pack (`cldr_agreement.py`, `ne` mapped to NE in `LOCALE_FILES` and the `ne-np` stratum in `NATIVE_LABELS`): `ne` 15/24 with and without the pack, Wikidata's `ne-np` 10/10 both ways, neither file holding one of the words; the record gains NE's rows, unchanged. nepal-candidates (`snapshot.py --local nepal-candidates --base <this checkout> --languages ne`): 296 of its names move, all by the word leaving the title for the given name in the default order (a lone word after it going from given to family: `गुरु चौधरी`), 209 opening `श्री`, 42 `Guru`, 41 `गुरु`, 1 `GURU`, 1 `श्री.`, and 2 the glued `सु.श्री`; under either family-first order the word is placed as the family name instead (`श्री राम बहादुर थापा` → family `श्री`, given `राम` under `FAMILY_FIRST`, given `थापा` and middle `राम बहादुर` under `FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST`). Pairs (`local_corpora.py alike nepal-candidates --languages ne`): 125,833 of 126,981 read alike under the default, 126,040 under `ne`, 211 now alike and 4 now apart. The 211 all hold `श्री` (4 `गुरु` too), the romanization writing it as a name word (Shree 191, Shri 6, Sri 2); the `Guru` and `गुरु` pairs move together and stay alike. Of the 4 apart, 3 romanize `श्री` as `Mr.` (`Mr. Bal Bahadur Singh`), which the romanization reads as the salutation and the pack does not, and 1 is the defect below. The RFC's 2026-10-27 figures (123,440 → 123,636, 198 fixed, 2 broken) were measured on `cfe8bf92` by roles per word; this measure is `alike`'s structure, on a later tree.
  - **Not a rule change.** rules/H9's When already names "a title word that is also borne as a given name", and the choice adds three words to that class without moving its limits: `His Holiness Guru Prasad` keeps title `His Holiness Guru` (a style partner), `Swami Guru Prasad` reads title `Swami`, given `Guru` (H9's Accepted, a religious title behind any title but a style), and `Guru. Prasad Sharma` keeps title `Guru.` by rules/H2's shape, while `श्री. कृष्ण श्रेष्ठ` reads given `श्री.`, H2's shape never reading a Devanagari word (its Accepted clause on abugidas). Pinned in tests/v2/test_locales.py.
  - **Open: a period-joined token whose only title chunk the choice makes possibly given reads as a name word, in both directions.** `_vocab.period_joined_vocab` makes a token a title where ANY chunk is in `titles`, and under `ne` `श्री` has moved to `titles_ambiguous`. That costs `सु.श्री.` (सुश्री, Ms., written with its periods), title under the default and given under `ne`, 2 nepal-candidates names; and it fixes a given name glued behind the word, `श्री.राम थापा`, title `श्री.राम` under the default and given `श्री.राम` under `ne`. Whether a possibly-given chunk counts there is open; a fix would move the default for every word of `TITLES_AMBIGUOUS`, and its grid holds both `सु.श्री.` and `श्री.X`.
  - **Recompute:** the three commands above from a checkout of this change; the CLDR record with `cldr_agreement.py`; the word counts with `tools/name_usage.py guru गुरु श्री`.
- **2026-10-30 #754, sub-phase 4g — THE COMPOUND GIVEN NAME IS A RULE KIND: `Policy.two_given_names`, rules/O7, OFF BY DEFAULT AND A `[rules]` KEY; NO DEFAULT PARSE MOVES.** Carries out item 7 of the 2026-10-28 #754 output-model entry and the 4g row of its item 9. The rule kind is built as the 4f entry's item 1 says a new one is: a `Policy` field with `ADDED_IN` (`3.0.0`, so a 2.8 `Policy` pickle loads with it off, tests/v2/data/policy_v28.pickle), a `flag` row in `tools/vocab/build.py`'s `RULE_KEYS` with its default `false` refused, a row in `_locale._RULE_VALUES`, and a mechanisms.md entry, DECLARED-SPAN, which it shares with rules/O6's switch, whose mirror it is. No file turns it on: whether `es` and `pt` do is 3g's. What the rule reads, its precedence against O6 and O3, and the measurements are decisions/O7's 2026-10-30 entry; the `Maria do Carmo Silva` shape the RFC's table names stays unread, the particle chain running to the end of the name, and is Open there. Item 6 of the 4f entry, deriving `DEVIATES` from the rules, stays open: this rule has no surface in the name for a pack to declare.
- **2026-10-30 #753, phase 3f (its doctorates' `default = false` superseded by the 2026-11-06 #1115, the pack titles entry, which reads them by default, so the default reads `Jan Novák, MUDr` as the pack does) — THE CZECH, SLOVAK, SLOVENIAN AND CROATIAN PACKS: `locales.CS`, `SK`, `SL` AND `HR`, ONE `Locale` PER LANGUAGE, EACH, LIKE `locales.NE`, A CHOICE OF ITS ONE TAG; `pan` AND `roz` BECOME READINGS OFF BY DEFAULT; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** Carries out 3f of item 11 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry.
  - **One pack per language,** as the RFC's API section already had it ("each language is a pack of its own"), and for three reasons measured here: the files hold different words (sl and hr only the generations, sk `ArtD.`, cs `pan` and `roz`), so a shared pack would turn on Czech `pan` and `roz` for a Slovenian caller; the choice is a set, so a caller with data in two of them passes both (`parser_for(locales.CS, locales.SK)`); and a pack is then nothing but its tag, `Locale(code="cs", lexicon=Lexicon.empty(), languages=frozenset({"cs"}))`, auditable as its file. Each module ships no `DEVIATES`, as phase 3d's contract has it for a pack that is nothing but a choice: its deviations are `changed_words` of its choice, and the contract test now also refuses a choice pack whose choice changes no word (decisions/deviates-registry's 2026-10-30 phase 3f entry).
  - **The readings, all written `default = false`, so `_compiled.py` is unchanged.** 3c's generations `ml` and `st` in all four. In cs and sk, the academic titles written before the name as `academic` readings with their case masks (`mudr`, `mddr`, `mvdr`, `judr`, `phdr`, `rndr`, `paeddr`, `pharmdr`, `thdr`, `mgr`; cs also `mga` and `bca`) and the degrees written after it as `credential` readings (`drsc`; sk also `artd`): #848's open question 1, whether `Mgr.`, `MUDr.` and `PhDr.` follow `Ing.` to the suffix after a comma, is answered for the pack by Czech usage, title, since they stand before the name. `mgr` changes no compiled set, en and fr reading it as Monsignor already; under the choice its kind is `academic`. cs's and pl's `pan` exclusions become `salutation` readings, and cs's `roz` exclusion a `birth_marker` reading, each keeping its collision in its note. So `Jan Novák, MUDr` reads title `MUDr`, given `Jan` under `locales.CS` where the default reverses it (given `MUDr`, family `Jan Novák`), `Jan Novák MUDr.` reads the title at the end (rules/H5), `Jan Novák, DrSc` suffix `DrSc`, `Jana Nováková roz. Svobodová` maiden `Svobodová`, `Janez Novak st.` under `locales.SL` suffix `st.`, reported suffix-or-name, and `capitalized()` writes `MUDr.` for `mudr.`. Every reading was swept over the four comma shapes, the three orders, dotted, bare and one-case spellings and a family comma behind a `Freiherr` head; tests/v2/test_locales.py's `test_the_west_and_south_slavic_packs_read_what_the_default_refuses` pins a row of each, default and pack side by side.
  - **Left out, with the reason:** `bc` (bakalář) is an exclusion, measured: under a Czech choice it moved 23 of ctgov-officials' 536,981 officials, one a Czech `Bc` and the rest the US nursing credential Board Certified or initials (`Lora L. Beebe, PhD, PMHNP, BC` read title `BC`; `ACNP-BC` recased `ACNP-Bc`), and `Bc.` with its period is a title at the head by its shape anyway. `ing` stays mul.toml's ambiguous credential: an `academic` reading beside its `given` reading would be a title only inside a title phrase (rules/H9), moving nothing but `Ing` in a phrase, and its dotted spelling is a title by shape. `doc` (docent) is an English nickname (US SSA 1,391 given-name births, nam_dict band 4), recorded as no reading of any file; an `academic` reading would need that `given` reading beside it, which makes it a title only inside a title phrase (rules/H9), so it would move nothing a pack wants, and the dotted `doc.` is a title by shape already. Slovak `rod.` and `rodená`, the masculine `rozený`, the Slovene and Croatian academic abbreviations (`dr. sc.`, `mag.`, `univ. dipl. inž.`) and Slovak `pán` and `pani` are unvetted, not refused.
