Two remedies were weighed and declined: moving en's and fr's Saint readings to mul.toml, which prefer never drops, would keep them under every choice but say they are every language's, and changes `Reading.languages` for the default's `st` from `('en', 'fr')` to `('mul',)`; and a rule that a generation reading never displaces a title is the prefer semantics item 2 of the 2026-10-27 entry rejected for no evidence. The way to keep them is to choose a language that holds them too: `parser_for(locales.CS, Locale(code="fr", …, languages=frozenset({"fr"})))` reads `St Clair Smith` title `St` and `Jiří Dienstbier st.` suffix `st.`, and fr's choice changes only `père` besides. (2) **The ungated `ml` stays ungated.** `Novak, ML` reads suffix `ML` with nothing reported, as the default reads `Smith, JR`: no name is `ml`, so there is no name reading to gate it, and initials are the clash, which a pack chooser accepts (ctgov's `Clements ML` reads suffix `ML`, reporting given-or-family). (3) **The inert one-letter `h` and `i` are re-deferred** to the packs that turn them on, es (3g) and de, which no sub-phase schedules; neither letter is in these four files.
  - **Measured** 2026-10-30 on `40675146` plus this change, Python 3.11.17. Committed corpora, `snapshot.py --languages TAG`, entries moved (held only by `corpus_cldr.jsonl` among them): `cs` 17 (6), `sk` 15 (4), `sl` 15 (4), `hr` 15 (4), `pl` 11 (2). Fifteen of each are `st` and `ml` moves, 3c's fourteen and rules/S11's new `[cs]` example `Jiří Dienstbier st.`, which this change adds to the rules corpus; cs and pl add `Pan Tomasz Nowak` and `Pan Nowak Tomasz Jan` (under `FAMILY_FIRST`), both now reading title `Pan`; pl's other 9 are its conjunction `i`, which choosing pl already moved. The CLDR locale view, newly recorded for the four packs in `cldr_agreement_locales.json`: whole renderings agreeing cs 14/24, sk 15/24, sl 16/24, hr 18/24, default and pack alike; the pack moves two sl renderings and no other, each `ml.` from family to suffix (`g. Janez Peter Novak ml., univ. dipl. inž.`, family `Novak`), both still disagreeing elsewhere. Local corpora against the base's default readings (`snapshot.py --local SOURCE --base <checkout of 40675146> --languages TAG`), counted by the first changed word each moved name holds: `ctgov-officials` cs 178 (st 69, mudr 44, pan 17, drsc 15 and 2 `Dr. Sc.`, phdr 12, rndr 7, pharmdr 6, ml 3, and one each of paeddr, mddr, mvdr), sk 161 (the same less pan), sl and hr 72 (st 69, ml 3); `orcid-researchers` cs 245 (st 124, pan 46, phdr 16, ml 14, mudr 11, judr 8, drsc 6, mga 5, paeddr 5, rndr 4, roz 3, and one each of thdr, pharmdr, bca), sk 191, sl and hr 138 (st 124, ml 14). Read by kind: the titles and `drsc` are fixes wherever they move a field (`Denisa Wilkes, MUDr` stops reversing, `Christian Salmon, DrSc` keeps its given name, `Prof. MUDr.` reads one title run); every `pan` move is a Chinese name read with a title (`PAN DAI` → title `PAN`), no Czech salutation among them; `roz` fixes one (`Lenka Šenkárová (roz. Veverková)` → maiden `Veverková`) and costs two given names `Roz` (`ELLA ROZ PERRY` → maiden `PERRY`); `st` is 3c's (Saint names, and `PhD St.`, a PhD student, read as the generation). The fielded scores, `local_agreement.read` over the records of the four countries: ORCID's 56,808 records from CZ, SK, SI and HR agree 52,239 under the default and 52,240 under cs and sk (`dr.sc. Sara Soldo`, HR, fixed), unchanged under sl and hr; PSC's 3,578 Czech, Slovak, Slovenian and Croatian filers agree 3,505 under every pack, cs and sk fixing `Mudr Jozef Stec` and moving `Mr Judr Branislav Fridich`, whose filer typed `Judr` in the forename box. The same run over `psc-individuals`: cs 416 (st 395, pan 16, mudr 2, and one each of judr, mvdr and roz, `Miss Naila Roz Roz` → maiden `Roz`), sk 399, sl 395 (all st; hr not run, its choice changing the same two words the same way); `tse-donors` cs 9 (st 5, roz 2, pan 2, `PAN SHISHEN` → title `PAN`); `ukec-individuals` cs 9 (st 8, and `Victor MGA Killearn`, only its forced case repair moving, `MGA` → `MgA`). Recompute: the commands above, and `uv run python -c "from nameparser import _reading_index as ri; print(sorted(ri.changed_words(ri.selected(['cs']))))"` for each tag's words.
  - **Found, not changed:** a gated generation behind a particle run after a family comma stays in the given part unreported, `Novák, Freiherr von Berg st.` reading given `von Berg st.` under these packs as the default reads `Dumas, Freiherr von Berg fils` (rules/S11); the default reading of the same `st.` is stranger, family `st. Novák`. Pinned in the 3f table. And with one word before a family comma, `Novák, st.` reads title `st.` by its period shape (rules/H2) under all four packs, reporting suffix-or-name with the generation as the alternative though the reading taken is a title, as the default reads `Dumas, fils.`; the ungated `Novák, ml.` reads suffix `ml.`. `Novák st., Jan` keeps family `Novák st.`, as the default keeps `Dumas fils, Alexandre`: the gated word before a family comma is a name word. Both pinned in the 3f table.
  - **Open:** `pan`, kept because the work order names it: the corpora show its cost (81 Chinese names across ctgov, ORCID, PSC and TSE read with the title) and no gain, the shape that excluded `bc`, and as a title on its own it also takes a lone given name after a family comma (`Smith, Pan` reads title `Pan` with no given name, a bare `Pan` title `Pan`), so whether it stays in the Czech pack is a question for the reviewer. Whether the uncontested titles (`mudr`, `judr`, `phdr`, `rndr` and the rest, no name reading and no clash found) belong in the default rather than a pack is a default change, measured on ctgov, outside phase 3's rule that nothing moves the default.
- **2026-10-30 #753, phase 3f amended — `locales.CS` AND `locales.SK` READ TWO FAMILY NAMES: cs.toml AND sk.toml TURN ON `two_family_names` (rules/O6) IN A `[rules]` TABLE, WITH NO GIVEN-NAME LIST; `SL` AND `HR` STAY CHOICES ALONE; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** Decided in chat on 2026-10-30, on the measurement below. Amends two claims of the phase 3f entry for these two packs: "a pack is then nothing but its tag", and "Each module ships no `DEVIATES`".
  - **What changes.** cs.toml and sk.toml each gain `[rules]` `two_family_names = true`, compiled into the pack's policy patch (`PACK_RULES` in tools/vocab/build.py gains `cs` and `sk`). Each pack becomes a choice with a patch beside it, as `locales.ES` is, and declares `DEVIATES` for the patch's part only. That declaration is rules/O6's, `_locale.deviates_by_two_family_names`, now one predicate for the es, cs and sk packs (decisions/deviates-registry's entry of this date). The married double surname written without a hyphen stays whole: `Alena Pešová Řehořová` gives family `Pešová Řehořová`, where the default gives middle `Pešová`.
  - **Measured** 2026-10-30 on `bfb91163` plus this change, Python 3.11.17, records agreeing in every field, from these runs, each against a checkout of `bfb91163` (`--base`): `local_agreement.py cz-candidates --base` (the default, matching the base), `cz-candidates --locale cs --base` and `cz-candidates --locale sk --base` (each pack now, its moves against the pack at `bfb91163`), `orcid-researchers --base`, `orcid-researchers --locale cs --base` and `orcid-researchers --locale sk --base` (each report's CZ and SK rows). Three figures no report prints are read with `local_agreement.read` and `FIELDED` over the records under two parsers: the ORCID per-country moves, the cz-candidates moves against the default, and that the cs and sk packs read the ORCID CZ and SK records alike.
    - cz-candidates (fetched 2026-10-30; data kv2022 2026-03-28, ps2025 2025-10-05; 170,181 records): default 165,002 (96.96%); `locales.CS` before this change 165,027 (96.97%); now 165,880 (97.47%), 1,295 records now agreeing and 442 now disagreeing against the pack before, 1,318 and 440 against the default (+878); `locales.SK` now 165,878.
    - orcid-researchers (data 2025-10-17), country CZ, 20,000 records at the corpus's cap: 18,999 (94.99%) under the default and the pack before alike, now 19,282 (96.41%), +635 and −352.
    - orcid-researchers, country SK, 12,962 records: 12,293 (94.84%), now 12,496 (96.40%), +347 and −144. On ORCID the cs and sk packs read alike: they differ on none of the CZ or SK records, measured.
    - The default's readings do not move: both corpora's `--base` checks without a locale match the base, and the committed snapshot is unchanged.
  - **The cost**, the same in all three populations: every record that now disagrees has a given box of two or more words, read as a given name and a family name (`Petr Pavel Novák` gives family `Pavel Novák`). cz-candidates: 442 of its 473 such records. ORCID CZ: of the 620 such records with a family box, 396 agree before and 220 now. ORCID SK: 166 of 240 before, 45 now. Such a name is about sixteen times as common on ORCID (4.4% of CZ records) as on the registers (0.28%); both sources' given boxes hold the middle names too, so why is not measured. A family comma keeps both readings, rules/O6 standing down there (`Novák, Petr Pavel`). The CLDR locale view, fictional renderings, moves the other way: whole renderings agreeing, cs 14/24 → 12/24 and sk 15/24 → 11/24, its native double surnames gained and its given-given-surname renderings lost, re-recorded in `cldr_agreement_locales.json`. And a birth marker the chosen language does not list, standing between two surnames, is taken into the family: `Jana Nováková rod. Svobodová` reads family `rod. Svobodová` under both packs (the default reads middle `Nováková rod.`), as does `rodená`, and cs's `roz.` under `SK`; under `CS` the listed `roz.` routes the maiden name as before, and the Czech `rozená`, listed on by default, does under both. Slovak `rod.` and `rodená` are birth markers in no file, Open below.
  - **Open: a Czech and Slovak given-name list,** which would keep a listed given name out of the family as the Spanish list does (rules/O6's veto). Its ceiling, measured with a circular list (every word of each population's own given boxes): cz-candidates 166,298 (97.72%), ORCID CZ 19,574 (97.87%), ORCID SK 12,624 (97.39%). No population-wide source is published:
    - The Ministry of the Interior published given-name and surname frequencies from the population register, the last for 2016, and withdrew them; its page says it is not authorized to publish statistics (archiv.mv.gov.cz/clanek/cetnost-jmen-a-prijmeni.aspx, read 2026-10-30).
    - ČSÚ holds no register of names (its press release of 2005-04-21, csu.gov.cz/cesky_statisticky_urad_nema_pristup_k_prijmenim_obyvatel). It publishes the top 100 given names of newborns a year, as XLSX for 2022 to 2025 (csu.gov.cz/nejoblibenejsi_detska_jmena), under CC BY 4.0 by its site terms: a different generation from the adults these packs read, and how many of the two-given-name records it covers is not measured.
    - The Spanish INE list is no stand-in: carried beside the pack, it moved 186 cz-candidates records, 134 to agreeing, through the international names it holds (`Jan`, `Marie`, `Anna`).
  - **Open: the Slovak birth markers** `rod.` and `rodená`, which no file lists, so the switch reads them into the family (above); a `birth_marker` reading in sk.toml is vocabulary work, measured on its own.
  - **Left alone:** `two_given_names` (rules/O7) stays off, not measured on these populations. `SL` and `HR` read one family name, no population of theirs measured.
  - **Two packs setting a scalar to the same value no longer warn.** `parser_for` warned whenever two packs set one scalar field, even to the same value; with `cs`, `sk` and `es` all turning `two_family_names` on, the most natural stack for these packs, `parser_for(locales.CS, locales.SK)`, would have warned, failing a caller running with warnings as errors, though nothing was overridden (found in this PR's review). It warns now only where the later value differs, AGENTS.md's "invariants guard harm, not no-ops": a pack agreeing with another changes no parse. tests/v2/test_parser.py pins both sides; docs/locales.rst's composition list says so.
- **2026-10-30 #753, phase 3h — THE `ga`, `gd` AND `pt-BR` PACKS: THE IRISH CLAN FORMS AND `sb` TURN ON; `F` AND `N` STAY OUT, MEASURED; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** Carries out 3h of item 11 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, and for `nic` and `sb` the rest of item 9.
  - **What each pack turns on.** `locales.GA` chooses `ga`, `locales.GD` `gd` and `locales.PT_BR` `pt-BR` (code `pt_br`, its choice selecting `pt` too, item 6 of the phase 3b entry), each with an empty lexicon fragment and no policy patch. ga.toml: `nic` (daughter of), its exclusion converted, with a `given` reading (US SSA 77, Wikidata 173) and a `family` one (US Census 108), so it is a possibly-given particle and a leading `Nic` stays given, reported (rules/P1); `mhic` and `uí`, the married forms of `mac` and `ó`, `may_be_given = false`; `mic`, the genitive of `mac`, with `given` (US SSA 26) and `family` readings (ORCID's family box: Romania 4, Ireland none). Each clan reading is `default = false` and `same_as` its head where it has one. gd.toml: `nic` alone. pt-BR.toml: `sb`, its exclusion converted to an `agnomen` reading `default = false`, `same_as = "sobrinho"`, with a declared `family` reading as its siblings have, and `cased = "SB"`, so the S.B. degree's capitals survive `capitalized(force=True)` under the pack, the recasing that was half of its exclusion's reason; the mask is the word's, so Sobrinho's spelling recases to it too (`José Silva Sb.` force-repairs to `SB.`). Irish `nic`, `mhic` and `mic` carry their title case as `cased` too, as `ní` and `uí` do: a particle reading without one is lowercased by case repair (rules/R4), so `MÁIRE NIC GIOLLA PHÁDRAIG` repaired to `Máire nic Giolla Phádraig` under the pack, where the default gives `Nic` (found in review). Counts as of 2026-10-30 from `tools/name_usage.py nic mic mhic uí --boxes`.
  - **`mhic` stays out of gd.toml.** Written in Scottish Gaelic it mostly links a patronymic chain, `Iain mac Dhòmhnaill mhic Sheumais`, which the default reads family `mac Dhòmhnaill mhic Sheumais` only because `mhic` is unlisted; listed, it starts a second particle run and the chain splits at it, middle `mac Dhòmhnaill`, family `mhic Sheumais`, as the default already splits `Iain mac Dhòmhnaill mac Sheumais`. The Irish married form (`Máire Mhic Gabhann`) is the word's modern written use, so ga.toml keeps it and accepts the chain's split (`Seán mac Seáin mhic Thomáis`, pinned nowhere and moving no corpus name).
  - **`F` and `N` are excluded, the pack included.** #843 left them to this pack. Turned on, both letters measured on tse-donors (2026-10-30, `snapshot.py --local tse-donors --languages pt-BR --base` against `--languages pt`, each moved name scored by its registry spelling, on a tree with `f` and `n` written as `agnomen` readings `default = false`, `same_as = "filho"` and `"neto"` with a declared `family` reading, as `sb` is, and rebuilt): of the filed/registry pairs they moved beyond what `pt` moves (242 pairs, 241 distinct filed names), each scored by whether its registry spells the agnomen and counted under the first letter its reading took as the suffix, `F` was the agnomen in 48 and a letter abbreviating a surname in 104 (`ANA MARIA F`, registry `ANA MARIA FILGUEIRAS`), `N` in 18 and 59 (`EDINEI VITAL N`, registry `EDINEI VITAL DO NASCIMENTO`), last or before another generation word alike (`ADINALDO VILHENA F JUNIOR`, registry `FERREIRA JUNIOR`). The filings are the evidence the letters were proposed from, and they abbreviate the other words too, so no writing tells the two apart even inside the language: each is an `excluded` agnomen in pt-BR.toml with these counts. `sb` moved 13 names' roles beyond `pt`'s, the registry spelling SOBRINHO in every one (the pairs reading alike, of the 6,090 whose filed name holds `f`, `n` or `sb` as a word, rose from 11 under `pt` and the default to 22, alike meaning given, middle, family and suffix each equal with an agnomen letter spelled out as its word), and one more name's `capitalized()` view alone, its mask writing a mid-name `SB` (`VIVIANE G A DA SB FERRUGINI`) where the default writes `Sb`.
  - **The default does not move.** The build reports `_compiled.py` current; the committed snapshot gained the three names rules/S11's new examples add to the rules corpus and no existing entry moved; `snapshot.py --local tse-donors --base` against master `40675146` matches the base.
  - **What each choice moves,** measured 2026-10-30 on `40675146` plus this change, Python 3.11. Committed corpora (`snapshot.py --languages TAG`): `ga` and `gd` 4 entries each, every one `mc` (`John Smith MC` family `MC`, where the default reads the Military Cross), the prefer of the phase 3b entry's turning-on bullet on the `mc` both files already held; `pt-BR` 40, `pt`'s 38 (1 held only by `corpus_cldr.jsonl`) and the two of rules/S11's new examples that read `SB` (`JOSE CARLOS SILVA SB`, `Ana SB`). CLDR's locale view (`cldr_agreement.py`, `ga`, `gd` and `pt` mapped to the packs, CLDR's `pt` being Brazilian): `ga` 14/24, `gd` 15/24, `pt` 17/24 renderings agreeing with and without the pack, recorded. Fielded registers, scored over every record holding a word the choice changes (`local_agreement.py`'s `read` under each parser): PSC, `ga` 4 fixed (`Clodagh Nic Lochlainn`, `Orlaith Mhic Mhathuna`, and `Damien Patrick Mc` by the `mc` prefer) and 1 broken (`Nicholas Nic Rawlings Limited Rawlings`, a company filed as a person), `gd` 3 and the same 1; ORCID, `ga` 24 fixed and none broken over every country (23 in Ireland and Great Britain, `Pauline Uí Dhuibhir`, `Éimí Mhic an Ridire`), `gd` 20 and none (19 in Ireland and Great Britain). `pt-BR`'s moves there are all `pt`'s: PSC 12 broken, each a trailing `Sr` read as the title Senhor (`Gary Holloway Sr` family `Sr`), ORCID 21 fixed (the one-case `e`) and 11 broken.
  - **Open for 3g:** a `pt` choice prefers `pt.toml`'s title `sr` (Senhor) over English's generation, so a trailing `Sr` reads as a title or the family name under `pt` and `pt-BR` (PSC's 12 above); whether the generation survives a Portuguese choice is 3g's, which owns `pt`'s titles. **Accepted: under the pack a written S.B. degree reads as Sobrinho's generation.** In ctgov-officials (`snapshot.py --local ctgov-officials --languages pt-BR --base`, against `--languages pt`), 17 names move beyond `pt`'s: 12 only in `capitalized(force=True)`, a mid-name `SB` keeping its capitals, and 5 where the degree after a comma or a credential now fills `generation` rather than `credentials` (`Howard S. Gordon, MD SB`, `Waleed Riad, MD, AB, SB`), one of them moving fields (`Silvio Brugger, SB`, given `SB` by default, suffix under the pack). The degree is a suffix either way; which view holds it is the cost of a choice made for Brazilian data. The recipe for the fielded figures is the scratch scorer this entry describes: `local_agreement.read` per record under `Parser()` and `parser_for(pack)`, over the records whose name holds a word of `changed_words(selected(pack.languages))`.
- **2026-10-30 #753, phase 3e (its `sushri` bullet's wait for 3i superseded by 2026-10-30 #753, file-strict, and carried out by decisions/indic-honorifics' 2026-11-03 (Derek) #345/#870 entry) — THE HINDI AND BENGALI PACKS, `locales.HI` AND `locales.BN`: THE ROMANIZED `smt`, `shrimati` AND `shriyut` (HI) AND `smt`, `shrimati` AND `adhyapak` (BN) TURN ON AS TITLES; `shri` AND `sushri` STAY OUT, MEASURED; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** Carries out 3e of item 11 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, for #345's Latin forms.
  - **What each pack turns on.** `locales.HI` chooses `hi` and `locales.BN` `bn`, each with an empty lexicon fragment and no policy patch, so each is a choice alone and declares no `DEVIATES` (the phase 3d entry's item 2). hi.toml gains `salutation` readings `default = false` for `shrimati` (f), `smt` (its `same_as`) and `shriyut` (m), each `after_given` as its Devanagari spelling is; bn.toml gains `shrimati` and `smt` the same way and `adhyapak` as an `academic` reading `default = false`. None of the words has another reading in any file, so the choice turns each on and prefers nothing: `changed_words` is `{shrimati, shriyut, smt}` for `hi` (`sushri` joining it on 2026-11-03, decisions/indic-honorifics) and `{adhyapak, shrimati, smt}` for `bn` (pinned by `test_hi_and_bn_pack_contents`). A word turned on is read wherever the title vocabulary is: at the head, behind a listed title, after a family comma, ahead of one with a name word behind it (rules/H6, `Shrimati Gandhi, Indira` → family `Gandhi`), where it ends the given part after one (rules/H8, `Sitharaman, Nirmala Smt`), written with its period at the end of the name (rules/H5, `Indira Gandhi Shrimati.` → family `Gandhi`), and as a chunk of a period-joined token (the Accepted bullet below). Written with its period, rules/H2's shape already reads it as a title by default at the head, behind a listed title, after a family comma and alone after one (`Smt. Nirmala Sitharaman`, `Dr. Smt. Rekha Sharma`, `Sitharaman, Smt. Nirmala`, `Rekha Sharma, Smt.`), so a dotted spelling moves wherever else the title vocabulary is read, which a sweep of 18 shapes (head, behind a title, mid-name, end, end before a suffix, and each comma shape with a one-word, full and empty head, a second comma and a credential beside it) finds to be: ending the name, a suffix peeled behind it or not (H5, `Rekha Sharma Smt. Jr.` → title `Smt.`, family `Sharma`); ending the given part after a family comma (H8, `Sitharaman, Nirmala Smt.`); ahead of a family comma with a name word behind it (H6, `Smt. Gandhi, Indira`); ending what follows an empty head, which is not a family comma (rules/C15), so H8 does not name it (`, Rekha Smt.` → title `Smt.`, family `Rekha`, where the default reads given `Rekha`, middle `Smt.`); any comma part after the name that holds it beside a credential or past a second comma (rules/C2, `Rekha Sharma, Jr., Smt.` → title `Smt.`, suffix `Jr.`, where the default reads suffix `Jr., Smt.`; `Rekha Sharma, PhD Smt.`; `Sharma, Rekha, Smt.`); and a period-joined chunk; bracketed at the end it counts as ending the name, its period keeping it from the nickname (`Rekha Sharma (Smt.)` → title `Smt.`, family `Sharma`, while `(Smt)` stays the nickname); mid-name it stays a name word (`Rekha Smt. Sharma`); bare at the end of the name it stays a name word, as every bare title does (`Indira Gandhi Shrimati`). Each reading's counts are on hi.toml's reading, bn.toml's pointing to them, from `tools/name_usage.py smt shrimati sushri shriyut shri adhyapak --boxes --country IN --country BD` (2026-10-30) and a pass over ORCID's whole extract by box and position.
  - **`shrimati` ships, weighed against its two name bearers.** ORCID's whole extract has 3 given boxes holding it: `SHRIMATI`|`SHETTY` and `Shrimati`|`Shetty`, likely one person, the word alone as the given name, and `Shrimati Datta`; under either pack `Shrimati Shetty` reads title `Shrimati`, family `Shetty`, and no given name, a cost. The title side is nepal-candidates' 16 romanizations, whose Devanagari is the salutation in every one (`श्रीमती` 11, `श्रीमति` 4, the spaced `श्री मती` 1), and the word's Devanagari twin, a default title. That data is Nepali, not the Hindi or Bengali data the packs are for, which no corpus here holds, so the measured split is 16 leading uses as the title against 1 person bearing it as a name (the Shetty pair; `Shrimati Datta`, a given box with no family box, reads equally as the salutation and a surname), from populations beside the pack's own. That is the reverse of `shri`'s 198 openers against 1 fix, and at this volume the title reading holds under the Standing criterion, which pools the populations in persons: 16 openings as the title against 1 name, though within ORCID alone the name (the Shetty pair) is at least as many as the title (`Shrimati Datta` at most). `srimati`, the same word in Bengali spelling, is not judged by the same counts (Open below): it was not measured as a reading.
  - **`shri` is left out: its exclusion stays in hi.toml, with the counts.** In every population that writes it, a name opens with it far more often than the salutation does. ORCID's whole extract (24,990,909 records) has 198 given boxes opening with `Shri`, 52 of them `Shri` alone and most of the rest a given name written in two words (`Shri Kant`, `Shri Ram`, `Shri Lakshmi`, `Shri Vaishnavi`), Hindi and Tamil names alike; nam_dict bands it 2 in India, and Wikidata holds 7 given. Turned on (measured on a tree whose hi.toml held it, its exclusion removed, as a `salutation` reading `default = false`, and bn.toml the same reading), it broke 20 filed names and fixed none: scored box by box, PSC 2 (`Mr Shri Ram Neupane`, its forename `Shri`), ORCID's local corpus 5 and PatentsView 4 (`Shri Ram Yadav`), and FEC 9, each `SHRI` the first name after the family comma (`BANGALEY, SHRI` → title `SHRI`, no given). It moved nepal-candidates' 10 `Shri` romanizations to the title, 4 of them the one Devanagari word `श्रीनारायण` (`Shri Narayan Paswan`), and in jrc-names fixed 1 (`Shri Dharmendra Pradhan`). No CLDR rendering writes it (CLDR's `hi_Latn` writes the salutation `Shree`). This is not a clash prefer settles, since the word has no other vocabulary reading, nor one strict would: its bearers are the pack's own language's. It is the same-language clash the RFC's phase 3 section sends down phase 2j's route, a `given` reading and the title phrase (rules/H9), as default work; Open below.
  - **`sushri` is left for strict.** ORCID's whole extract has 16 given boxes opening with it, 14 of them an Odia given name beside an Odia family name (`Sushri Sangita` Behera, `Sushri Smita` Sahu, `Sushri Soumya` Jena), the other two one person's `Sushri Das` . Turned on, it moved 2 nepal-candidates names (`Sushri Pingala Tharu`, the Devanagari `सुश्री`, a default title, so both now read alike) and 1 jrc-names name (`Sushri Umashri Bharti`, the salutation), and 1 ORCID record wrong both ways. The clash is a name of another language, which prefer cannot drop and strict, once name readings say whose names they are (3i), would. The Standing rule writes such a reading `default = false` in its file, but here a plain `hi` choice would turn it on, and the data a Hindi choice is for holds the Odia names, so the reading waits for strict, which nothing in the files can yet ask for; no reading is written, and this bullet holds its evidence until one is.
  - **`shriyut` ships on no evidence either way.** No source `tools/name_usage.py` reads counts a bearer, ORCID's whole extract holds no box with it, and no committed or local corpus writes it; its Devanagari twin `श्रीयुत` is a default title. The pack turns on the twin's romanization because nothing measured contradicts it; nothing measured supports it either, so its leading use is unmeasured, not shown to be the title.
  - **`adhyapak` ships at the cost of a Marathi family name written first.** ORCID's whole extract has 17 family boxes holding it, every one alone (`Anoop Adhyapak`), and 1 given box, after another word, and no given box opens with it. Those bearers do not count against the title the way `shri`'s do: the title claim acts at the head, and a family name alone in its box is written last in the given-first order the corpora hold, the position rules/H1's Accepted clause leaves to `graf` and `greve`, whose bearers write them last too. Its title use is unmeasured, no corpus holding the word; it ships, as `shriyut` does, on its native spelling's reading and the absence of a leading name. Bare at the end of a name it keeps its place (`Anoop Adhyapak`), while one written family first reads it as the title, and so, as rules/H1's Accepted clause states for `graf` and `herra`, do a family name written with a period behind it (rules/H5, `Anoop Adhyapak.` → title `Adhyapak.`, family `Anoop`) and one with a name word behind it before a family comma (rules/H6, `Adhyapak Kumar, Anoop` → family `Kumar`). No corpus holds it as a word, so nothing was measured to move under `bn` for it; the counts above are bearers, not moves.
  - **The default does not move.** The build reports `_compiled.py` current, only `_index.py` gaining the rows; tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes with no re-record; the local corpora against master `b57468db` (below) differ from the base only under the choice.
  - **What each choice moves,** measured 2026-10-30 on `b57468db` plus this change, Python 3.11, with `snapshot.py --languages TAG` and `snapshot.py --local SOURCE --base <master checkout> --languages TAG`. Committed corpora: none, under either, from a population holding no row that writes any of the four words (a whole-word, case-insensitive scan of every committed `corpus*.jsonl`, 2026-10-30). CLDR's locale view (`cldr_agreement.py`, `hi` and `hi_Latn` mapped to HI, `bn` to BN; the native view's `hi-in`, `bn-in` and `bn-bd` strata too): `hi` 31/48 and `bn` 14/24 renderings agreeing with and without the pack, Wikidata's native `hi-in` 60/60 and `bn-in`, `bn-bd` 40/40 both ways; the record gains their rows, unchanged. nepal-candidates: 17 names under each choice, the 16 `Shrimati` romanizations and `Smt.Nagiya` below, 15 of them each word leaving the given name for the title and the word after it taking the given name (`Shrimati Renu Yadav` → title `Shrimati`, given `Renu`); `Shrimati Kushwaha`, two words, reads title `Shrimati`, family `Kushwaha` and no given name, a cost, as `Mrs Kushwaha` reads by default; and `Smt.Nagiya Devi Awadha` takes the glued token whole into the title, `Devi` taking the given name (the Accepted bullet below). jrc-names, ctgov-officials, psc-individuals, orcid-researchers, patentsview-inventors, fec-individuals and ukec-individuals: none under either, a null over populations that hold not one row writing `smt`, `shrimati`, `shriyut` or `adhyapak` as a word (the same scan of each source's `raw.jsonl`; a substring scan finds FEC 11, jrc-names 5 and PSC 3, all inside other words, `SMTH`, `Arsmtrong`), so it is evidence of nothing beyond that; nepal-candidates' 17 rows are the only ones any source holds; every move these held on the tree with `shri` and `sushri` was one of those two words (the figures above, FEC's 9 a first name `SHRI` after the family comma, `BANGALEY, SHRI` → title `SHRI` and no given). Pairs (`local_corpora.py alike nepal-candidates --languages TAG --base`): 125,833 of 126,981 read alike under the default, 125,839 under each choice, 11 now alike and 5 now apart. The 11 pair `Shrimati` with `श्रीमती`, a default title (10), or the spaced `श्री मती` (1); the 5 pair it, or `Smt.`, with `श्रीमति`, short i, which hi.toml does not list, so the Devanagari reads a given name there (Open below).
  - **Accepted: a word glued behind the period reads as the title under the pack.** nepal-candidates' `Smt.Nagiya Devi Awadha` (`श्रीमति नगिया देवी आवधा`) reads title `Smt.Nagiya` under either pack, where the default reads given `Smt.Nagiya`: `_vocab.period_joined_vocab` makes a period-joined token a title where any chunk is a title, the shape the phase 3d entry's Open names for `श्री.राम`, here in the other direction.
  - **Accepted: `SMT` as a US credential reads as the title in every comma part that holds it.** NPPES has 4 of its 4,824,544 credentialed providers writing `SMT` (`tools/name_usage.py smt`, the pinned August 2026 file). Under either pack it leaves the suffix for the title wherever a comma part after the name holds it, alone or in a credential run, before or after the other credentials and past a second comma (`Jane Doe, SMT`, `Jane Doe, MD, SMT`, `Jane Doe, PhD SMT`, `Jane Doe, SMT MD`, each giving title `SMT` and the rest of the suffix, where the default reads it all as the suffix), and where it ends the given part after a family comma (H8, `Doe, Jane SMT`, middle `SMT` by default). Bare after the name with no comma (`Jane Doe SMT`) both read it as a name word. A Hindi or Bengali choice is not made for US healthcare data, and 4 providers in 4.8 million is the measured size of the cost.
  - **The phase 3d entry's question, a `given` reading of `श्री` in hi.toml or bn.toml, is answered no.** For Hindi the evidence is CLDR's: of the four renderings the phase 3d entry found moving under `ne`, two are `hi` (`givenFirst` and `surnameFirst`), one `kok` and one `mr` (`givenFirst` each), each writing `श्री` as the title, so `श्री` stays a title under `hi` and `hi.toml`'s `श्री` note stays true. For Bengali the answer is unmeasured: no CLDR rendering and no committed corpus holds `শ্রী` (2026-10-30), so `bn` keeps the default's reading of it, a title, by default rather than by measurement.
  - **Open: `shri`, and the romanizations outside 3e's list.** Whether `shri` takes phase 2j's route in the default, a `given` reading beside its title one so that it is a title only inside a title phrase, is default work this entry does not do; its counts are on its exclusion. CLDR's `hi_Latn` writes the salutation `Shree`, the spelling nepal-candidates romanizes `श्री` as a name word in 191 pairs (the phase 3d entry); nepal-candidates' romanized names open with `Shreemati` 47 times, three times `Shrimati`'s 16, 6 of them paired with `श्रीमति`, 41 of the 47 paired with `श्रीमती` and none opening an ORCID given box, and with `Srimati`, Bengali's romanization of শ্রীমতী, 12 times, 11 of them paired with the salutation (`श्रीमती` 10, `श्रीमति` 1), against 4 ORCID given boxes holding it alone (Naik, Nayak, Ghosal, Basu), the direction `shrimati`'s counts take; none was measured as a reading here. 18 nepal-candidates pairs open their Devanagari with `श्रीमति`, short i, a spelling of `श्रीमती` hi.toml does not list.
