- **2026-10-30 #753, phase 3g (its titles' `default = false`, all but `doña`'s, superseded by the 2026-11-06 #1115, the pack titles entry, which is the default change this entry left for later) — THE SPANISH AND PORTUGUESE PACKS, `locales.ES` AND `locales.PT`: THE TITLES THAT LEAD A NAME WITHOUT A COMPLEMENT TURN ON; `don`, `dona`, `dom` AND `maestro` STAY OUT, MEASURED; `sr` KEEPS ITS GENERATION AND `h` IS EXCLUDED; `ES` READS TWO FAMILY NAMES; NO TITLE-RUN CONNECTIVE; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** Carries out 3g of item 11 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry.
  - **What each pack turns on.** `locales.ES` chooses `es`, turns `two_family_names` on through es.toml's `[rules]` table (sub-phase 4f's schema, `PACK_RULES` gaining `es`), and carries `nameparser/locales/_es_given_names.py`'s list as its lexicon fragment, `Lexicon(given_names=GIVEN_NAMES)`, as `locales.ZH` carries its surnames; the switch and the list are decisions/O6's, and the decision to turn them on, made in chat, and its measurement are decisions/O6's 2026-10-30 phase 3g entry, which also records why `two_given_names` stays opt-in (decisions/O7's). `locales.PT` chooses `pt`, with an empty fragment and no patch, until a Portuguese given-name list exists (the same entry). es.toml writes `default = false` the academic readings `lic`, `licenciado`, `licenciada`, `lcdo`, `lcda`, `ingeniero`, `ingeniera`, `arq`, `mtro`, `mtra`, `doctora`, `profesor`, `profesora` and the salutation `doña`; pt.toml `doutor`, `doutora`, `professora` and `profa`. Each is `default = false` because phase 3 moves no default parse (item 12), not because another language collides with it; whether some belong in the default is a default change, left for one. Counts are on each reading, from `tools/name_usage.py --boxes` and a count of the names in every local corpus that open with the word or open the part after a comma with it.
  - **Excluded, measured, the packs included:** `don` (es.toml), `dona` and `dom` (pt.toml), each a given name that opens more names than the title does, Spanish and Portuguese data included (the phase 3e criterion, counted in persons over every population measured): `Don` opens 80 ClinicalTrials.gov officials' names, 86 PatentsView inventors', 11 PSC filers', 2 of tse-donors' 3 and 2,819 FEC given parts after the family comma, against jrc-names' 874 openers that mix the name and the Italian clergy's title; `Dona` and `Dom` alike (their reasons hold the counts). `maestro` is a Spanish and Italian family name (ORCID's family box 43 records in Spain) whose form of address is written `Mtro.`. `doña` ships though it is a family name in Spain (ORCID's family box 18): the title claim acts in front, as for `graf` and `herra` (rules/H1's Accepted clause on a title word borne as a name, rarely as the first word of one), so where a name opens with the family name the pack gives it to the title, accepted costs that no local corpus name takes (2026-10-30): before a family comma with a name word behind it (rules/H6), `Doña Ortega, María` reads title `Doña`, family `Ortega` where the default gives family `Doña Ortega`, and under `FAMILY_FIRST` `Doña Ortega Ana` reads title `Doña`, family `Ortega`, given `Ana`, where the default gives family `Doña`. `ing` stays excluded (mul.toml): written with its period it already reads as a title at the head by rules/H2's shape.
  - **`sr` keeps its generation under both packs.** The phase 3h entry left it Open for 3g: es.toml and pt.toml each held only a salutation reading of `sr`, so a choice preferred it and English's generation left, and a trailing `Sr` read as the family name (`Gary Holloway Sr` family `Sr`, PSC 12 filers, 10 of them Filipino by nationality, their names written in Spanish, and 1 American and 1 English). Each file now writes a `generation` reading beside the salutation, names written in Spanish and Portuguese writing the English generation too, so the choice leaves `sr` a dual position decides, as the default reads it, and `sr` leaves both choices' deviation surfaces; under `pt-BR` too.
  - **`h` (hijo) is excluded, the pack included.** es.toml wrote it `default = false` in phase 2 for this pack to turn on. Turned on, it moved 6,569 FEC names, 6,565 of them a middle initial H (`ABBOTT, DALLAS H` read generation `H`), about 35 ORCID records (`Chan, Charles W H`; `de Faria Santos, H.`, left with no given name), 11 JRC-Names ones (`Anthony Floc h`) and 8 ClinicalTrials.gov officials (`Amy h, MD`), and read hijo in none, measured 2026-10-30 as below, so it is an `excluded` generation with these counts, as `f` and `n` are in pt-BR.toml (the phase 3h entry). rules/T5's Except bullet now says so; de's `I.` stays off by default for a German pack.
  - **Office titles that take a complement stay out of this cut** (`ministro`, `secretario`, `presidente`, `director`, `alcalde`, `juez`, `diputado`, `senador`, `gobernador`, `rector`, their feminines and their Portuguese forms): an office is written in apposition after the name in Spanish and Portuguese, set off by commas and lowercase, and the shape that would need the connective rule below is the shape they would take.
  - **Declined: a connective that continues a title run** (item 11's "a connective that counts only inside a title run", which the work order left open). The shape is unwritten in name data. Re-measured here over 8,526,439 names, the ten local corpora cached on 2026-10-30 before the day's two new ones (`ctgov-officials`, `fec-individuals`, `jrc-names`, `nepal-candidates`, `orcid-researchers`, `patentsview-inventors`, `psc-individuals`, `tse-donors`, `tw-donors`, `ukec-individuals`; `cz-candidates` and `govuk-people`, fetched later that day, raise a re-run's total to 8,700,382) and the ten committed corpora (`tools/differential/corpus*.jsonl`): a name opening with an office or academic title followed by `de`, `del`, `da`, `do`, `dos` or `das` occurs 16 times, and none is a title phrase with a name behind it, which is what the decline rests on. 11 are a particle-led surname behind the title (`General De Gaulle` 8 times in jrc-names, `Dr. de la Vega, Juan`, `Dr. Do Van Johnson, MD`, `Prof. Del Bo' Cristian`), the costs such a rule would pay; 2 are a given name behind the title that opens with the connective's spelling (`Dr. Del Booth`, PSC's forename box `Del`; `Professor De Wet Swanepoel`, the Afrikaans given name De Wet), costs too; 1 is no title at all (`Cardeal da Conceicao Manuel`, ORCID's given box `Cardeal da Conceicao`); 1 is a title phrase with no name behind it (`Prefeito de Salvador`); and `Dr. De` is a fragment. The title set is wider than the work order's offices, so it counts the cost cases a connective rule would reach as well: `ministro`, `ministra`, `secretario`, `secretaria`, `secretário`, `secretária`, `presidente`, `presidenta`, `director`, `directora`, `diretor`, `diretora`, `alcalde`, `alcaldesa`, `prefeito`, `prefeita`, `juez`, `jueza`, `juiz`, `juíza`, `diputado`, `diputada`, `deputado`, `deputada`, `senador`, `senadora`, `gobernador`, `gobernadora`, `governador`, `governadora`, `rector`, `rectora`, `reitor`, `reitora`, `profesor`, `profesora`, `professor`, `professora`, `doctor`, `doctora`, `doutor`, `doutora`, `dr`, `dra`, `prof`, `profa`, `decano`, `decana`, `ingeniero`, `ingeniera`, `engenheiro`, `engenheira`, `licenciado`, `licenciada`, `cónsul`, `consul`, `embajador`, `embajadora`, `embaixador`, `embaixadora`, `consejero`, `consejera`, `conselheiro`, `conselheira`, `vereador`, `vereadora`, `comandante`, `general`, `coronel`, `capitán`, `capitão`, `obispo`, `bispo`, `arzobispo`, `arcebispo`, `cardenal`, `cardeal`, `fiscal`, `procurador`, `procuradora`, `magistrado`, `magistrada`, `canciller`, `chanceler`. `lic`, which this change ships, is not in it; with it, `Lic. De la Cruz Chávez` makes a 17th leading hit (18 in all), a cost. The part after a comma opens that way once (`María Xiomara Rojas Villar, profesora de la Universidad Central …`, ORCID). Revisited when a corpus with more titles exists; a survey of title-rich sources the same day (Spain's BOE, Mexico's DOF, Brazil's DOU) found offices written after the name in apposition, lowercase. Recompute: a scratch count over the `corpus.jsonl` of each local corpus named above and every `tools/differential/corpus*.jsonl`, a line cut at `"\n"` only, a name whose first word, its periods stripped and case-folded, is one of the titles listed above and whose second is one of the six connectives, and each part after a comma tested the same way.
  - **DEVIATES.** `locales.ES` is not a choice alone, so it declares `DEVIATES` for its patch and fragment only (the phase 3d entry's model, `_pack_deviates` oring in the choice's derived surface): rules/O6 acts only on three name words and the list only where O6 acts, so a name of three or more words, commas read as spaces, or of three or more runs of letters declares, a Han, hangul or kana run counting twice for the segmentation that may divide it. The gate found both widenings: `Mary-​Jane Smith`, words separated by a zero-width space, and `马丁·路德·金` and `김민준 John`, where segmentation or the interpunct makes the third word. It is written with `str` methods, since the registry contract reads a module-level pattern as a marker regex to sweep. `test_choice_rotators_cover_every_changed_word` now sweeps every pack with a choice, not only a pack that is nothing but one, so ES's rotators are held to its choice's surface too.
  - **The default does not move.** The build reports `_compiled.py` current; the committed snapshot needs no re-record.
  - **What each choice moves,** the choice alone (`snapshot.py --languages TAG`; `snapshot.py --local SOURCE --base <detached master> --languages TAG`; ES's patch is measured in decisions/O6's entry), measured 2026-10-30 on `5c3acb56` plus this change, Python 3.11. Committed corpora: `es` none; `pt` 35 and `pt-BR` 37, the phase 3h entry's 38 and 40 less the three `Sr` names (`J. Smith Sr.`, `Smith, Sr.` and a CLDR family-first rendering holding `Sr.`), the rest `do` and `e` as before. Local corpora under `es`: FEC, PSC, tse-donors and UKEC none; ClinicalTrials.gov 128, ORCID 33, JRC-Names 2, PatentsView 1. About 140 of those 164 are a title the choice turns on, read at the head or after a comma where the default read a given name (`Adam Zając, Profesor`, given `Profesor` and family `Adam Zając` by default, title `Profesor`, given `Adam`, family `Zając` under the choice; `Doctora Yenny Nereida Montilla Polo`; `Arq Jose Luis Canovas`); the rest move a credential written with `Lic.` or after a comma between the title and the suffix (`Erik Blennow Nordström, PhD, Lic.Psych.`, `Vivian Brache, LIC`), or a given name glued behind `Lic.` (`Lic.Alina Maren Ferrier`) into the title with it, accepted. Under `pt` and `pt-BR` the new titles move 9 ClinicalTrials.gov officials (`Andre P dos Santos, Professor Doutor`), 4 JRC-Names spellings (`Doutor Estranho`) and 1 ORCID record (`Professora Rosária`), the other moves being the `do` and `e` the phase 3b and 3h entries recorded; under `pt-BR` PSC's moves fall from 50 to 38, the 12 `Sr` filers no longer among them (both `snapshot.py --local psc-individuals --languages pt-BR`, the 50 on this branch before the `sr` reading was added, which reads PSC as master's `pt-BR` does, the new titles moving no PSC name); the remaining 38 are 37 trailing `Do` (`Mr Graham Do`), whose reported alternative the particle reading changes, and one `capitalized(force=True)` view, `sb`'s mask in `Mx Sb Dylan Beer`. CLDR's locale view (`cldr_agreement.py`, `es` mapped to ES, PT in `NO_CLDR`, CLDR's `pt` being Brazilian and read with PT_BR): `es` 16 of 24 renderings agree in every role with and without the pack, its patch included, which moves one given-first rendering, CLDR's foreign `Christopher Hans Jacobsen`, to family `Hans Jacobsen`, the cost to a name the pack is not for; `pt` 17 of 24 under PT_BR as before; the record gains ES's rows.
  - **Open: the one-letter abbreviations `C.` (Ciudadano) and `D.` / `D.ª` (Don, Doña).** A survey of title-rich sources (2026-10-30, samples): Mexico's DOF signs `C.` in 7.3% of 150 signatures (`C. Hugolino Mendoza Sánchez`, given `C.`), Spain's BOE writes `Excmo. Sr. D. José Luis Pardo Cuerdo` (given `D.`). Each is written exactly as an initial (rules/T5) and a single letter, so rules/H2's shape refuses it, and a reading would take every `C. Pérez` and `D. Smith` written with an initial; unmeasured against that cost here, no reading written.
  - **The survey's other leads, weighed:** BOE writes `don` and `doña` lowercase before every appointee (66 and 53 of 229 headlines), the one source where `don` leads as the title; it stays excluded, the given-name openers outnumbering it in every population measured, and the counts are in its reason. DOF spells out `Maestro` now and then, `Mtro.` in 2.7%; `maestro` stays excluded. Brazil's DOU writes `Des.` (39) and `Min.` (20), offices behind the cut here (and `Des.` already a title by H2's shape), and `Sra.`, `Senhora`, `Sr.`, `Senhor`, already titles.
  - **Recompute:** the commands above from a checkout of this change against a detached checkout of master; the word counts with `tools/name_usage.py <word> --boxes --country ES --country MX --country BR --country PT --country AR --country CO` and the openers by reading each local corpus's `corpus.jsonl` and counting the names, and the parts after a comma, whose first word, its periods stripped and case-folded, is the word.
- **2026-10-30 #754, sub-phase 4h — THE SHAPE INVENTORY PER ORDER: THE ARRANGEMENTS EACH ORDER IS WRITTEN IN, LISTED IN tools/differential/shapes.py WITH WHAT EACH READS AND READ UNDER THEIR ORDER BY A TEST; NO READING MOVES.** Carries out the 4h row of item 9 of the 2026-10-28 #754 output-model entry, and the RFC's plan to make shapes first-class as far as listing them: what each order accepts is now a table, not prose.
  - **The model: a shape stays the corpus tag, with its arrangements listed beneath it.** `ARRANGEMENTS` holds one row per written arrangement, each naming the `SHAPES` id it is written under and inheriting that shape's order and `min_baseline`. One `Shape` per arrangement was the alternative and was not taken: the shape id is the corpus tag that carries the order (`corpus_shapes.jsonl`, read by `snapshot.py`), `build_conventions_corpus._shape_for` resolves an order to exactly one shape, tests/v2/cases.py hand-copies the ids, and compare.py skips an entry below its shape's `min_baseline`; several shapes per order would restate one order and one baseline in each. The default order keeps a shape per comma family (2 a family comma, 3 a suffix comma); each family-first order has one shape for every comma family, so its family-comma rows (`Wang, Xiaoming`, `Nguyen Jr., Thi Mai`, `, Thi Mai`) are tagged 4 or 5 though those notations write no family comma, the first corpus lines to be, every comma line tagged 4 or 5 before being a suffix comma. That comma lays the part after it out given-first under every order (decisions/O7), so what the tag carries for them is the order the corpus reads them under, and the row's own notation names the arrangement; the shapes.py docstring says so. Shapes 6 and 7, carried by the script rather than a declared order, have no rows.
  - **What a row records:** the arrangement in `SHAPES`' notation, a name written in it, the positions it instantiates (`POSITIONS`), every role that is not empty as the tree reads it, the kind of every ambiguity reported, and, where a person would not read the name so, a mark saying why the reading stands: `accepted`, the decisions/ key whose entry decided it, or `misread`, the open issue holding it; a row no decision has weighed carries neither and is listed under Open below. The positions are a title at the front, between the family and the given name, at the end and after a full head's comma; a suffix without a comma, before one and after one; a full, one-word and empty head before a comma; a middle name, a particle, a nickname; and nameparser/AGENTS.md's sweep items: a nickname-only head, a connective and a particle surname before a comma, two title-particles at the head, a dual second in a title run, a trailing title after an empty head, the spelling sweep's shape paths (a roman numeral, an unlisted dotted suffix, an undotted suffix word, a title inferred by its period shape) and a letter written decomposed (NFD). tests/v2/test_shape_inventory.py reads every row under its order, holds each order to cover every position it has a slot for (the default order has none between the family and the given name, `NOT_A_SLOT`), holds every position a row claims to its text by a vocabulary-read predicate, one per position (a numeral needs a roman-numeral word, a one-word head one name word before the comma as rules/C3 counts it, `nfd` a text NFC changes), so a mislabelled row fails rather than counting as cover, and holds every mark to a decisions file that exists. The predicates were checked by mutation: moving the default order's only `numeral` onto `Dr. John Smith`, and adding `comma-full-head` to `(Bob), PhD`, each fails.
  - **A marked row is pinned as read, not marked xfail.** An xfail needs the reading the row should give, and a non-strict one stops watching the row, so a change that moved it would pass. Pinned, any move fails, and the change that moves it edits the row and its mark in the same diff.
  - **What it found,** 2026-10-30 on `1f6bdf1d`: 104 arrangements, 36 under the default order, 38 under `FAMILY_FIRST` and 30 under `FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST`, every one reading as its rules say save the unweighed rows under Open. Eight hold #938's shapes, a title between the family and the given name or bare at the end of a family-first name; the six of them in the first draft were recorded as misread pending #938, and all eight are accepted since #938 was declined the same day (decisions/H5's 2026-10-30 #938 entry, the measurement): under `FAMILY_FIRST` `Wang Dr. Xiaoming`, `Wang Dr Xiaoming` and `Wang Insp. Xiaoming` (given the title, middle `Xiaoming`), `Brühl Prof. Dr. Ada Cornelia` (given `Prof.`) and `Wang Xiaoming Senator` (middle `Senator`); under `FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST` `Nguyen Dr. Thi Mai` and `Nguyen Insp. Thi Mai` (middle `Dr. Thi`, `Insp. Thi`) and `Nguyen Thi Mai Senator` (given `Senator`, middle `Thi Mai`). The default order's `John Smith Prof` (family `Prof`) carries the same mark, rules/H5's Then being unscoped by order. A period-marked title at the end reads as the title under every order. The other marked rows: `, Ma Dr.` and `, Xiaoming Dr.` read family `Ma`, `Xiaoming`, rules/H1's title and one name word, the exception decisions/C15 states (`C15`); `Mesnil Jean de` under `FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST` reads given `de`, the declared given slot, rules/P1's Accepted bullet (`P1`); and `Vega y Lopez Jr., PhD Jones` under the default order reads given `Vega`, middle `y`, a connective surname counting its words so that the comma is a suffix comma (decisions/C1's #613 entry, recorded in decisions/C3; `C1`). Its comma-led twin `Vega y Lopez, Juan` is listed as a full head for the same count. A family comma lays the part after it out given-first under every order (decisions/O7), so `Nguyen, Thi Mai` reads given `Thi` under `FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST`.
  - **The corpus and the snapshot.** `build_shapes_corpus.py` adds the arrangements' (shape, text) pairs to the case table's: `corpus_shapes.jsonl` gains 95 lines (300 to 395; 9 arrangements were already tagged rows), and the snapshot gains 80 entries (the other 15 already in another corpus under the same order); the re-record added lines and changed none, so no reading moved.
  - **Negative control,** in the test's docstring, each by its population: with every row parsed under the default order, 49 of the final grid's 68 family-first rows fail (of the 19 that pass, 15 hold a family comma, an empty head included, and 4 lay out no name word by order, `(Bob), PhD` and `Freiherr St van Berg MA`); the first grid's 64 rows failed 31 of their 40.
  - **Recompute:** `uv run pytest -n0 tests/v2/test_shape_inventory.py`; the counts by order from `ARRANGEMENTS` and `SHAPES` (each row's order is `SHAPES[row.shape].order`); the corpus and snapshot counts from `git diff --numstat` against `1f6bdf1d` after `uv run python tools/differential/build_shapes_corpus.py` and `uv run python tools/differential/snapshot.py --record`; the control by parsing each family-first row with `Parser()`.
  - **Disclosed, not added:** the two title-particles at the head written with periods (`Freiherr St. van Berg MA`, `Freiherr. St. van Berg MA`) read as the bare spelling does, the title run whole, under every order.
  - **Open, unweighed and held by no issue, so these rows carry no mark:** (the particle clause superseded by decisions/P11's 2026-11-04 (Derek), #670 entry, which folds a lowercase one in a mixed-case name; both rows below read family `van Gogh` and `du Ménil` since, marked with a note naming rules/P11) an ambiguous particle opening a family-first name is left where position puts it and reported, as root AGENTS.md's particles paragraph describes, though no decision weighs it there (`van Gogh Vincent` under `FAMILY_FIRST`: family `van`, given `Gogh`; `du Ménil Thi Mai`, its letter decomposed, under `FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST`: family `du`, middle `Ménil Thi`); rules/P1 covers a never-given particle only. And `Vega y Lopez Jr., PhD Jones` under the family-first orders reads given `y`, middle `Lopez` (`FAMILY_FIRST`) and given `Lopez`, middle `y` (`FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST`): the default order's division of the words laid out by the order, which decisions/C3 records under the default order only. And a Hungarian title after a married `-né` word, before the woman's birth family name (`Bakainé dr. Korbács Melinda`), 102 of 37,250 names in the 2024 municipal candidates and every one misread today (decisions/H5's #938 entry has the measurement): what licenses the title there is the `-né` word, so it is a question for a future `hu` pack, which no sub-phase schedules, and not for a general rule.
- **2026-10-30 #753, file-strict (its "native-script vocabulary" superseded by 2026-11-07 #1148: name readings included; its verdict's "the rest of its API is proposed, not decided, and nothing is built", its "Proposed, not decided" bullet, decided save `locales.choose`, which is reversed, and its files-with-no-readings bullet's clause that strict `vi` needs vi.toml readings first superseded by 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j; its verdict's "NOT `mul.toml`", item 2, that `mul.toml` is not in a strict choice, item 3's `PhD`, and the files-with-no-readings bullet's "the native-script pool and that file's name readings" superseded by 2026-11-02 #1005) — STRICT IS FILE-STRICT: A STRICT CHOICE READS ONLY THE CHOSEN LANGUAGES' FILES AND NATIVE-SCRIPT VOCABULARY, NOT `mul.toml` OR `und.toml`, SO IT NEEDS NO 3i; THE `strict=True` ARGUMENT STANDS, THE REST OF ITS API IS PROPOSED, NOT DECIDED, AND NOTHING IS BUILT.** SUPERSEDES: decision 6's strict variant in the 2026-10-15/16 entry, and in the 2026-10-27 #753 entry item 2's definition of strict (other languages' name readings and their markers leave), item 4 (strict waits until name readings say whose names they are) and 3i in item 11. Item 1's `strict=True` argument of `parser_for`, over the whole choice, stands. The definition and items 1 to 3 were decided in chat on 2026-10-30; the measurements were taken on 2026-10-31 on master `b577e9a8`. The design overview is docs/design/vocabulary-model-rfc.md's "Strict: the chosen files and nothing else".
  1. **The definition.** A strict choice compiles only the chosen languages' files, each tag selecting its own and its ancestors', plus native-script vocabulary, which stays on because it selects itself by script (the CJK honorifics; the Korean surname list, outside the per-word model, is in no choice's compile and stays too); everything else leaves, vocabulary and name readings alike. A word with no reading in what is compiled is read by position and shape alone, so under strict `vi`, `do`, which only en.toml and pt.toml hold, is a name word, while the shape rules still act (`Dr. Minh Do` keeps title `Dr.`, rules/H2; `Minh Do, M.D., Ph.D.` keeps suffix `Ph.D.` past the second comma, rules/C2, but reads given `M.D.`). For a credential, the harm item 4 of the 2026-10-27 entry guarded against (`Minh Do` → suffix `Do`) does not arise today, measured 2026-10-31: no strict choice of one tag ungates a credential acronym, each credential's gating name reading sitting in its own file. It holds only while name readings are added to more files and never moved: moving `do`'s Đỗ readings from en.toml to vi.toml would ungate its credential under strict `en`. For the other kinds it can, where the vocabulary reading is in a chosen file and the gating name reading only in a dropped one (the ungated-words bullet below; strict `pt` reads `Do Van Minh` family `Do Van Minh`). 3i, the attribute naming each name reading's bearers' languages, is still not needed for strict: under file-strict that case is remedied by data, a name reading written in the chosen file whose names carry the word. Name-strict, the earlier definition, under which other languages' titles, credentials and particles stayed and only their name readings left, is set aside with 3i.
  2. **`mul.toml` and `und.toml` are not in a strict choice.** `mul.toml` stays as it is: its entries name several languages each or "the European languages" (`dr`, `prof`), or carry no note (the numerals, `&`), so under file-strict it behaves as one more language's file. Open: entries may later move between en.toml and mul.toml, or into the files of the languages that write them, so that strict `en` can shed problem words; which words those are is not yet known.
  3. **Dropping English credentials under strict `vi` is accepted in principle.** Vietnamese writes its degrees in Vietnamese, before the name (`TS.`, `ThS.`, `GS.`, `PGS.`, `BS.`, stacked as `PGS.TS.`), and has no generational suffix; English `PhD` and `MD` come with English-context writing. Measured below rather than assumed.
  - **Measured: ORCID agreement per country** (`local_agreement.read` box by box over each country's capped records in the cached extract, fetched 2026-10-27; the records whose given box opens with a title word left out, 1,717 for IN, 437 for NP and under 70 for every other country, since ORCID has no title box and a holder who typed `Dr` there scores a fix whenever `dr` leaves the vocabulary; fixed and broken against the default). "choice" is the shipped pack carrying the language, else a bare choice of the tag:

    | Country | records | default | choice | strict | strict + en |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | VN | 20,000 | 65.48% | none, no file holds `vi` | 64.28% (+349 −589) | 64.34% (+306 −534) |
    | KR | 20,000 | 92.42% | 92.42% (+0 −0) | 92.54% (+41 −17) | 92.51% (+33 −16) |
    | CN | 20,000 | 84.54% | 84.54% (+0 −0) | 84.65% (+23 −3) | 84.61% (+16 −3) |
    | JP | 20,000 | 95.59% | 95.59% (+0 −0) | 95.64% (+30 −20) | 95.54% (+11 −20) |
    | CZ | 20,000 | 95.12% | 96.54% (+635 −352) | 96.42% (+626 −367) | 96.43% (+625 −364) |
    | SK | 12,962 | 95.01% | 96.58% (+347 −144) | 96.53% (+347 −150) | 96.59% (+348 −143) |
    | PL | 20,000 | 96.88% | 96.88% (+0 −0) | 96.74% (+4 −32) | 96.78% (+4 −25) |
    | DE | 20,000 | 95.01% | 95.00% (+0 −1) | 94.42% (+8 −125) | 94.44% (+7 −120) |
    | ES | 20,000 | 48.04% | 93.47% (+9,295 −215) | 93.21% (+9,286 −258) | 93.22% (+9,285 −255) |
    | MX | 20,000 | 31.33% | 93.01% (+12,534 −204) | 92.93% (+12,524 −210) | 92.93% (+12,523 −209) |
    | BR | 20,000 | 47.30% | 47.33% (+5 −0) | 46.90% (+18 −99) | 47.22% (+9 −26) |
    | PT | 20,000 | 69.99% | 69.99% (+0 −0) | 69.87% (+1 −25) | 69.90% (+1 −19) |
    | NP | 17,541 | 90.85% | 90.86% (+1 −0) | 90.74% (+18 −37) | 90.85% (+3 −3) |
    | IN | 20,000 | 79.82% | 79.82% (+0 −0) | 79.85% (+23 −17) | 79.80% (+6 −10) |

    The ES, MX, CZ and SK gains are the packs' `two_family_names`, in the choice and strict alike; strict against the choice is CZ +9 −33, SK +5 −11, ES +11 −63, MX +6 −22, BR +13 −99, PT +1 −25, NP +17 −37 and IN +23 −17, its breaks mostly other languages' words borne in that country's names, counted as the breaks whose name holds the word (DE `de` 45 and `van` 39 of 124, BR `junior` 62 and `jr` 12 of 99, both in en.toml, pt.toml holding only `júnior`, ES the Catalan `i` 39 of 63). VN's loss is ORCID's family box holding the middle names, which the default's French `le` and Dutch `van` happen to land on (`Manh Le Ngo Duc`, family box `Le Ngo Duc`); holders who write `Văn`, which no vocabulary reads, box it with the family 20 times of 73, against 144 of 802 for `van`. KR, CN and JP gain from Latin words that are their names (`bin`, `do`, `gen`, `de`, `du`).
  - **Measured: the `van` chain.** ORCID VN, `van` before the last word, the name split at spaces only: 802 records (803, 404 and 3 split at commas too, `Master Thuy Van, TT` joining), the default reading it into the family in 403 and the holders in 144; strict reads it there in 2, and the 802 agree 583 times against the default's 479 (boxed with the given names, 658: 354 to 583; boxed with the family, 144: 125 to 0). PSC's Vietnamese filers, which box the middle name apart: 156, 5 boxing `van` as the surname; the default reads it into the family in 97 and agrees 62 times, strict + en in none and 147 times. Over all 1,499 Vietnamese PSC filers, strict + en fixes 93 and breaks 36, 91 of the fixes holding `van` and 20 of the breaks `le`.
  - **Measured: the cost of other languages' credentials and salutations.** PSC: strict `vi` moves 865 of 1,499 Vietnamese filers off their boxes (the parse's given name `Mr` in 466, `Ms` 213, `Mrs` 98, `Miss` 73, `Mr Anh Cong Tran` → given `Mr`), strict + en 36; South Korean, 390 of 556 and 13. ClinicalTrials.gov officials, distinct names, whose name before the first comma holds one of 18 common Vietnamese surnames (nguyen tran le pham hoang huynh phan vu vo dang bui do ho ngo duong ly truong dinh): 1,697, 1,247 with a suffix by default; strict `vi` changes 1,112 of those suffixes and reads one of the default's suffix words (the suffix split at spaces and commas) as the given name in 1,079 (`Ngoc P Ly, MD` → given `MD`), strict + en 9 and 0. With 18 Korean surnames (kim park choi jung jeong kang cho yoon yun jang lim oh seo shin kwon hwang ahn yoo): 5,686, 4,224 with a suffix; strict `ko` 3,678 and 3,569, strict + en 5 and 0. Counted over raw.jsonl's records instead, which repeat an official per study, the figures are 2,319, 1,764, 1,603 and 1,562, and 8,068, 6,208, 5,550 and 5,426. The slice is rough and the corpus holds no ground truth: these are moves.
  - **Measured: what each file lacks to stand alone,** the words a country's ORCID or PSC records write that its strict choice loses: nine of the ten rows a bare `dr` and three `prof` (mul.toml; `Dr.` stays a title by its period; the pt rows rank neither), and in PSC the salutations (en.toml); cs and sk also `ing` (mul.toml), `phd`, `md`, `msc`, `mph`, `jr` (en.toml); pl `maj`, `phd`, `md`; de `v` (mul.toml), `md` and the immigrant particles; es `i` (ca, de, mul and pl), `e` (it and pt), `phd`; pt and pt-BR `junior`, `jr` and the Italian particles; ne and hi `phd`, `md`. The losses that are gains, Latin words that are that country's names: ko `jo` (fil), `se`, `uk`, `ma`; zh `ma`, `meng`, `si`, `chi`, `che`, `du`; ja `gen`; ne and hi `kc`, `das`.
  - **Measured: files with no readings.** hu.toml, kk.toml, kok.toml, vi.toml and zu.toml hold only exclusions, so `selected` refuses each tag; strict `vi` needs vi.toml readings first. 26 more hold no Latin-script vocabulary reading, so strict of one is the native-script pool and that file's name readings (am, as, be, bg, el, gu, ja, km, kn, ko, lo, mk, ml, mr, my, ne, or, sd, shn, si, ta, te, th, ti, uk, zh).
  - **Measured: a derived marker does not always leave with the reading it gates.** Where a vocabulary reading is in a chosen file and the name reading gating it only in a dropped one, strict keeps the reading ungated: 16 words, 17 readings (`st` both as a particle and as a generation), 27 word-and-tag pairs over 13 tags, each strict choice of one tag compared with the plain choice of the same tag: `cheikh` and `sultan` (ar), `la`, `marquis`, `marquise` and `st` (fr), `mac` (ga, gd), `nic` (gd, its name readings in ga.toml), `del`, `la`, `san` and `santa` (it), `bin` and `sultan` (ms), `den` and `tho` (nl), `da`, `do` and `santa` (pt, pt-BR), and the generation `st` turned on by `cs`, `hr`, `sk` and `sl` (its `given` reading in en.toml). Under the plain `cs` choice `Novák, St` reads given `St`, reported suffix-or-name, under strict `cs` suffix `St` with no report; under `gd`, `Nic Smith` reads given `Nic`, reported particle-or-given, under strict `gd` family `Nic Smith`. Strict `pt` reads `Da Costa` family `Da Costa` (given `Da` by default and under the choice); strict `ar` reads `Sultan Ahmed Khan` title `Sultan`, `sultan`'s `given` reading sitting in tr.toml though the name is Arabic too. The remedy under file-strict is data, a name reading in each file whose names carry the word.
  - **Proposed, not decided (the RFC section has the arguments):** a strict call raising where it chooses no language; no `Locale.strict` field; the shipped language-choice constants kept for packs that carry something, ZH, JA, RU and TR_AZ as they are, with `locales.choose(*tags)` for a bare choice; a strict choice turning on the chosen files' `default = false` readings and no other file's; patches and fragments untouched, so `locales.ES`'s given-name list (1,000 entries under strict `es`, measured) and `locales.ZH`'s surnames stay; `Lexicon.given_names` and `Lexicon.surnames` outside the compile, untouched; stacking a union; a caller's own listing of a dropped word dropped, as 3b's delta drops it (a base adding `dr` and `zzz` keeps `zzz` under strict `vi`, measured), a caller adding it after the call.
  - **3i's evidence, kept** from the RFC paragraph this entry retires, should 3i be wanted again: all measured 2026-10-28 on master `c8357e74`, not on this entry's tree: of the 227 name readings, 165 words carried one; `do`'s family box led by Brazil in ORCID (none of Brazil's 7,227 `do` alone, 2,629 opening with it and 4,598 holding it inside) but by share Vietnam (1.90% against 0.81%; PatentsView 1.78% and 0.64%), Vietnam having 618 boxes of `do` alone and Korea 151; `kim`'s family box 17.67% of ORCID's Korean holders and 13.30% of PatentsView's, its given box 0.93% of PatentsView's Danish inventors. Recompute: `uv run python tools/name_usage.py do kim --no-wikidata --boxes`, and the whole-box split from the cached ORCID extract's `family` and `country` keys.
  - **Candidate next steps, not taken here:** Vietnamese degree prefixes as vi.toml readings, each checked for collisions and bearers per nameparser/vocab/AGENTS.md first; `ông` and `bà` stay excluded, Ông being a Vietnamese surname, a clash within Vietnamese strict does not settle; 3h's `nl` prepositions and 3e's `sushri` as data with the clashing name readings declared in ko.toml, vi.toml and or.toml, gated under the plain choice and not under strict, though the plain `nl` choice still reads `Park In Hwan` family `In Hwan` and `Nguyen To Nga` family `To Nga`, item 4's recorded cost, the marker acting at the head only (re-run 2026-10-31), which a follow-up must settle before shipping them; `_reading_index`'s `UNCHOOSABLE` comment and `selected`'s refusal text, which say no choice removes a reading of `mul` or `und`, updated with 3j; the 16 ungated words read one by one.
  - **Recompute:** a strict choice of the tags `T` is `compile_readings(choose(rows, T))` from `nameparser._reading_index`, `rows` the `index()` rows whose tag is in `T` or whose text has a letter and no letter whose `unicodedata.name` contains `LATIN`, applied to `Lexicon.default()` as `_parser._apply_choice` applies `_choice_delta` (the delta taken against `compile_choice(frozenset())`, the markers of a dropped base entry removed), and a pack's own patch and fragment applied by `parser_for(dataclasses.replace(pack, languages=frozenset()), base=Parser(lexicon=that))`. Count a word's share of the moves as the moves whose name, split at spaces and commas with edge periods dropped and casefolded, holds it. Score ORCID with `local_agreement.read(record, local_agreement.FIELDED["orcid-researchers"], parser, local_agreement._title_words(parser.lexicon))` over the cache's `raw.jsonl` records of each country, and PSC the same with `"psc-individuals"` and the `nationality` key; the title-word filter is the given box's first word, split at spaces and periods and casefolded, in {dr, prof, professor, mr, mrs, ms, miss, dra, ing, mgr, assoc, assist, er, engr, drs, ir}. The ungated words: for each choosable tag, the words in a marker (`particles_ambiguous`, `suffix_acronyms_ambiguous`, `suffix_words_ambiguous`, `titles_ambiguous`) of the plain choice of that tag (`parser_for(Locale(code="x", lexicon=Lexicon.empty(), languages={tag})).lexicon`) that stay in the base set of the strict lexicon and leave the marker, or join `titles`; compared with `Lexicon.default()`'s markers instead, it misses the readings a choice turns on (`st` under cs, hr, sk and sl, `nic` under gd) and finds 15 words. The scripts are in no tree: they are a few dozen lines each from this recipe.
- **2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j (its "native-script vocabulary" superseded by 2026-11-07 #1148: name readings included; its built clause that a caller's listing of a dropped word is dropped by the delta superseded by 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface, in effect since 4i-c, 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-c; its head's "ONLY THE CHOSEN FILES AND NATIVE-SCRIPT VOCABULARY", item 4's "every Latin-script reading leaving" and the `UNCHOOSABLE` bullet's "a strict one drops both" superseded by 2026-11-02 #1005) — FILE-STRICT IS BUILT: `parser_for(..., strict=True)` COMPILES ONLY THE CHOSEN FILES AND NATIVE-SCRIPT VOCABULARY; A STRICT CALL CHOOSING NO LANGUAGE RAISES; NO `locales.choose`; THE WORDS STRICT UNGATES ARE DERIVED AND HELD BY A TEST; A FILE HOLDING NO READING IS A STRICT CHOICE ONLY; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** SUPERSEDES: in the 2026-10-30 #753, file-strict entry, its verdict's "the rest of its API is proposed, not decided, and nothing is built"; its "Proposed, not decided" bullet, every item decided as proposed save `locales.choose(*tags)`, which is left out; and the files-with-no-readings bullet's "strict `vi` needs vi.toml readings first". Carries out 3j of item 11 of the 2026-10-27 #753 entry, with the definition of the 2026-10-30 entry. The four API points below were decided on 2026-10-31; the rest of the 2026-10-30 entry's proposed bullet is built as proposed (no `Locale.strict` field; the chosen files' `default = false` readings turned on and no other file's; patches and fragments applied after the strict compile, untouched; `Lexicon.given_names` and `Lexicon.surnames` outside it; packs stacked as a union; a caller's listing of a dropped word dropped by the delta). Built on master `8438ccbb`.
  1. **A strict call that chooses no language raises `ValueError`.** The message names the packs given and offers a pack that chooses a language, the first pack's code where a strict choice accepts it as one (`parser_for(locales.ZH, strict=True)` offers `Locale(code='zh', lexicon=Lexicon.empty(), languages=frozenset({'zh'}))`), else `en`; tests/v2/test_parser.py runs the offered code.
  2. **No public `locales.choose`.** A bare choice is a `Locale` with an empty lexicon and the tags, the spelling docs/locales.rst teaches.
  3. **The words strict ungates are derived**, `_reading_index.strict_ungated`, each strict choice against the plain choice of the same tags and never against the default's markers, which miss a reading the choice turns on; tests/v2/test_reading_index.py holds every one-tag strict choice to a per-tag table, which was the 2026-10-30 entry's 16 words and 27 word-and-tag pairs over 13 tags exactly on 2026-10-31, the strict-gap batches of 2026-11-03 moving it, so a word that joins or leaves is a reviewed diff.
  4. **A strict choice accepts a tag whose file holds no reading** (hu, kk, kok, vi and zu then; vi.toml holds readings since #1108): under strict it is not inert, every Latin-script reading leaving. A plain choice of one is still refused, its message now saying a strict one accepts it. The reading index records every file's tag for this (`FILES` in nameparser/config/_index.py, read by `_reading_index.files`), a file holding only exclusions writing no row of `READINGS`.
  - The build refuses `prefer` and `strict` as data, saying a strength is the caller's (`parser_for(..., strict=True)`), where it had said each was reserved for phase 3; the RFC's phase 3 API section had decided the message.
  - `_reading_index`'s `UNCHOOSABLE` comment and `selected`'s refusal text, which said no choice removes a reading of `mul` or `und`, now say a plain choice never does and a strict one drops both.
  - **The command line** gains `--strict`, which needs `--locale` (exit 2 without it). `--locale` falls back from a pack code to a bare choice of a language tag (`--locale ko`), and a code that is neither exits 2 listing the pack codes and the tags. Under `--strict`, a pack that chooses no language chooses its own code (`--locale zh --strict`), and one whose code is no language (`tr_az`) exits 2, naming a tag to give instead. A pack's language with a region or script subtag (`zh-CN`, `zh-Hant`, `es-MX`, `ja-JP`) exits 2 naming the pack, since a bare choice of it would drop the pack's segmentation, policy and segmenter in silence (found by review of this PR: `--locale zh-CN 王小明` read family `王小明` where `--locale zh` divides it; the RFC declines folding a bare tag into `locales.get` for a reason of its own, a choice no test has measured), while a tag spelled for a pack's code (`pt-BR`) is that pack. These are the debug helper's, chosen with the build rather than decided as API.
  - **Measured: the 2026-10-30 entry's ORCID table re-run on the built code**, its recipe with `parser_for(pack, strict=True)` (and a bare `en` pack beside it for strict + en) in place of the simulation, on this phase's branch over `8438ccbb`, Python 3.11: every cell matches, 14 countries by four columns.
  - **Measured: the worked examples on the built code**, default order, against the default: under strict `ko`, `Kim Do Hyun` reads middle `Do`, family `Hyun` (default family `Do Hyun`, pt's particle), `Kim Bin Hyun` middle `Bin` (ar's and ms's particle), `Ra Min Jun` given `Ra` (de's academic title), `Kim Min Ra` family `Ra` (en's credential), back to suffix `Ra` under strict `ko` + `en`; strict `ja` reads `Gen Hoshino` given `Gen` (default title), strict `zh` `De Xu` given `De`, family `Xu` (default family `De Xu`). Every example the 2026-10-30 entry gives parses on the built code as it says.
  - The default does not move: tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes unchanged, a plain choice compiling as before.
- **2026-11-01 #754, the `Lexicon` surface proposed (item 1's title warning decided by 2026-11-01 #754, the title warning; the rest decided as recommended by 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface) — `add` AND `remove` TAKE WORDS AND A READING KIND, `readings_of` ANSWERS QUERIES, THE CONSTRUCTOR TAKES ONLY THE LISTS AND MASKS, THE `Lexicon` RECORDS ITS CHOICE SO CHAINED CHOICES UNION, AND A 2.x PICKLE LOADS WHERE ITS SETS TRANSLATE BACK; PROPOSED, NOT DECIDED, AND NOTHING IS BUILT.** Proposes the API the 2026-10-28 #754 `Lexicon` surface entry left open (its method names) and the chained-choices entry's open option, for the maintainer to decide before sub-phase 4i starts. The arguments and the options weighed are the RFC's Phase 4 section "The `Lexicon` surface: configuring by reading"; this entry records what is proposed and what was measured.
  1. **Configuring.** `Lexicon.add(*words, kind, **properties)` and `Lexicon.remove(*words, kind=None)`, `kind` a vocabulary kind as the files write it (the five categories with a kind of their own name, `title`, `suffix`, `particle`, `conjunction` and `bound_given`, taking it on `add`; any category, `name` and `relation` included, matching every kind of it on `remove`), the properties the category's behavioral ones (`default` and `cased` refused), a property given its unwritten value removed, and `add` of a kind the word holds updating that reading in every file. Derived markers follow through `compile_readings` at construction. An `add` of a title reading, save one that only updates a held title reading's properties, warns where, in the compile under no choice, the word's `given` reading makes it a title only inside a phrase, naming `remove(w, kind="given")` (`add("dean", kind="title")`); no other edit warns for changing nothing at construction, since a later choice can make it matter (`add("do", kind="preposition")` under `en`). The `generation`, `credentials` and `patronymic` views read a listed word's kind from the lexicon's compile rather than the shipped index, rules/R8 and rules/R9 amended in 4i-b, so a kind a caller states reaches them. Weighed against a positional `add(word, kind)`, a reading object, and new method names beside an `add` kept to refuse.
  2. **Queries.** `Lexicon.readings_of(word)`, a tuple of a new public `LexiconReading` (text, kind, category, properties, languages) under the lexicon's choice; the repr counts readings by kind against the nearer named lexicon. Weighed against publishing `IndexReading` and against a predicate per category.
  3. **What stays public.** `surnames`, `given_names` and `capitalization_exceptions` as the constructor's only fields, `empty()`, `default()` and `|`; a frozen, slotted dataclass whose state is a base, an ordered log of the caller's edits, a choice, the lists and the masks, equal when all five are, the log compared as written, each edit stored frozen (structural, so equality survives a later choice; a base by reference or empty compared by name, loading nothing, a snapshot by its rows), the first three constructor parameters under underscore names so `dataclasses.replace` carries them, validated as `parser_for` validates a choice (a private route beside the 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j entry's item 2, "No public `locales.choose`. A bare choice is a `Locale` with an empty lexicon and the tags", which binds the public spelling and so is not excepted); a compile in `parser_for` refusing what construction refuses, wrapped with the choice (raising recommended; letting the caller's reading win, dropping the clashing shipped reading, offered as the alternative); `|` concatenating the logs, later wins, with `parser_for` joining the packs' fragments in pack order, a later pack winning as in the policy fold, and the caller's lexicon after them, and refusing two different choices or non-empty bases; the `nameparser.config` constants kept as read-only data.
  4. **The choice.** Recorded on the `Lexicon` (read-only `Lexicon.languages` and `Lexicon.strict`, `Parser.languages` and `Parser.strict` reading through), set publicly only by `parser_for` (the underscore constructor parameters carrying it through `replace` and pickle); the default's base every row of the reading index, its readings off by default included, which a choice turns on, held by reference and loaded only when a lexicon needs its rows, so the default keeps the 2026-10-29 lazy load (an eager base offered as the alternative); chained choices union, a caller's edits apply after the choice in the order written, never baked into the base, a strict mismatch in a chained call raises, and `_apply_choice`'s delta retires. If decided it SUPERSEDES both items of the 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b, chained choices entry, and under strict the 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j entry's clause that a caller's listing of a dropped word is dropped by the delta. Weighed against recording nothing and against a `Parser` field. Whether `Reading.languages` follows the choice is left to an optional 4i-d.
  5. **Pickle.** A `Lexicon` pickles its five parts, its base as a snapshot of the rows; a 2.x `Lexicon` state is translated as the nearer named base, `default` or `empty`, and a log replacing each word whose 2.x memberships differ from that base's compile (removing it first only where the base holds it, so the 2.8 fixture's `dean` lexicon equals a fresh `Lexicon.empty().add("dean", kind="title")`), so a 2.8 pack fragment stays empty-based, and loads where the compile gives its sets back (a word in both `titles` and `titles_ambiguous`, which 2.x reads as a title, compared as in `titles` alone), else is refused naming the fields that differ. Whether `Lexicon`'s guard learns `ADDED_IN` needs no work: it has admitted the marker since 2026-10-30 (decisions/A1's #959 prerequisite entry, PR #960).
  6. **Migration.** The three sites a third-party caller meets (`add`/`remove` with a set-field keyword, `Lexicon(...)` and `dataclasses.replace` with one, an attribute read) each refuse with code that runs, translated for the words named, by one translation shared with the pickle load.
  7. **Work order.** 4i-a (the pipeline reads the sets through one internal object), 4i-b (the break, with the tests, docs and migrate.rst's table), 4i-c (the choice recorded), 4i-d optional (`Reading.languages` follows the choice), none moving a default parse.
  - **Measured: the recipes.** On master `a3b1f3e1`, Python 3.11.17, 2026-11-01, a throwaway prototype compiling a lexicon's readings with `compile_readings` over `choose(index(), chosen)`, the whole index with its readings off by default, the caller's edits applied after the choice, folded into today's `Lexicon` through `_lexicon._vocab_fields`: eleven 2.x recipes and their proposed spellings parse every field alike (the RFC section's table: `Dean Smith`, `John Smith LEED AP`, `Professor Plum`, `Guru Prasad Sharma`, `Ram Bahadur DC`, `Van Johnson`, `ANA SILVA E SOUZA`, `Mohamad Ali Khan`, `Jane Smith formerly Jones`, `山田さん`, `Jane Doe Cha`); `add("dc", kind="given")` puts `dc` in the ambiguous acronyms; a configured lexicon compiles in 2.7 ms (mean of 20). The chained call `parser_for(EN, base=parser_for(PT))` reads `Maria do Carmo` family `Carmo` and `John Smith DO` family `DO` on the tree, and `do Carmo` and suffix `DO` with the choice recorded; `parser_for(EN, base=Parser(lexicon=Lexicon.empty().add(particles={"do", "van"})))` keeps `van` alone on the tree and both on the prototype. Recompute: build each pair of lexicons and compare `Parser(lexicon=...).parse(name)` role by role.
  - **Measured: the 2.x pickle translation.** Each set field maps to the readings that compile it (`titles_ambiguous` a title and a `given` reading, `particles_ambiguous` a particle and a `given` reading, `suffix_acronyms_ambiguous` a credential and a `family` reading, `title_phrases` its first word's `attaches`, a title reading of a `modifier` or `style` word folded into that reading, and a word `titles` also holds left out of `titles_ambiguous`'s translation); `compile_readings` over the translation gives back every set of `Lexicon.default()`, of `Lexicon.default().add(titles={"dean", "chancellor"})` with its overlap compared as `titles` alone (the two spellings parse 48 names over both words in four cases and twelve shapes alike, every field and ambiguity), and of both lexicons in tests/v2/data/lexicon_v28.pickle (`Lexicon.empty().add(titles={"dean"})` and `locales.RU`'s empty fragment), and does not for `Lexicon.empty().add(particles={"xo"}, particles_ambiguous={"xo"}, suffix_acronyms={"xo"})`, whose translated `given` reading also marks the acronym ambiguous. A pickle of the default as readings, every index row with its tag grouped by word as nameparser/config/_index.py writes them (2,261 rows, 2,190 on by default), is 33,122 bytes against 27,204 for today's sets, protocol 4. The first draft of this entry counted the on rows alone as the default's base, which a review on 2026-11-01 showed would undo every pack that turns a reading on (`es`'s titles, `ne`'s gate on `guru`, `ga`'s clan particles, `cs`'s `ml` and `st`), and claimed a round trip the `dean` recipe failed until the overlap rule above.
  - **Measured: the callers,** on `a3b1f3e1`, 2026-11-01. A configuring call is a call to `Lexicon`, `add`, `remove` or `replace` with a set field as a keyword, found by walking the syntax tree of each committed `.py` file (generated `nameparser/config/_*.py` left out) and by matching the code lines (`>>>`, `...`, indented) of each `.rst` and `.md` file; a read is an attribute load of a set field; `surnames` and `given_names` are left out of both, item 3 keeping them and `ParsedName` having properties of both names. Configuring calls: tests 204 in 26 files (107 `add`, 22 `remove`, 56 `Lexicon(...)`, 19 `replace`), user docs 29 in 4 (`customize.rst`, `locales.rst`, `migrate.rst`, and `release_log.rst`, history that stays), library 1 (`_default_lexicon`), docs/design 1, locale packs none. Reads: library 111 in 12 files (the parser's own, which 4i-a moves to the internal sets), tests 157 in 16, user docs 25 in 3, tools 17 in 4, 16 of them `Lexicon` reads in `tools/name_usage.py`, `tools/es_given_names.py` and `tools/differential/local_agreement.py` and one a counter in `tools/differential/local_corpora.py`. The first draft counted `surnames` in, giving tests 215 calls and 180 reads. `capitalization_exceptions` by keyword, which stays: 47 test calls and 1 library call.
  - **Checked:** a frozen, slotted dataclass built with `init=False` and a hand-written `__init__` refuses `Lexicon(titles=...)` and `dataclasses.replace(lexicon, titles=...)` with its own message, `replace` calling that `__init__`, and `__getattr__` refuses `lexicon.titles` with its own (2026-11-01, a throwaway class).
  - **Revised after an independent review on 2026-11-01** of head `c5263dc8`, each finding measured again here on `a3b1f3e1`: a 2.x state translated onto an empty base diverged from a fresh default under a later choice (`parser_for(PT, base=loaded)` read `John Smith DO` suffix `DO`, `parser_for(PT)` family `DO`), so it is translated as the default plus a log, and 2.8.0's pickled default, which differs from the tree's compile in 23 words, every one a suffix reading 3.0 added, round-trips that way exactly; when a removal applies was unstated, and the two orders differ (`remove("do", kind="credential")` chosen `en` reads `Maria do Carmo` middle `do` applied after the choice, as 2.x's delta reads it, and family `do Carmo` baked into the base); `dataclasses.replace` drops fields built `init=False`, so the base, log and choice are underscore constructor parameters, a hand-written `__init__` taking them shown to survive `replace` and still refuse `titles=`; the default holding its rows would have reversed the 3a lazy load (loading and building every row costs about 2 ms cached, 18 to 20 ms uncached, in this checkout); the views ignored a stated kind (on the tree, `add(suffix_words={"secundus"})` reads `Marcus Tullius Secundus` credentials `Secundus`, generation `''`); `add("dean", kind="title")` changed nothing in silence (0 of 45 shapes against 27 of 45 for 2.x `add(titles={"dean"})`, the review's grid); equality and `|` left a choice unstated; the strict half of the supersession was unmarked; and `name` and `relation` are categories with no kind of their name. A second review, of that revision, found its translation onto the default base would make a loaded 2.8 pack fragment (built from `Lexicon.empty()`) remove some 1,800 words through `|`, its no-op warning fire on edits a later choice makes matter, and its base equality unstated; the translation now takes the nearer named base, the warning is narrowed to a title made phrase-only by a `given` reading under no choice, and bases compare by name or, a snapshot, by rows.
  - **Revised after the second independent review** (2026-11-01, of `c5263dc8..911d97a0`): normalizing the log made lexicons that parse differently equal (`sir` with `addresses` removed and then `attaches` added, against the second edit alone), so logs compare as written; validation was claimed eager though a later choice can turn on a reading an edit clashes with (`add("st", kind="credential")` under `cs`), so `parser_for` compiles again and refuses with the choice named; the underscore parameters reach a choice outside `parser_for`, so they are validated (first marked an exception to 3j, a wording the third review below reverses); the title warning fired on property updates (`prince`), so a property update of a held title reading is exempt; a paragraph on loading a 2.x pickle of a chosen parser described a pickle no 2.x release can write (choosing languages arrived in 3.0), and is replaced by that fact, and the first wording of the exemption dropped `add("dean", kind="office")`, which warns again (found by review of the revision); `bound_given` is a fifth category with a kind of its name; and `|` put a pack's edits after the caller's, so `parser_for` joins the fragment under the caller's lexicon.
  - **Revised after the third independent review** (2026-11-01, of `911d97a0..f5b4e014`): the phrase quoted as 3j's, "a bare choice has no constructor", is the Standing line's restatement, not the 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j entry's text, whose item 2 is now quoted; that item binds the public spelling of a bare choice, so the underscore parameters are a private route beside it and no exception to it, and item 4 says the choice is set publicly only by `parser_for`; `parser_for` joins the packs' fragments in pack order, a later pack winning as the policy fold has it, before the caller's lexicon; edits are stored frozen; and the clash at `parser_for` is offered as raise (recommended) or caller-wins.
  - Not touched: no code, no test, no rule; `Lexicon`, `Parser` and `Locale` keep their 2.x shape until 4i-b.
- **2026-11-01 #754, the title warning (Derek) — AN `add` OF A TITLE READING THAT A `given` READING KEEPS A TITLE ONLY INSIDE A PHRASE WARNS, NAMING `remove(w, kind="given")`; DECIDED AHEAD OF THE REST OF THE `Lexicon` SURFACE, WHICH STAYS PROPOSED.** Decided on 2026-11-01, on item 1 of the 2026-11-01 #754, the `Lexicon` surface proposed entry. Under the configure-by-reading API, when it is built (4i-b), an `add` of a title reading warns at construction (`UserWarning`) where, in the compile under no choice, the word's `given` reading makes it a title only inside a phrase (rules/H9), the warning naming `remove(w, kind="given")`: the trap of the 1.4 recipe `add(titles={"dean"})`, whose literal translations `add("dean", kind="title")` and `add("dean", kind="office")` move no parse (0 of 45 shapes, against 27 of 45 for the 2.x spelling, measured by an independent review on 2026-11-01). It fires where the `add` creates a title reading or re-adds a held one with no property changed, and not where it only updates the properties of a title reading the word holds (`add("prince", kind="nobility", attaches=[...])`), the scope the review proposed with the decision. The reference is the compile under no choice, stated because a caller's edits apply after any later choice; nothing else of the proposal is decided by this entry.
- **2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface (Derek; item 5's translation of a 2.x pickle, item 6's per-word refusals and the decline of refusing every 2.x pickle, option (a), which 3.1 adopts, superseded by 2026-11-08 #1003) — THE CONFIGURE-BY-READING API IS DECIDED AS RECOMMENDED; CALLER-WINS AT `parser_for`, AN EAGER DEFAULT BASE AND REFUSING EVERY 2.x PICKLE ARE DECLINED; NOTHING IS BUILT.** Decides the 2026-11-01 #754, the `Lexicon` surface proposed entry, each item as it recommends; its arguments are the RFC's Phase 4 section "The `Lexicon` surface: configuring by reading", and its measurements that entry. SUPERSEDES: both items of the 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b, chained choices entry, and the 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j entry's built clause that a caller's listing of a dropped word is dropped by the delta (each in effect since 4i-c landed, 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-c).
  1. **Configuring.** `Lexicon.add(*words, kind, **properties)` and `Lexicon.remove(*words, kind=None)`, kinds and properties as the language files spell them; derived markers through `compile_readings`; the `generation`, `credentials` and `patronymic` views reading a listed word's kind from the lexicon, rules/R8 and rules/R9 amended when 4i-b builds it. The title warning was decided on 2026-11-01 (2026-11-01 #754, the title warning).
  2. **Queries.** `Lexicon.readings_of(word)` returns a public `LexiconReading`; the repr counts readings by kind against the nearer named lexicon.
  3. **State.** Five parts, a base, an ordered log of the caller's edits, the choice, the lists and the masks, compared structurally with the log as written and each edit stored frozen; the base, log and choice constructor parameters under underscore names, validated as `parser_for` validates a choice; `|` later-wins, `parser_for` joining the packs' fragments in pack order and the caller's lexicon last. An edit that clashes with a reading a later choice turns on raises at `parser_for`, naming the choice and the edit.
  4. **The choice.** The `Lexicon` records its languages and strictness, set publicly only by `parser_for`; chained choices union; the caller's edits apply after the choice, in the order written; the default's base stays lazy, keeping the 2026-10-29 lazy load (the 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3a entry).
  5. **Pickle.** Option (b): a `Lexicon` pickles its five parts, its base a snapshot; a 2.x state translates onto the nearer named base and a per-word log, loading where the compile gives its sets back and refused otherwise, naming the fields.
  6. **Migration.** The old spellings (`add`/`remove` with a set keyword, `Lexicon(...)` and `dataclasses.replace` with one, a set attribute read) fail loudly with a per-word translation, the one the pickle load uses.
  7. **Work order.** 4i-a, the pipeline reading the sets through one internal object; 4i-b, the break; 4i-c, the recorded choice; 4i-d, `Reading.languages` following the choice, optional; none moves a default parse.
  - Declined: letting the caller's reading win where it clashes with a reading a later choice turns on (the clash is refused at construction, and one rule at both points is the one a caller can predict); an eager default base, holding the index rows in `Lexicon.default()` (it would reverse the 2026-10-29 lazy load for every caller, about 2 ms cached and 18 to 20 ms uncached on the first use); refusing every 2.x `Lexicon` state, pickle option (a) (the default and the 2.8 fixtures translate back exactly, and the commonest customization, `Lexicon.default().add(titles={"dean"})`, does with a word in both `titles` and `titles_ambiguous` compared as in `titles` alone, the two spellings parsing alike). The alternatives the proposal weighed for its other items, each recorded there with its reason, are declined with them: a positional `add(word, kind)`, a reading object, new method names, publishing `IndexReading`, a predicate per category, recording no choice, a `Parser` field for it, and pickling the base by reference.
- **2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-a — THE PARSE READS THE VOCABULARY SETS THROUGH ONE INTERNAL OBJECT, `Lexicon._sets`, NOT THE PUBLIC FIELDS; THE THREE TOOLS MOVE TO IT; NOTHING PUBLIC CHANGES AND NO PARSE MOVES.** Carries out item 7's first PR of the 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface entry.
  - **What.** `_lexicon._LexiconSets`, a frozen, slotted object with one frozenset per `_VOCAB_FIELDS` entry: the 20 fields 4i-b makes internal, and `surnames` and `given_names`, which stay public but sit in the rosters beside them (`_assemble._listed_whole` walks all of `_VOCAB_FIELDS`, and `_vocab.in_any_wordlist` holds `surnames`; `claimed_as_non_name` holds neither), so that a roster is read off one object. Each `Lexicon` builds one in `__post_init__`, from its normalized fields by keyword (tests/v2/test_lexicon.py holds its field names to `_VOCAB_FIELDS`, in order, so a field added to one and not the other fails), into a slot `_sets` inherited from a plain base class (`_HoldsSets`): a dataclass field, even `init=False`, would have changed `dataclasses.fields(Lexicon)`, the pickled state and the 2.8 pickles' layout check. It is not compared, hashed or pickled; a load, `dataclasses.replace` and `|` rebuild it. Every library module but `_lexicon` reads `lexicon._sets.titles` where it read `lexicon.titles`; a membership test stays a C-level attribute read, one more attribute load and no frame.
  - **Measured: the reads,** on this branch over `b55d4d33`, by the 2026-11-01 entry's recipe (an attribute load of a set field, `surnames` and `given_names` left out): its 111 library reads in 12 files are 8 of `_lexicon`'s own, which stay, 2 `TailRead.titles` reads in `_assign` and `_group`, no lexicon's, and 101 that moved, in 11 files. Moved beside them: 5 reads of `surnames` and `given_names`, and 5 `getattr` reads over a roster (the three above and `_parser._apply_choice`'s two). The tools' 16 `Lexicon` reads moved to `_sets`, none to the index: `local_agreement` reads a pack parser's lexicon, which the shipped index does not describe, and `name_usage` reads `suffix_acronyms_ambiguous`, a marker the compile derives and no index row states. The 17th is `local_corpora`'s counter.
  - **Measured: the frames,** Python 3.11.17, the benchmark's counter over 50 names with a four-digit counter, `b55d4d33` against this branch: the reference row 311.82 and 311.82 (`uv run python tools/perf/call_count.py --against b55d4d33`); by the same counter, `Smith{i:04d}, John` 168.0 and 168.0, `de la Vega{i:04d}, Juan Q., Jr.` 334.16 and 334.16, `John Smith{i:04d}` 158.0 and 158.0. Wall clock over 6,000 names moved within the run-to-run spread (96 to 98 µs a name on both trees). `_CALL_BASELINE` is unchanged.
  - **The guard.** tests/v2/test_layering.py walks every library module but `_lexicon`, and every file under tools/, and refuses a vocabulary field read off a lexicon, one test per `_VOCAB_FIELDS` entry: in the library only a read through `<x>._sets`, or through a name `sets` that every binding in the module assigns from a `_sets`, passes, `TailRead`'s `read.titles` named as no lexicon, and a `getattr` of a lexicon, or of a field named as a constant off anything but a `_sets`, fails; in the tools, whose counters reuse the field names, a read off `lexicon`, `lex`, `<x>.lexicon`, `Lexicon.default()` or a name any binding gives one of those (an assignment, a walrus, a `Lexicon` annotation) fails, the AGENTS.md recipe's `L = Parser().lexicon` among them. Not followed, and stated in the check's docstring: a lexicon handed through a container, a call's return or an attribute other than `.lexicon`, and aliases per scope rather than per module. Its negative controls: a synthetic read in each spelling, library and tool, per field, must be refused, each binding form of `sets` and of an alias has a row, and `_UNGUARDED_READS` records what the check refuses on today's sources with `._sets` taken out again (114 in the library: 106 attribute loads, 5 roster `getattr`s, 3 of them through the `sets` the undoing makes a lexicon, and 3 `sets =` bindings; 16 in the tools, of 40 files). Mutated on the built tree, one `lex._sets.titles_ambiguous` in `_classify` written back as `lex.titles_ambiguous` failed exactly `test_no_reader_takes_a_vocabulary_field_off_a_lexicon[titles_ambiguous]`.
