The derived markers the views will read, a listed suffix word's kind and the patronymic particles (the RFC's item 1), join `_LexiconSets` as attributes, and `_vocab.suffix_kind` and classify's particle kind read them there in place of the index. With `dataclasses.fields(Lexicon)` changing in 4i-b anyway, `_sets` may become a private field then, the base class going. `_parser._apply_choice` reads `_sets` but still writes the public fields through `dataclasses.replace`; 4i-b gives it the readings to write until 4i-c retires it. What 4i-b also breaks, outside the readers the guard holds: the tests' 157 public-field reads (the 2026-11-01 count), and AGENTS.md's recipe for the titles in no suffix set, `L.titles - L.suffix_acronyms - L.suffix_words` on `L = Parser().lexicon`, which reads public fields and is rewritten then over `readings_of` or `_sets`.
  - The default does not move: tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes unchanged and tests/v2/test_cldr_agreement.py with it; the suite passes on Python 3.11 and 3.15 without the `ja` extra.
- **2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-b (its translated refusals and 2.x pickle load superseded by 2026-11-08 #1003, which deletes both) — A `Lexicon` HOLDS READINGS AND IS CONFIGURED BY THEM; ITS SET FIELDS ARE INTERNAL, EVERY 2.x SPELLING REFUSED WITH ITS TRANSLATION; THE VIEWS READ THE LEXICON'S KINDS; A 2.x PICKLE LOADS AS A BASE AND A LOG; NO DEFAULT PARSE MOVES.** Carries out item 7's second PR of the 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface entry, items 1, 2, 3 (but the choice), 5 and 6 of it; the choice recorded on the `Lexicon` is 4i-c's.
  - **What.** `Lexicon` is a frozen, slotted dataclass built with `init=False` and a hand-written keyword-only `__init__`, its fields `surnames`, `given_names`, `capitalization_exceptions`, `_base` (`"default"`, `"empty"` or a snapshot of index rows) and `_edits` (a tuple of `_lexicon._Edit`, each an `add` or `remove` stored canonical: words normalized, properties sorted pairs, a property given its unwritten value held as None), with `_cap_map` and `_sets` private fields built at construction. `add(*words, kind, **properties)`, `remove(*words, kind=None)` (a kind or a category), `readings_of(word)` returning the new public `LexiconReading(text, kind, category, properties, languages)`, a repr counting readings by kind (`Lexicon(default + given: -1)`), `|` concatenating the logs, the left's first, and refusing two different non-empty bases. A configured lexicon compiles its pool (the base's rows, the log applied in order) with `compile_readings`, kept per base and log; `Lexicon.default()` reads `_compiled.py` and loads no index, and neither does `parse("John Smith")` (`test_the_default_never_loads_the_reading_index`). The compile gives three internal sets the public ones did not record, `GENERATION_WORDS`, `CREDENTIAL_WORDS` and `PATRONYMIC_PARTICLES`, written to `_compiled.py` and re-exported by no config module (`CONSTANTS` takes `None` for one), which classify reads in place of the index lookups 4b and 4c made: rules/R8 and rules/R9 amended to read "the lexicon's". The title warning, the build's checks 4, 8 and 9 at `add`, and the three refusals are as the 2026-11-01 and 2026-11-02 entries decide; the refusals and the 2.x pickle load share one translation from sets to readings, `_lexicon._edits_toward`.
  - **Choices made inside the decided design, each a reading of it rather than a new decision.** (1) `add` and `remove` refuse `surnames` and `given_names` too, naming `lexicon | Lexicon(surnames={...})`: item 3 says the two lists change "through `|` with a fragment ... or `dataclasses.replace`", and the RFC rewrites customize.rst's `add(surnames={"김민"})` as that `|`, though its migrate table calls the lists "unchanged". (2) An `add` of a kind the word holds only off by default turns that reading on, in every file holding it (`add("st", kind="generation")` reads `st` as cs, hr, sk and sl's generation): a caller's reading is on, which is item 1's reason for refusing `default`. (3) `readings_of` leaves out `default` and `cased`, the first being on for every reading it returns and the second a mask the lexicon keeps in `capitalization_exceptions`; `LexiconReading.properties` is a tuple of sorted pairs, so the type hashes. (4) The repr counts a reading whose properties changed as one off and one on (`conjunction: +1-1`), and a log that changes no reading as `edits: N`. (5) `_parser._apply_choice` keeps 3b's delta and writes it as readings: the edits that take each word the choice changes to its sets under the choice, through `_edits_toward` with the choice's own readings as the kinds to take, appended to the log, until 4i-c retires it. For every single file tag a choice may name, the 78 tags holding a reading chosen plainly and the 83 files chosen strictly (2026-11-02), on the default, the empty lexicon and a configured one, the compiled sets equal the delta's in every field, the kind sets included: tests/v2/test_parser.py's `test_a_choice_written_as_readings_gives_the_deltas_sets`, one case per tag, its lexicons `_CHOICE_BASES`.
  - **What 2.x could state that readings cannot,** each found translating the tests, and each a configuration, not a default, that no longer has a spelling: a word that is a title and a given name written short at once (`add(titles={"chas"})`, which kept `chas` in `given_name_abbreviations`: the `given` reading that makes the abbreviation keeps a title reading one only inside a phrase, so a title everywhere is the `given` reading removed); a possibly-given particle beside an unambiguous credential (`particles_ambiguous` and `suffix_acronyms` on one word: the `given` reading gates the credential too), whose ordered arms in post_rules' P6 report stay and are held by a test building those sets directly; `may_be_initial` on a caseless letter (`و`), which the build refuses as `add` now does; and a three-word title phrase. A 2.x pickle holding any of them is refused, naming the fields. An orphan marker, by contrast, a marker entry its base set does not hold (`conjunctions_ambiguous` on a word that is no conjunction; `remove(titles={"air"})` leaving `air` a modifier), is dropped (`_lexicon._inert_dropped`), so the spelling translates and the pickle loads as the removal, the marker going with its word (the 2026-11-02 #754, a marker left behind entry below; 2.x did read such an orphan in two places, which that entry accepts moving) (found by the orchestrating review, 2026-11-02: the first draft offered no call for 270 of 786 default titles and 46 of 189 suffix words, and refused those pickles).
  - **Measured.** Frames, Python 3.11.17, `uv run python tools/perf/call_count.py --against 3804b1a1`: the reference row 311.82 on the base and 311.48 here, the memo of the index lookups classify no longer calls; `_CALL_BASELINE` is unchanged and the serial benchmark passes. A configured lexicon's compile, `Lexicon.default().add(...)` with the index loaded and the compile cache cleared, 2.09 ms (mean of 20). A pickle of `Lexicon.default()`, protocol 4, 39,658 bytes, its base the index's 2,261 rows. The suite's configuring calls moved to readings, every pinned parse kept but one, a reading's kind: `Lexicon.default().add("st", kind="suffix")` reads `St` as a post-nominal, where 4b's index lookup read the cs file's generation written off (rules/R8's amendment).
  - **Found by review, 2026-11-02.** `add("bisneto", kind="agnomen")` alone was accepted and read `Pedro Alves Bisneto` credentials `Bisneto`, an agnomen without the name reading the build's check 11 requires of a file, so `add` now refuses it too, naming `.add(w, kind="family")` (the design-docs review). And `parser_for(locales.CS, base=Parser(lexicon=Lexicon.default().add("st", kind="credential")))` built a parser that dropped the caller's credential in silence, where 3b's delta had raised its suffix set guard: `cs` turns on `st`'s generation, and a word holds one suffix kind. `_apply_choice` now compiles its edits and refuses a result short of the delta's sets, naming the words, wrapped with the languages chosen (rules/D2) (the code review). 4i-c's compile at `parser_for` replaces both with the decided raise naming the edit.
  - The default does not move: tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes unchanged and tests/v2/test_cldr_agreement.py with it.
- **2026-11-02 #754, a marker left behind — SUPERSEDED by 2026-11-08 #1003 (no 2.x call or pickle is translated, so no orphan is read) — A 2.x CALL OR PICKLE THAT REMOVED A WORD FROM ITS BASE SET BUT LEFT IT IN A MARKER SUBSET READS AS THOUGH THE MARKER WENT WITH THE WORD, FOR EVERY MARKER SET; THE TWO READINGS 2.x GAVE SUCH AN ORPHAN ARE ACCEPTED AS MOVING.** Decided in chat on 2026-11-02, on the review of 4i-b's fix commit. In 3.0 a marker is a property of its reading (`addresses="given"` on the title, `may_be_initial` on the conjunction, the `modifier` and `style` kinds, a name reading beside a suffix word) or a kind, so taking the reading out takes the marker: `remove(titles={"sir"})` removes `sir` as a title, and being a given-name title goes with it. 4i-b's `_lexicon._inert_dropped` drops each marker entry its base sets do not hold (`_MARKER_READ_WITH`), on both sides of every compare, so the refusal's translation of such a call and the load of such a pickle agree with it.
  - **Accepted: what 2.x read of an orphan, measured on master `3804b1a1` against this tree by the orchestrating review (2026-11-02).** (1) An orphan in `given_name_titles` still made rules/H2's period-shape title address the given name: with `remove(titles={"sir"})`, `Sir. Smith`, `Sir. Elton` and `Smith, Sir.` read given `Smith` and `Elton` on master and family on the tree; `add(given_name_titles={"zork"})` and `Zork. Smith` the same. (2) Any orphan counted as a listed word in the capitals shape's "unlisted" test (`_vocab.in_any_wordlist`): with `remove(titles={"air"})`, `John Smith, AIR` reads given `AIR`, family `John Smith` on master and suffix `AIR` on the tree, unlisted as any word a caller removed. tests/v2/test_lexicon.py's `_LEFT_BEHIND` pins the tree's readings, under the translated call and the loaded pickle alike; recompute by building the 2.x state with the word taken out of its base set alone, as that test's `_state_2x` does, and parsing each text on both trees.
  - Weighed against: keeping the orphan's two readings, which would need a reading that marks without its base, the very state the per-word model was built not to express.
- **2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-c (its "under strict, only the chosen files' and native-script ones" superseded by 2026-11-02 #1005: strict keeps `mul`) — THE `Lexicon` RECORDS ITS CHOICE OF LANGUAGES; CHAINED CHOICES UNION; A CALLER'S READINGS APPLY AFTER THE CHOICE; A CLASH WITH A READING THE CHOICE TURNS ON IS REFUSED AT `parser_for`, NAMING THE EDIT; `_apply_choice` RETIRES; NO DEFAULT PARSE MOVES.** Carries out item 7's third PR of the 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface entry, item 4 and the choice of item 3; the two entries it supersedes, the 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b, chained choices entry and the 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j entry's dropped-listing clause, are in effect from here.
  - **What.** `Lexicon` gains `_languages` (canonical BCP 47 tags) and `_strict`, constructor parameters beside `_base` and `_edits`, read through `Lexicon.languages` and `Lexicon.strict`, which `Parser.languages` and `Parser.strict` read through; they compare, hash and pickle with the rest, and are validated as `parser_for` validates a choice (`_lexicon._check_choice`: a tag no file serves, `mul`, a file holding no reading under a plain choice and strict with no language refused). A lexicon compiles from its base's readings filtered by `choose` (under strict, only the chosen files' and native-script ones first), then its log, then the build's checks over the words the log touches (`_lexicon._chosen_pool`, `_built`); the default base with no edits takes the shipped index's compile under the choice. `parser_for` unions base's recorded choice with its packs', joins the packs' fragments in pack order and base's lexicon after them, and records the choice with `dataclasses.replace`, where the compile and its refusals happen; `|` takes the choice of the side that has one and refuses two different choices or strictnesses, naming `parser_for`. `_parser._apply_choice`, `_choice_delta` and `_choice_readings` are gone, with `_lexicon._MARKER_BASES` and `_edits_toward`'s `hints`; `test_a_choice_written_as_readings_gives_the_deltas_sets` is rewritten as `test_a_choice_moves_only_the_words_the_callers_log_leaves_alone`, over the same 161 tags and three lexicons: the default under a choice compiles as the shipped index does, the empty lexicon stays empty, and a configured one differs from the choice alone only in entries holding a word its log names.
  - **Choices made inside the decided design, each a reading of it rather than a new decision.** (1) **The case masks.** A mask is no reading (item 3 keeps them a field), and a choice still moves them as 3b's delta did (`cs` sets `judr` to `JUDr`; a strict choice drops the masks of every file it drops): `parser_for` moves them in the field, undoing base's recorded choice's moves before applying the union's (`_lexicon._rechosen_masks`), so a chained choice moves them as the union does; `dataclasses.replace(lexicon, _languages=...)` moves none, the masks being the caller's field. The repr counts a chosen lexicon against the named one under the same choice with its masks so moved, and shows the choice by its tags (`Lexicon(default + title: +1, languages pt)`, `languages: 4` past three, `strict`), `_canonical` and the bound shared with `Locale`'s repr. (2) **A reading an unchosen file leaves off stays in the pool, off,** so a caller's `add` of its kind turns it on with its file named, as with no choice; under strict such a file's readings are not in the pool at all. (3) **The clash names the edit only where it is the choice's:** a log that clashes with its base alone (an `add` on a chosen lexicon) raises as it does with no choice. (4) **`parser_for`'s `strict` has three states, as `segmenter` does (decided 2026-11-02 in chat, on the review of this PR).** Omitted (`UNSET`, the default) keeps base's recorded strictness, so `parser_for(locales.RU, base=strict_parser)` and `parser_for(base=strict_parser, segmenter=None)` stay strict; an explicit `False` on a strict base raises `ValueError`, offering both spellings, a strict choice not being undone by a later call; an explicit `True` on a plain base with a choice makes the union strict; and `True` with no language anywhere raises as 3j's does. The first draft refused any mismatch, a policy-only pack and a segmenter swap on a strict parser included (found by the code review); its fix inherited the strictness whenever the call chose no language, which a `bool` default made indistinguishable from an explicit `strict=False`, so that call returned a strict parser in silence (found by the second review), and the `UNSET` default answers both. The annotation is `bool | _Unset`, as `segmenter`'s is. (5) **The wrap** `parser_for` gives an error (rules/D2) is a plain `TypeError` or `ValueError` built from the message, where `raise type(exc)(msg)` failed on `_lexicon._MaskValueError`, whose constructor takes three arguments (latent since #459, found by 4i-b's hand-over); `test_an_error_applying_a_choice_names_the_choice` raises both through a stand-in mask. (6) **A shipped mask the caller's lexicon holds unchanged yields to a pack's mask of the word,** as a shipped reading yields to a pack's reading: the default lexicon's field carries every shipped mask, so the right-biased `|` alone let `Parser()`'s masks outrank every pack's (`phd` written `PHD` in a fragment read `PhD`; found by the code review of this PR); a mask the caller changed or added outranks the pack's. (7) **`_edits_toward`'s kind-set branch went with the delta,** the only caller that passed the kind sets. (8) **Found by the orchestrating review of this PR, 2026-11-02, and fixed:** a pack's case mask lost to the choice's moves where master kept it (`judr` written `JUDR` in a fragment read `JUDr` under `cs`, and its `phd` dropped under strict), so the choice now moves base's masks and the packs' are laid over them (`_parser._with_packs`); a fragment that clashed with base's readings, or with a reading the choice turns on, named no pack, so a failed join is built again pack by pack on the error path and the first pack that fails it is named, a clash of base's own with the choice naming the languages and the packs that chose them (rules/D2); and a pickle written by 4i-b, which held its choice as edits and no choice fields, was refused, so `_languages` and `_strict` carry `_ADDED_IN` and such a pickle loads choosing nothing, reading as it did (tests/v2/data/parsers_4ib.pickle, written on `db7e8fc4`).
  - **Found adding coverage for the translation, 2026-11-02, and fixed:** the calls a 2.x refusal offers for an agnomen ran it before the name reading that gates it (`.add('zzq', kind='agnomen').add('zzq', kind='family')`), which `add` refuses since 4i-b's review added check 11's agnomen clause to it, so the offered calls raised; `_edits_toward` now writes a word's name readings first, which moves the order of two pinned translations and of a 2.x pickle's log, not what either compiles to. The choice's delta, retired here, had been the other path through the translation's property and kind branches, so tests/v2/test_lexicon.py's refusal table gains rows reaching them.
  - **Accepted: a caller's mask written as the shipped value yields to a pack's (decided 2026-11-02 in chat, on the review of this PR).** A `Lexicon` keeps its masks as a field, with no base and no log, so a caller's own mask is read as one that differs from the shipped mask under base's choice: a caller who writes exactly the shipped value, under a pack that sets that key, gets the pack's mask (`phd` written `PhD` by the caller under a pack's `PHD` reads `PHD`, where `Phd` stays `Phd`). No shipped pack sets a mask. Weighed against reading the caller's whole field as its own, which would let no pack override a shipped mask and a mask the choice moved outrank a pack's, and against giving the masks a base and a log as the readings have, which item 3 declined.
  - **Accepted: a mask the caller deleted comes back when a strict choice is chained.** Undoing base's choice's mask moves restores the shipped value of each mask that choice dropped, and cannot tell one the caller deleted first: with `bsc` deleted from the default's masks, `parser_for(EN, base=parser_for(KO, base=Parser(lexicon=mine), strict=True), strict=True)` holds `bsc` and `parser_for(KO, EN, base=Parser(lexicon=mine), strict=True)` does not (found by the code review of this PR). Telling them apart would need the masks to be a log as the readings are, which item 3 declined to make them.
  - **What moved, each the decided supersession.** `parser_for(EN, base=parser_for(PT))` reads `Maria do Carmo` family `do Carmo` and `John Smith DO` suffix `DO`, where 3b's delta read family `Carmo` and family `DO`; `parser_for(EN, base=Parser(lexicon=...add("do", "van", kind="preposition")))` keeps both particles, where the delta kept `van` alone; under strict `vi` a base adding `dr` and `zzq` as titles keeps both, where 3j's delta kept `zzq`; a choice over a base built from `Lexicon.empty()` turns no reading on, where the delta joined the chosen files' off readings; and a pack's fragment yields to the caller's lexicon (no shipped fragment holds a reading or a mask, ZH's and ES's holding lists only, so no shipped pack moves). Each is pinned in tests/v2/test_parser.py.
  - **Measured.** On this branch over `db7e8fc4`, Python 3.11.17: tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes unchanged; `uv run python tools/differential/cldr_agreement.py` reports the CLDR locale view and the Wikidata native locale view matching their records (JA not measured, namedivider absent); frames, `uv run python tools/perf/call_count.py --against db7e8fc4`, the reference row 311.48 on both, `_CALL_BASELINE` unchanged and the serial benchmark passing.

- **2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-d (decision (1)'s strict clause, "`mul` and `und` gone", and decision (3)'s strict `pt` reading of the Ph./D. pair superseded by 2026-11-02 #1005: strict keeps `mul`, and `phd` with it; "assemble's two memos" by 2026-11-05 #1085, which makes them one) — `Reading.languages` NAMES THE FILES WHOSE READING IS ON IN THE PARSE'S OWN `Lexicon`; A CHOICE NAMES WHAT IT READS AND NOT WHAT IT DROPS; A CALLER'S READING NAMES NONE; NO PARSE MOVES (decided 2026-11-02 in chat, on the proposal below).** Carries out item 7's optional fourth PR of the 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface entry, and the question its item 4 left to it; supersedes item 1 of the 2026-10-29 #754, 4a's follow-up entry in its default's-files semantics, and the 2026-10-29 #753, phase 3b entry's bullet that `Reading.languages` does not follow a choice, whose second reason, that a parse could not know its choice, 4i-c removed.
  - **Decided, three questions.** (1) **The files on in the lexicon, not the chosen files only.** A reading names the files whose reading of that kind is on in the pool the parse's `Lexicon` compiled from: the default's files with no choice; under a plain choice the chosen files and every other file whose reading the choice's preference left on (`de` under `pt` names `pt`, `van` keeps `nl`, which `pt` does not hold), name readings never leaving (decision 15); under strict the chosen files and native-script files, `mul` and `und` gone with their readings. Declined: the chosen files only, which reports `()` for a word only an unchosen file holds, the same answer as "no file holds it", and false. (2) **A caller's log follows the same rule, with or without a choice.** A caller's own reading (file tag `""`) names no file, as before; an `add` of a kind a file holds off by default turns that file's reading on and names it (`Lexicon.default().add("st", kind="generation")` names `cs`, `hr`, `sk` and `sl` for `Jiří Dienstbier st.`, where it named none), and a `remove` stops naming the file whose reading it took. Declined: keeping a lexicon with no choice on the default's files, a second rule for one case. (3) **The `Ph. D.` pair under a strict choice is a separate question.** `split_pair` merges it by its pattern whatever the vocabulary (decisions/phd-merge), so under `parser_for(locales.PT, strict=True)` and `Lexicon.empty()` it still reads `credential`, source `vocabulary`, where the joined `PhD` reads family; it named `en` before this change and names none now. Whether the parse or its source should change goes to a follow-up issue, drafted, not filed. (Answered 2026-11-02 by #1000, decisions/phd-merge: the pair is a credential only where the lexicon lists `phd`, so under those two it reads as the two words it is written as.)
  - **What.** `Lexicon` gains a private slot `_files`, built at construction beside `_sets` and never compared, hashed or pickled: a `_lexicon._ReadingFiles`, one per base, log and choice (`_reading_files`, an `lru_cache`, so equal lexicons share it), hashed by identity. Assemble's two memos, `_languages` and `_part_languages`, take it as a fourth argument, so a hit stays C-level and costs no frame. Its `languages(text, kinds)` answers the default (base `default`, no log, no choice) from the index's rows through `languages_of`, unchanged; a choice with no log from the word's own rows under `choose` (and the strict filter), which is exact since a choice's preference is per word; and a log or a snapshot base from the pool `_built` compiled at construction. Reading the whole pool on every miss was the prototype's first form and cost about 2 ms on the first listed-word parse (`parser_for(locales.PT)`, 0.23 to 2.5 ms), which the per-word path removes. `ParsedName`, `Token` and `Reading` store nothing new, so no `ADDED_IN` marker: a pickled parse keeps the tuple it was parsed with, and an old `Lexicon` or `Parser` pickle builds the slot on load.
  - **What moved, measured over the committed corpora's 6,531 names** (on master `854087be` against this branch, Python 3.11.17), only `languages`: under `pt` 309 names, each narrowing (`de` from `es fr it nl pt` to `pt` in 273, `da`, the one-case `E`, `sr`, `dra`, `santa`); under `cs` 11 (`Mgr.` from `en fr` to `cs`; `st.`, `ml.` and `pan`, read only under the choice, from `()` to `cs`); under `es` 299; under strict `en` 109 (`Ma.` from `en fil` to `en`, `Baron` from `de en fr nl` to `en`, `jr.iii` from `en mul` to `en`); under strict `pt` 337, 21 of them the `Ph. D.` pair going to `()`; with no choice and no log none. A check over nine configurations (the default, `pt`, `cs`, `es`, strict `en`, strict `pt`, the default with an `add` and a `remove`, `pt` over a caller's `add`, and `Lexicon.empty()`) found the per-word paths agreeing with the whole pool's answer on every name. tests/v2/test_parser.py's `test_reading_languages_name_the_default_files_under_a_choice`, which pinned the old answer, is rewritten as `test_reading_languages_follow_the_choice`, beside tests for strict, a caller's log, an alternative and a pickled parser.
  - **Measured.** tests/v2/test_snapshot.py passes unchanged; `uv run python tools/differential/cldr_agreement.py` reports the CLDR locale view and the Wikidata native locale view matching their records (JA not measured, namedivider absent); frames, `uv run python tools/perf/call_count.py --against 854087be`, the reference row 311.48 to 311.72, all of it the first parse of each word's miss (311.0 on both in a second pass), `_CALL_BASELINE` unchanged and the serial benchmark passing; the first listed-word parse is unchanged in time for the default and for `parser_for(locales.PT)`.
- **2026-11-02 #754, the 2.x translation's lifetime (Derek) — THE TRANSLATION OF 2.x SPELLINGS AND PICKLES IS MIGRATION SCAFFOLDING: IT STAYS IN 3.0, A 2.x `Lexicon` PICKLE LOADING WITH A `DeprecationWarning`, AND IS DELETED IN 3.1.** Sets the lifetime item 6 of the 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface entry left unstated: its old spellings and item 5's 2.x pickle share one translation, `_lexicon`'s `_target_readings`, `_inert_dropped` and `_MARKER_READ_WITH`, `_edits_toward`, `_translation` and `Lexicon._changed`, with `_translated_2x` and `_refusal` built on it, about 500 lines grown across 4i-b's review rounds, and nothing a 3.0 caller writes reaches it.
  - **3.0.** A 2.x `Lexicon` state loads as item 5 says and then warns, a `DeprecationWarning` saying it was written by 2.x, that 3.1 will refuse it, and to re-pickle the loaded object under 3.0; it is raised once per `Lexicon` loaded, after the load succeeds, so a `Parser` or locale pack holding one warns once, and a refused state raises without it. `_warn_at_caller` attributes it to the caller's `pickle.loads` line, the C unpickler adding no Python frame between it and `__setstate__`. A pickle written by 3.0, 4i-b's layout included, does not warn. The old-spelling refusals are unchanged: they raise already, with their per-word translation, so no warning is added to them.
  - **3.1.** The translation is deleted (#1003). An old spelling (`add`/`remove` with a set keyword, `Lexicon(...)` or `dataclasses.replace` with one) raises a short fixed `TypeError` naming the field and pointing at docs/migrate.rst's table of kinds, with no per-word call; a set attribute read keeps its fixed `AttributeError`, which never translated. A 2.x `Lexicon` state is refused with `ValueError` saying to re-pickle under 3.0, as 2.7 refused a 2.6 pickle.
  - **Why a minor release.** Neither removal breaks a call that works in 3.0: every old spelling already fails there, and only its message shortens, and a pickle break ships in a minor with a release-log bullet, as 2.6 and 2.7 shipped theirs. A 3.0 release in between gives the one re-pickle that keeps a 2.x pickle.
  - Declined: keeping the translation through 3.x (it costs a reader of `_lexicon` 500 lines for a migration done once per pickle and once per call site); warning on the old spellings too (they raise, and a warning beside a raise says nothing more); deleting it in 3.0 (decided against on 2026-11-02 in the `Lexicon` surface entry: the default and the 2.8 fixtures translate back exactly).
- **2026-11-02 #1007, a kind as a whole (Derek) — `remove(kind=..., every=True)` TAKES OUT EVERY WORD'S READINGS OF A KIND, AND `words(kind=..., **properties)` LISTS A KIND'S WORDS.** Decided in chat with #1007, whose one use with no replacement was naming every word of a kind: customize.rst turned titles off with `remove(*TITLES, *TITLES_AMBIGUOUS, kind="title")`, which made the reader learn that the compiler splits a kind across two sets, and bound given names off with `remove(*BOUND_GIVEN_NAMES, kind="bound_given")`.
  - **The remove.** Written `every=True`, with a kind and no words, and stored as an edit with no words (`remove()` alone still raises, since `Lexicon.empty()` says that). The keyword is explicit because `remove(*words, kind=...)` with an empty `words` is the same call as `remove(kind=...)`: written bare, an empty computed list, the spelling customize.rst taught for the constants, would have taken out the whole kind silently where 2.x and 3.0 before it raised (the PR's review; decided in chat). It applies where the log applies, after the choice of languages: it reaches every word holding a reading of the kind or category, a reading a later choice turns on included (`parser_for(locales.ES, base=Parser(lexicon=Lexicon.default().remove(kind="academic", every=True)))` reads `Lic Juan Pérez` with no title), so it is not a list of the words the base held when it was written. The build's checks run over the words it touched, so a whole remove of `given`, `family` or `name` is refused on the default as the same remove written word by word is, naming the word (`abu`, a bound given particle with no given reading left; `fh`, an agnomen with no name reading). Over the default, the compiled sets equal those of the remove written word by word for every other kind and category, measured over all 35 (2026-11-02); the pools differ only in the readings a file writes off by default, which the whole remove takes too (34 for `title`, `judr`, `lic` and `doctora` among them). The repr's call list keeps it apart from a remove of words of the same kind, which it would otherwise join and name only those words.
  - **The list.** `words(kind=...)` answers in the terms a caller configures: the sorted words holding a reading of a kind or category on in this lexicon, with `properties` as `add` spells them narrowing to the readings carrying them, an unwritten value selecting the readings that do not write the property. Over the default it equals each compiled set or union it replaces: `words(kind="title")` is `TITLES | TITLES_AMBIGUOUS` (816 words), `words(kind="title", addresses="given")` is `GIVEN_NAME_TITLES`, and `particle`, `conjunction`, `bound_given`, `birth_marker` and `suffix` are their sets (2026-11-02). The derived markers (`*_AMBIGUOUS`, `NON_GIVEN_NAME_PARTICLES`) are not properties and have no `words` spelling; `readings_of` answers them per word.
  - **Declined:** a `kinds()` listing or a set-valued return. A sorted tuple is deterministic in a doctest and in a repr, and a caller wanting a set writes `set(...)`.

- **2026-11-02 #1005 (Derek) (its "native-script vocabulary" superseded by 2026-11-07 #1148: name readings included; its criterion bullet's `ms` clause superseded by 2026-11-03 #1005, the mul.toml audit: `ms` moves to `mul.toml` whole, with `miss`) — A STRICT CHOICE READS `mul.toml`, AS IT READS NATIVE-SCRIPT VOCABULARY; `mul.toml` TAKES A WORD THAT HARMS NO LANGUAGE AND WOULD OTHERWISE BE DUPLICATED INTO EVERY LANGUAGE'S FILE: THE DEGREES `phd`, `msc`, `mba`, `mph`, `bsc` AND THE SALUTATIONS `mr`, `mrs` MOVE THERE FROM en.toml; `md` STAYS, A LEADING `Md` ABBREVIATING MUHAMMAD.** SUPERSEDES: in the 2026-10-30 #753, file-strict entry, its verdict's "NOT `mul.toml`", item 2 (`mul.toml` not in a strict choice), item 3's `PhD` and the files-with-no-readings bullet's native-script pool; in the 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j entry, its head's definition, item 4's "every Latin-script reading leaving" and its `UNCHOOSABLE` bullet; the strict clauses of the 2026-11-02 #754 sub-phase 4i-c and 4i-d entries; the 2026-10-20 #751 rule that no credential is in `mul.toml`; and in decisions/phd-merge, the 2026-11-02 #1000 entry's strict `pt` examples, its closing Open answered. Decided in chat on the measurements below, which weighed three shapes: (a) strict always reads `mul.toml`, with words moved there; (b) `mul.toml` stays out of strict and a caller chooses it; (c) words move there but strict reads it only when chosen. No compiled set of the default moves (`tools/vocab/build.py` reports `_compiled.py` current), and over ClinicalTrials.gov's 352,104 distinct officials the default parse moves none; `Reading.languages` of a moved word names `mul` where it named `en`.
  - **The criterion.** A word joins `mul.toml` where it harms no language, reading it in names of any language never breaking one, and where its alternative is a copy in every language's file. A word whose name reading stands in a language's file, or that is written as a name's abbreviation, stays in that file, since a strict choice keeps a moved word's vocabulary reading and drops its name reading where that sits in an unread file, ungating it (the 2026-10-30 entry's derived-marker clause; decision 15's harm); a word whose name readings are all in `mul.toml` with it, as `ing`'s are, keeps its gate under every choice. So `md` stays in en.toml, a leading `Md` abbreviating Muhammad (#853; decided in chat after review, below), and `ms`, being written as South Indian initials (ORCID's India family boxes `Sarulatha MS`, `Radha MS`; PatentsView India 6 inventors, `Rekha MS`); `mx` stays, being the abbreviation for Mexico (decided in chat); and the 48 credentials with a name reading stay: moved in the simulation, they broke 207 more Vietnamese ORCID records (`do` 179, `Tan Tai Do` → family `Tai Do`), 159 Chinese PSC filers (`ma` 103, `meng` 28) and 16 Nepalese (`kc`). `mr` and `mrs` were checked for the same use: no name word and no initials written so in the ORCID, PatentsView and PSC boxes (3.5M records), the dotted `M.R.` already reading as initials.
  - **Why (a).** (1) Leaving `mul.toml` out harmed strict `en` itself: `Dr John Smith` read given `Dr`, and over ctgov strict `en` changed 27,776 of the 46,804 default titles (professor 9,976, dr 6,151, prof 6,055, doctor 4,001), and 1,064 with `mul.toml` read. (2) Under (c), a caller who did not choose `mul` would lose every moved word, strict `en` included: with the six degrees first proposed (`md` among them) moved, strict `en` changed 204,622 of ctgov's 252,110 default suffixes, against 2,324 before this change (`mul.toml` unread, nothing moved) and 302 under (a) as built. (3) `mul` could not be chosen (`_reading_index.selected` refuses it, `UNCHOOSABLE`), so (b) and (c) needed a new spelling besides. What (a) gives up is that strict cannot shed a `mul.toml` word; a caller removes one from the lexicon they build on, as any default reading (`parser_for(pack, base=Parser(lexicon=Lexicon.default().remove("phd", kind="credential")), strict=True)` reads `Ana Souza, PhD` given `PhD`). `mul` stays no language to choose, every choice now keeping its readings.
  - **What it recovers, measured** (2026-11-02, this change over master `c1ceb3b3`, Python 3.11.17, the cached local corpora; the figures for after are the built code's, re-run once `md` was kept in en.toml). ctgov's distinct officials, 252,110 with a suffix by default: strict `pt` changed 223,019 of them before (218,315 reading a suffix word as the given name; `md` 156,060, `phd` 53,957, `msc` 4,254 of the lost words) and 171,535 after (167,694), `md` 156,445 of the words lost; with `md` moved too it was 14,174 (11,537). Its 46,804 default titles changed 33,457 before, 7,721 after. Strict `ko` 171,620 suffixes after. The rest are en.toml credentials and titles left there (`ms` 1,609, `pt` 1,411, `do` 1,327, `mbchb` 1,175, `pharmd` 1,122, `rn` 915; `master` 1,452, `lecturer` 1,000): moving every en.toml credential with no `given` or `family` reading that no other file holds, 602 of them (607 less `abd`, `bca`, `mc`, `ra` and `vd`, counted on `c1ceb3b3`, `ms` among them), with `mr`, `mrs` and `mx`, brought strict `pt` to 3,216, and was not taken, most of those being English-context post-nominals. ORCID, the RFC's 14 countries scored as in the 2026-10-30 entry: strict's breaks against the default move by at most 7 records in every country but NP, where they fall from 37 to 7 (`phd` was in 30 of the 37), the largest of the rest PL (32 → 26) and SK (150 → 145). PSC by nationality, against the default (the set-aside by the default's title words, so the same records are set aside in every column): strict before, after, and strict + `en` after read Polish filers 10.64%, 85.76%, 99.13%, Indian 21.23%, 85.01%, 93.27%, Chinese 43.51%, 82.73%, 99.61%; before, `mr` alone was the parse's given name in 9,253 Polish breaks. PSC is a UK register, English-context data, so strict without `en` is still the wrong choice there; the moved `mr` and `mrs` are what makes it cost a fifth rather than nine tenths.
  - **`md` kept in en.toml (Derek), measured.** Proposed with the six degrees and moved in the PR's first build; an independent review found it fails the criterion above, a leading `Md` abbreviating Muhammad (#853), and that after a family comma a strict choice reading it from `mul.toml` took that given name for a credential: under strict `bn`, `Rahman, Md` read suffix 'Md' and `Karim, Md Abdul` suffix 'Md Abdul', where master's strict `bn` read given 'Md': a strict choice without `en` is the one reader of the word as Muhammad there, the default and every choice reading `en` taking it for the credential (`Rahman, Md` → suffix 'Md'). Decided in chat on that finding: `md` stays, keeping master's readings under every choice. What it costs, by the recipe below: 175,935 distinct names hold the token `md` (ctgov 162,576, ORCID 5,912, FEC 5,612, PSC 1,382, nepal-candidates 316, PatentsView 123, and 14 across five more files), and in the family-comma shape `X, MD ...` 337 of them read given `MD` under strict `bn` (ctgov 132, FEC 196, ORCID 7, PSC 2). Of those, 12 have a name word after `MD`, the shape a Muhammad takes (`KARIM, MD FAZLUL`; FEC 10, PSC 2), though not all are one (`JAFFE, MD NIKKI` is a doctor's), and 325 have nothing, a comma or a listed suffix after it, the shape a doctor's credential takes (`KANG, MD, PhD`, `LAHEY, MD`), though not all are one (`Wahiduzzaman, Md` in ORCID is a Muhammad's); a PSC filer's `Mr, Md. Abu Bokkor Seddeki` reads title `Md.` by its period and is not counted. Every strict choice without `en` reads those 337 and the 156,445 ctgov suffixes above (`John Smith, MD` → given `MD`) so, and every choice reading `en` reads them as the default does. The trade was weighed and `md` kept: shown that keeping it costs hundreds of doctors' names under strict without `en`, against a handful of Muhammads, the maintainer said "Keep it in en.toml". Recompute: every distinct name of each `~/.cache/nameparser-tools/local/*/corpus.jsonl` (a JSON string per line); a name holds `md` where a token, split at whitespace and commas, edge periods stripped, the rest removed and casefolded, is `md`; parsed with `parser_for(locales.BN, strict=True)`, counted where the given name folds to `md`; the family-comma shape is one token before the first comma and `md` the first token after it, split by what follows `md` in that part (nothing, a comma, a word in the default's `suffix_acronyms` or `suffix_words`, or another word).
  - **Prefer is unchanged for the seven.** Prefer never drops `mul.toml`'s readings, so moving a word there keeps it under a plain choice whose file holds another vocabulary reading of it; no file but en.toml held one of the seven, so no plain choice moves. The five other no-name-reading credentials such a file does hold (`abd` ar, `bca` cs, `mc` ga and gd, `ra` de, `vd` nl) were kept out on that ground.
  - **Strict's ungated words** (`_reading_index.strict_ungated`, the reviewed table in tests/v2/test_reading_index.py) are unchanged, the test passing as it stood: the strict side of that comparison moved, gaining mul.toml's words, but the plain choice of each tag read them already, so both sides read them alike and they ungate nothing; check 13 of `tools/vocab/build.py` keeps it so, refusing a mul.toml word with a name reading in another file.
  - **Recompute.** The simulation patches `nameparser._reading_index.strict_kept` in a fresh process per configuration (keeping `mul` rows; retagging a moved word's en.toml vocabulary rows `mul` and leaving its name rows; or dropping them for (c)) and builds each parser with `parser_for(pack, strict=True)`, `pack` a shipped pack or a bare `Locale(code=t, lexicon=Lexicon.empty(), languages={t})`, plus a bare `en` pack for strict + `en`; on the built code, no patch. ORCID and PSC are scored with `local_agreement.read(record, local_agreement.FIELDED[source], parser, local_agreement._title_words(Lexicon.default()))` over each country's or nationality's first 20,000 records in `raw.jsonl` (ORCID leaving out the given boxes opening with a title word, the 2026-10-30 entry's filter); ctgov parses each distinct name of `corpus.jsonl` and compares title and suffix with `Parser()`'s, a word "lost" when it is in the default's suffix (or title), split at spaces and commas, edge periods dropped, periods removed and casefolded, and not in the configuration's. The scripts are in no tree.
- **2026-11-03 #1005, the mul.toml audit (Derek) — `ms` AND `miss` MOVE TO `mul.toml` FROM en.toml, EACH WHOLE: `ms` WITH ITS CREDENTIAL, `miss` WITH ITS FAMILY READING; EVERY OTHER `mul.toml` WORD STAYS, `ing` INCLUDED; NO DEFAULT PARSE MOVES FOR EITHER.** SUPERSEDES: in the 2026-11-02 #1005 entry, the criterion bullet's clause keeping `ms` in en.toml as South Indian initials. Decided in chat on 2026-11-03 ("ms, miss, mba"), on the strict-gap report and the audit below; `mba`'s family reading, decided with them, is decisions/suffix-acronym-collisions' 2026-11-03 entry. No compiled set of the default moves for `ms` or `miss` (`tools/vocab/build.py` reports only `mba` joining `SUFFIX_ACRONYMS_AMBIGUOUS`), and no plain choice moves, en.toml having been the one file holding a reading of either.
  - **What strict lost to them.** The strict-gap report (`tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps`, #1025: each language tag's plain choice against its strict one over the ORCID, PatentsView and PSC records of the countries it is written in, scored against the filed boxes, a broken record laid to the word whose own token moved; 83 tags, the first 20,000 records a group, on 7ce9cbf4) ranked `ms` and `miss` the top strict loss in most tags, nearly all of it PSC, the UK register whose filers write a salutation before their names whatever their nationality, the class `mr` and `mrs` moved for: strict `zh` broke 4,832 records (`ms` 2,852, `miss` 1,025), `fil` 4,899 (`ms` 3,788), `pa` 3,008, `ro` 2,577, `pl` 2,093; summed over tags (a record counted under each tag its country stands under), `ms` broke 38,117 records and fixed 11, `miss` broke 28,537 and fixed 2. PSC's title boxes hold `Ms` 65,690 times across 197 nationalities and `Miss` 71,779 times across 186 (data 2026-10-22). On this change: strict `zh` 965, `fil` 1,066, `pa` 155, `ro` 60, `pl` 70, every tag's `ms` and `miss` breaks 0, and strict `en` 7,362 before and after.
  - **Why each moves whole.** With `ms`'s salutation in `mul.toml` and its credential left in en.toml, a strict choice without `en` read the degree as a title wherever it stands past a comma: 46,838 of the 51,461 ClinicalTrials.gov, FEC, ORCID and PatentsView names holding `ms` or `miss` moved under strict `ko`, `hi`, `pl` and `zh` alike (`A Israni, MD, MS` → title 'MS', where the credential had read suffix 'MS'), and strict `en` moved 1. So `ms` moves with its credential, and every strict choice reads it as the default does. The cost is the initials #1005 kept it for: under a strict choice without `en`, `Radha MS` reads suffix 'MS' as the default does, where it read family 'MS'; ORCID's and PatentsView's India family boxes hold `MS` 135 and 15 times, and 4 given boxes open with `MS` as initials (`MS Venkataramayya`), against the 38,117 records recovered. `miss` moves with its family reading (Census 2010 164) because check 13 of `tools/vocab/build.py` refuses a `mul.toml` word with an on-by-default name reading in another file. The reading derives no gate on a salutation, only a `given` reading moving a title into `TITLES_AMBIGUOUS`: deleting it moved none of 18,618 grid cells (the PR's review, 2026-11-03).
  - **The audit of every other `mul.toml` word, against the 2026-11-02 #1005 criterion** (incidence: tokens of every record, split at whitespace and commas, edge periods stripped, casefolded, also with every period removed; bearers by `tools/name_usage.py --no-wikidata --boxes`). `dr` and `prof` are written in 143 and 54 ORCID countries (3 records or more) and 128 and 13 PSC nationalities; `doctor`, `professor` and `docent` in English-context data from many countries (`docent` by ClinicalTrials.gov's Swedish, Finnish, Czech and Uzbek officials, 79 of its 80 names after a comma), none with a name reading in any file; the numerals and `&` everywhere (FEC `ii` 2,838, `iii` 7,872); `phd` in 75 ORCID countries; the other #1021 words as that entry measured. All stay. Recorded, not acted on: `doctor` is borne as a surname (Census 2010 3,465; PSC `Cyrus Nadir Doctor`, `Maricar Doctor`), which the trailing position reads as a name already, and `doctor` and `professor` as southern African given names (ORCID `Doctor Senamela`, `Professor Khupe`), which the title takes in front under every choice, the default included: a vocabulary-collisions question, not a `mul.toml` one.
  - **`ing` stays.** It is written after a name by Czech, Slovak, Romanian, Austrian, Belgian (`Gunter Reekmans, Ing`), Québécois (`ing.`) and Serbian (`Dipl Ing`) officials and as the pan-European `EUR ING`, and its name readings (Census 2010 1,814, 56% Asian or Pacific Islander; a given name in Malaysia and Singapore and in Sweden) sit in `mul.toml` with it. Moving its credential to cs, sk, de, ro, nl, fr, sr and hr and its names to en and sv was measured under the default, each tag's plain choice and each strict choice (162 configurations) over the 29,097 local-corpus names holding `ing` or `mba`: the default and every plain choice unmoved; those eight languages' strict choices moved 52 names, the credential ungated (`Alvin Ing` → suffix 'Ing'); every other strict choice moved 26, the credential lost (`Daniela Ponce, Ing` → given 'Ing'). Worse both ways.
  - **Recompute.** `tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps` (#1025) on the tree before and after; the `ms` split and the `ing` move as `config_diff.py diff` over the configurations named, with `--readings`, in this tree and in a scratch tree carrying the move, the two files compared line by line; the simulated trees are in no branch.
- **2026-11-03 #1005, the strict-gap batches (Derek) — FIVE BATCHES OF WORDS A STRICT CHOICE LOST, EACH ITS OWN CHANGE: `jr`, `junior` AND `jnr` MOVE TO `mul.toml` (BATCH A, THIS ENTRY, WHICH MOVES NO DEFAULT OR PLAIN-CHOICE PARSE); fil.toml TAKES `de`, `del`, `san`, `la` AND `los`; ca.toml `de`, `del` AND `la`; si.toml `de`; ar.toml A `given` READING OF `sultan`, EACH MEASURED IN ITS OWN ENTRY.** Decided in chat on 2026-11-03 ("All five, here in order"), on the re-ranked strict-gap report (`tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps` on master f48c88a3, a broken record laid to the word whose own token moved; saved with its simulation in the session notes, not in a tree). Each batch is its own PR, measured on master; this entry records the decision and each batch's measurement as it lands, beside the change.
  - **`jr`, `junior`, `jnr` → `mul.toml`.** Written after names in every country's data, generations with no name reading in any file, so check 13 admits them: strict choices broke 1,036 records on `jr` across 15 languages' countries (fil 408, pt-BR 183, de 48, fr 48, zh 36, ta 36, ms 34, es 27), 246 on `junior` (pt-BR 153, pt 25, es 18) and 13 on `jnr` (ak), and every one falls to 0 with the move (simulated). It costs the records where a holder typed `Junior` into the family box or as a given name and strict, reading it as a name, agreed: pt-BR 57, fr 11, es 5, pt 3, fil 1, ak 1 (the review's re-run, 2026-11-03); and a given name `Junior` after a family comma reads as the generation, as the default reads it, with a report (`Silva, Junior João` under strict `pt-BR` → family 'Silva', suffix 'Junior João', suffix-or-name, where strict read given 'Junior'). On the change, over a grid of 21 shapes (each comma shape, cased, upper, lower and dotted spellings, a credential behind it) under the three orders: the default and the plain `pt-BR`, `fil` and `en` choices move no cell, nor strict `en`; strict `pt-BR`, `fil`, `ko` and `de` move 57 cells each, every one onto the default's reading of the generation (`Silva, Junior` → suffix 'Junior', where strict read given 'Junior'), save the parts of those names another file's word decides (`MD`'s report, en's `do`, pt's `da` and `de`). `junior` is also a given name (ORCID's given boxes 480 times, Brazil, Ghana and Cameroon most), which no file records: a vocabulary-collisions question, not a `mul.toml` one.
  - **Recompute.** `tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps` on the tree before and after (`gaps fil pt-BR de fr zh ta ms es pt ak` reaches every row above); the grid is the 21 names `Paulo Silva Junior`, `Paulo Silva Jr`, `Paulo Silva Jr.`, `Paulo Silva, Jr.`, `Silva, Paulo, Jr.`, `Silva Jr, Paulo`, `Silva Jr., Paulo`, `, Jr.`, `Silva, Jr.`, `Junior Silva`, `Silva, Junior`, `Paulo Junior Silva`, `PAULO SILVA JUNIOR`, `paulo silva junior`, `Paulo Silva JNR`, `Paulo Silva Jnr.`, `Paulo Silva Jr., MD`, `Dr. Paulo Silva Jr`, `Silva, Paulo Jr`, `Paulo de Souza Junior` and `Jr`, each parsed in both trees under the three orders and `default`, `plain:PT_BR`, `plain:fil`, `plain:en`, `strict:PT_BR`, `strict:fil`, `strict:en`, `strict:ko` and `strict:de` (`config_diff.build` names each), and the cells compared; the review's own grid of 10 spellings by 21 shapes over 15 plain and 17 strict choices moved 0 default, plain or strict `en` cells and 438 strict ones, every one onto the default's reading save another file's word.
- **2026-11-03 #1005, strict-gap batch B, fil (Derek) — fil.toml TAKES THE SPANISH-DERIVED PARTICLES OF FILIPINO SURNAMES, `de`, `del`, `san`, `la` AND `los`; NO DEFAULT OR PLAIN-CHOICE PARSE MOVES; UNDER STRICT `fil`, `del`, `la` AND `san` READ AS NEVER-GIVEN.** One of the five strict-gap batches decided in chat on 2026-11-03 ("All five, here in order"), on the re-ranked report (`tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps` on master f48c88a3; the batches' own entry is the 2026-11-03 #1005, the strict-gap batches entry). fil.toml held `dela` alone of these, so strict `fil` read every other one as a name word: it broke 473 records on `de`, 94 on `del`, 70 on `san`, 46 on `la` and 19 on `los` (`Helbert De La Cruz`, `Joshua Del Prado`, `Raul San Diego`, Philippine ORCID, PatentsView and PSC records), and with batch A's `jr` the language's 1,066 broken records fall to 28 (simulated with all five batches). Each entry copies es.toml's kind and properties (`de` and `los` state `may_be_given = false`, no file holding a given reading of either).
  - **What moves.** The compiled default does not (`tools/vocab/build.py` reports `_compiled.py` current). Over a grid of 21 Filipino names (each comma shape, cased, upper and lower spellings, a title and a generation beside them) under the three orders, the default and plain `fil` move no cell; strict `fil` and strict `fil` + `en` move 50 each, toward the default's reading save one class: `del`, `la` and `san` hold a `given` reading in es.toml alone, which strict `fil` drops while it keeps fil.toml's particle reading, so there they read as never-given particles, and a bare `Del Rosario` reads family 'Del Rosario' where the default reads given 'Del' and reports particle-or-given. That reads the Filipino surname as the surname it is; `_reading_index.strict_ungated`'s reviewed table gains `fil: del, la, san`, as it holds `it`'s for the same three. The same class after a family comma loses the given name: `Santos, Del` reads family 'Santos Del' where the default and plain `fil` read given 'Del', as the default already reads `Santos, De`; 26 names of a 1-in-4 sample of the local corpora take that shape (`BITTNER, DEL`, mostly FEC). On Philippine records the class costs 2 (`La Reina Eunice Santos` and `La Trang` read all-family, `la` broke 2 under strict `fil` on the change), `del` and `san` none (the review's run, 2026-11-03). Not added: `las` (`Las Piñas`, 3 Philippine records under strict `fil`), left for a later batch. A plain choice reads `Reading.languages` of `de` and `la` with `fil` among the files (the default too; plain `fil` names `fil` alone).
  - **Recompute.** `tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps fil` on the tree before and after; the grid is the 21 names `Juan De La Cruz`, `Juan dela Cruz`, `Maria Del Rosario`, `Jose San Juan`, `Juan De Los Santos`, `De La Cruz, Juan`, `Del Rosario, Maria Luisa`, `, De La Cruz`, `Cruz, Juan de la`, `JUAN DE LA CRUZ`, `juan de la cruz`, `Juan De Guzman Jr.`, `Del Rosario`, `La Cruz`, `San Juan`, `Juan Del`, `Maria La Paz Reyes`, `Dr. Jose De Leon`, `Del Pilar Santos`, `Juan Santos, De La` and `Ma. Teresa De Los Reyes`, each parsed in both trees under the three orders and `default`, `plain:fil`, `strict:fil` and `strict:fil+en` (`config_diff.build` names each), and the cells compared; the review's own grid of 39 names agreed in direction, and `config_diff.py diff default plain:fil --local all --match '(?i)\b(de|del|la|san|los)\b' --sample 4` read 0 of 61,947 names differently in either tree.
- **2026-11-03 #1005, strict-gap batch C, ca (Derek) — ca.toml TAKES THE CATALAN PARTICLES `de`, `del` AND `la`; NO DEFAULT OR PLAIN-CHOICE PARSE MOVES; UNDER STRICT `ca`, `del` AND `la` READ AS NEVER-GIVEN.** One of the five strict-gap batches decided in chat on 2026-11-03 ("All five, here in order"), on the re-ranked report (`tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps` on master f48c88a3; the batches' own entry is the 2026-11-03 #1005, the strict-gap batches entry). ca.toml held the conjunction `i` alone, so strict `ca` read Catalan names' particles (`de la Torre`, `del Castell`) as name words: over the records of Spain and Andorra it broke 626 records on `de`, 208 on `del` and 197 on `la`, and the language's 1,032 broken records fall to 198 on this change alone (188 with batch A's `jr`). It also loses fixes, 182 to 26: 160 records (PatentsView ES 71, ORCID ES 61, PSC Spanish 28), nearly all Spanish names whose filer boxed a devotional or particle element with the given name (`María del Carmen García`, given box 'María del Carmen'), which strict `ca` now reads as plain `ca` and the default do, having agreed with the boxes only by lacking the particles; the review found no Catalan name read worse than by the default (2026-11-03). Each entry copies es.toml's kind and properties (`de` states `may_be_given = false`).
  - **What moves.** The compiled default does not (`tools/vocab/build.py` reports `_compiled.py` current). Over a grid of 16 Catalan names (each comma shape, cased, upper and lower spellings, `i` joining two surnames, a title and a credential beside them) under the three orders, the default, plain `ca` and strict `ca` + `es` move no cell; strict `ca` moves 35, toward the default's reading save the class batch B met: `del` and `la` hold a `given` reading in es.toml alone, so under strict `ca` they read as never-given particles (`Del Castell` → family 'Del Castell', where the default reads given 'Del' and reports particle-or-given), and `_reading_index.strict_ungated`'s reviewed table gains `ca: del, la`. The same class after a family comma loses the given name (`Castell, Del` → family 'Castell Del', `Torre, La` → family 'Torre La', where the default reads given 'Del' and 'La'), as the default already reads `la Torre, De`. A plain choice reads `Reading.languages` of `de` and `la` with `ca` among the files (the default too; plain `ca` names `ca` alone); under plain `ca` es.toml's `given` readings of `del` and `la` survive the preference, so `Del Shannon` still reports particle-or-given. Not added: the Catalan plural `dels` (`Pere dels Arcs` reads middle 'dels' under every choice, the default included; 2 names in the local corpora), no file holding it, so the report could not rank it.
  - **Recompute.** `tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps ca` on the tree before and after; the grid is the 16 names `Jordi de la Torre`, `Joan del Castell`, `Montserrat Puig i Vila`, `de la Torre, Jordi`, `Del Castell, Joan`, `Puig, Joan de`, `, de la Torre`, `JORDI DE LA TORRE`, `jordi de la torre`, `Del Castell`, `La Torre`, `Maria de Lourdes Puig`, `Joan Miró i Ferrà`, `Dr. Jordi de la Torre`, `Anna de Puig, PhD` and `Josep Maria de Sagarra`, each parsed in both trees under the three orders and `default`, `plain:ca`, `strict:ca` and `strict:ca+es` (`config_diff.build` names each), and the cells compared; the review's own grid of 3,636 cells agreed (default, plain `ca` and strict `ca` + `es` unmoved), and `config_diff.py diff default plain:ca --local all --match '(?i)\b(de|del|la)\b' --sample 4 --readings` wrote byte-identical readings in both trees over 61,582 names.

  - **`delos`.** `Delos Santos` and `Delos Reyes` write `de los` as one word, as `Dela Cruz` writes `de la`, and every configuration read it as a name word (`Juan Delos Reyes` → middle 'Delos', family 'Reyes', 1.4.0 and 2.8.1 alike). It is also a given name (US SSA 1,324 male births, Wikidata 44, 27 of them US citizens, `Delos Wheeler`), so it takes a `given` reading beside the `preposition` one and is a particle that may be given, as `dela` is: `Juan Delos Reyes` → family 'Delos Reyes', `Delos Wheeler` → given 'Delos' with particle-or-given reported. Over the 18 names of the grid below that hold `Delos` (each comma shape, cased, upper and lower spellings, a title, a generation, an initial), under the five configurations and three orders, `Delos` reads cell for cell as `Dela` does (0 of 270 cells apart).

  - **The masks.** A particle is lowercased by case repair unless its word carries a `cased` mask (rules/R4), so `delos` turned particle would have repaired `JUAN DELOS REYES` to `Juan delos Reyes`, where the default, reading it as a name word, gave `Delos`; and `dela` already repaired to `Juan dela Cruz`. ORCID's 2025 Philippine family boxes write `Dela` 352 times, `dela` 60 and `DELA` 59, and `Delos` 93, `delos` 17 and `DELOS` 15, so each word takes its title case as its mask: `capitalized()` gives `Juan Dela Cruz` and `Juan Delos Reyes`, and a name written `Juan dela Cruz` keeps its own case unless forced. One committed corpus entry moves, `JUAN DELA CRUZ`, in its case view alone.
  - **What moves,** measured 2026-11-03 on master 39839b91 against this change (`tools/differential/measure.py --base`, the fielded scores with `local_agreement.py SOURCE --base --list`). Committed corpora: none in a role. Fielded: ORCID 40 records fixed, none broken; PSC 27 fixed, 1 broken; PatentsView 6 fixed, 5 broken. Every fix is a Filipino surname joining its particle (`Mr Archie Delos Santos` → family 'Delos Santos', as filed). Four of the six breaks, all PatentsView, are a maternal surname written before the family name in Philippine usage and typed in the given box (`Herbert delos Santos Naval`, filed family 'Naval'), which the particle run now carries into the family, as rules/P2 reads `Dela` already (`Maria Dela Cruz Santos` → family 'Dela Cruz Santos'); the other two, PatentsView's fifth and PSC's one, are the surname cut across two boxes, the particle typed in the given or middle box (`Randy John Delos Santos`, filed given 'Randy John Delos', family 'Santos'; `Mrs Leonida Delos Santos`, filed middle 'Delos'). The local snapshots move the same class in ctgov-officials (2, `Rowena Delos Santos` and the same with `, MD`) and jrc-names (1, `Kian Delos Santos`), each to family 'Delos Santos'. FEC: 3 names after a family comma whose given part ends in `Delos`, an American middle name (`REYNOLDS, FREDERICK DELOS` → family 'DELOS REYNOLDS', reported particle-or-given), the shape `Cruz, Juan Dela` already reads, a cost rules/P6's Accepted bullet on an ambiguous particle covers. The masks move case views alone. Unforced, the source's capitals are repaired to `Dela` and `Delos` where the particle was lowercased (`ANGELA DELA CRUZ` → `Angela Dela Cruz`): tse-donors 41 (Brazilian surnames of Italian origin, `DELA COSTA`), fec-individuals 18, orcid-researchers 12. Forced, psc-individuals 168, orcid-researchers 161, patentsview-inventors 37, ctgov-officials 4 and jrc-names 2, mostly the same repair of a source in one case, but a writer's own lowercase particle is now forced up too, where forcing had kept it (orcid-researchers 25 of the 161, `Amelyn dela Peña` → `Amelyn Dela Peña`; patentsview-inventors 7 of 37; ctgov-officials 1 of 4), and a few `DeLa` become `Dela`: the cost of a mask under `force=True`, which writes the mask over whatever case the source used (`snapshot.py --local SOURCE --base`, rerun after the masks). `config_diff.py gaps fil` (plain against strict `fil`): ORCID PH agreement 95.18% → 95.35% plain and 95.24% → 95.42% strict, PSC Filipino 98.73% → 98.96% plain, the 28 strict-only breaks falling to 25, `las` leaving the list.
  - **Recompute.** `tools/differential/measure.py --base <checkout of 39839b91>`; `local_agreement.py orcid-researchers|psc-individuals|patentsview-inventors --base <that checkout> --list 50`; `config_diff.py gaps fil` in both trees; the case counts over ORCID's whole extract (`ORCID_2025_10_summaries.v1.jsonl.gz`), the words of each Philippine record's `family` matched against `dela` and `delos` without regard to case and tallied as written; the grid is the 28 names `Juan Delos Reyes`, `Ma. Luisa Delos Santos`, `Delos Wheeler`, `Delos Reyes, Juan`, `Reyes, Juan Delos`, `, Delos Reyes`, `Delos Reyes`, `Juan Delos`, `JUAN DELOS REYES`, `juan delos reyes`, `Juan Delos Reyes Jr.`, `Dr. Juan Delos Reyes`, `Delos Santos, Ma. Luisa`, `Wheeler, Delos`, `Delos`, `Juan Santos, Delos`, `Delos A. Wheeler`, `Juan Delos Santos Cruz`, `Maria Las Piñas`, `Las Piñas, Maria`, `Las Piñas`, `Maria Las`, `Juan de las Casas`, `MARIA LAS PIÑAS`, `maria las piñas`, `Maria de Las Piñas Cruz`, `Cruz, Maria Las`, `Juan Dela Cruz`, each parsed by `config_diff._parser` under `default`, `plain:fil`, `strict:fil`, `strict:fil+en` and `plain:es` and the three orders, in both trees for the moves, and in this tree with `Delos` respelled `Dela` for the cell-for-cell claim, over the 18 names holding `Delos` (the review's own 28 shapes found 0 of 420 cells apart on the tree, 277 on master).
- **2026-11-03 #1038 (Derek) — `mister` AND `madam` MOVE TO `mul.toml` FROM en.toml, `mister` WITH ITS FAMILY READING; `sir`, `director` AND `ceo` STAY IN en.toml; NO ROLE MOVES UNDER THE DEFAULT OR A PLAIN CHOICE.** Decided in chat on 2026-11-03 ("madam + mister only"), on the strict-gap report (`tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps` on master f48c88a3, as #1038 cites it). The five words were in en.toml alone, so a strict choice without `en` read each as the given name in front of a name (`Director John Smith` → given 'Director' under strict `fr`), nearly all of it in the UK register of people with significant control, whose filers write an English title before names of every nationality. `mister` and `madam` are forms of address, the class `mr`, `mrs`, `ms` and `miss` moved for (the 2026-11-02 and 2026-11-03 #1005 entries), and every strict choice reads them now. `sir` is a British honour rather than a form of address and is borne as a given name (US SSA 2,172 births); `director` and `ceo` are job titles a filer typed into a title box. A strict choice of another language leaving those three is the intended effect of choosing it, and `Lexicon.add` puts them back for a caller who wants them.
  - **What moves.** The compiled default does not (`tools/vocab/build.py` reports `_compiled.py` current, the reading index alone rewritten), and en.toml was the one file holding a reading of either word, so no plain choice moves a role; the title's `Reading.languages` names `mul` where it named `en`, under the default and every choice, as the 2026-11-02 #1005 entry records for its words. `mister` moves with its family reading (Census 2010 1,211) and its refused `after_given`, since check 13 of `tools/vocab/build.py` refuses a `mul.toml` word whose name reading on by default stands in another file, as for `miss`; `madam` has no name population (US Census below 100, US SSA below 5 a year, no local-corpus record writing it as a name). On master 39839b91 against this change, over the 83 language tags: the records strict broke fall from 29,879 to 29,681 and the records it fixed stay 9,150; `madam`'s and `mister`'s breaks, 106 and 93 summed over tags (a record counted under each tag its country stands under), fall to 0, and no word breaks in their place. By tag, strict `fr` 4,480 → 4,407, `ar` 375 → 351, `de` 6,249 → 6,229, `it` 479 → 468; strict `en` 7,371 before and after.
  - **Recompute.** `tools/differential/config_diff.py gaps --json` on the tree before and after, each tag's `broke` and `fixed` summed over its result's `groups`, and the two words' rows read from its `words`.

- **2026-11-03 #658 — THE BRETON `ar` IS `br.toml`'s, OFF BY DEFAULT; `M.` IS EXCLUDED; NO FRENCH PACK; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE FROM THESE TWO.** Two of #658's three words; the third, `fils`, the 2026-10-25 #752 entry decided. A `locales.FR` pack turning on `père` was built and dropped in the same change: `père` is read by default, gated as `fils` is (decisions/generational-words' 2026-11-03 #684 entry, decided in chat), so a choice of `fr` changes no word (`_reading_index.changed_words(selected(["fr"]))` is empty), and a vocabulary pack must change one. The choice of `fr` stays what the phase 3f entry's remedy names for a Czech caller who keeps the English Saint.
  - **`ar`, the Breton article (#658), is a reading off by default in a new `br.toml`.** It is right in Breton (`Katell ar Gall`) and wrong as a default: read as a particle over every local corpus and the committed corpora (`config_diff.py diff default 'expr:Parser(lexicon=Lexicon.default().add("ar", kind="article"))' --local all --committed --match '(?i)\bar\b'`, 2026-11-03), it moved 84 of the 202 names holding the word, none Breton, counted by where the word stands: 56 inside the name, paired initials or the Arabic article taken into the family behind them (`Alessandro AR Rambaldi`, `Abd Al-Rahman AR Awad`); 13 opening it (`AR Bhatti`); 7 the whole given part after a family comma (`FOOTE, AR`); 7 ending a part: 5 comma-less Brazilian filings whose family shrinks to the word (`GECILDA DE FATIMA FISCHBORN AR` family 'DE FATIMA FISCHBORN AR' → 'AR') and 2 after a family comma, whose given part's last word joins the family (`BREEN, MARY AR` family 'BREEN' → 'AR BREEN'); and 1 dotted (`Salma AR. Mohamed`). So it is not French and not the default: a `br` choice reads it (`parser_for(Locale(code="br", …, languages=frozenset({"br"})))`), and no pack makes that choice, no name data source measured holding a Breton name. `tools/differential/config_diff.py` lists `br` in `NO_COUNTRY`, not under France, so a French run of its gap report does not count the particle as the French choice's cost. It states `may_be_given = false`, the build's requirement for a particle with no given reading. Its counts are on its note.
  - **`M.` (Monsieur) is excluded, a French choice included.** A single letter written as an initial is (rules/T5), and in names nearly always one: 782 ORCID records and 168 PatentsView inventors open with `M.` before two or more name words (2026-11-03), each an initial by the box it was filed in, and no local corpus writes the salutation; CLDR's `M. Jean-Nicolas … de Bouchart fils` is the one, a fictional rendering. A reading would take the initial of every `M. Smith`, by default or under a choice of `fr`. Like es.toml's `h` (the phase 3g entry), no writing tells the two apart. The reason is on fr.toml's `m`.
  - **What moves.** Nothing under the default from these two: `snapshot.py` matches the committed record. fr.toml's academic titles, which do move the default, are decisions/salutation-titles' 2026-11-03 #804 entry, and `père`, decisions/generational-words'.
  - **Recompute.** The `ar` counts by the `config_diff.py` call above, the 84 classified by the position of the word in the name; the `M.` counts by a count of the names in each local corpus's `corpus.jsonl` whose first word, or the first word after a comma, is `M.`, by the number of words behind it.

Under the pack `Maestra Ana Ruiz Paz` reads title 'Maestra', given 'Ana', family 'Ruiz Paz'. `maestro` stays excluded as phase 3g decided (a Spanish family name, ORCID's family box 43 records in Spain): #980 asked whether a title-phrase-only reading would serve both, and it would not, the DOF writing the bare form, and a title-phrase gate (rules/H9) is derived from a `given` reading, which neither word has. Maestra Olga Santillán Rodríguez` the parse reads `C.` as an initial, the given name, and `Maestra` stays a middle name).
