- **2026-11-05 #1079 — THE FOUR NAMES PHASE 4 WAS TO USE STAY RESERVED, UNBUILT, FOR 3.x, AND THEIR BUILD MESSAGE NAMES THE RELEASE RATHER THAN A PHASE.** 3.0 ships without phase 4's position and bare-letter properties and its glued words, so `bare_only`, `leading_only`, `trailing_only` and `separators` stay build errors, which now say each is "reserved, unbuilt, for 3.x" (`tools/vocab/build.py`'s `RESERVED`), where the 2026-10-25 #752, phase 2h entry had them name phase 4. nameparser/vocab/README.md's table of them follows, and tests/v2/test_vocab.py holds the two equal. Decided in chat during the 3.0 release sweep.
- **2026-11-05 #1085 — ASSEMBLE'S LANGUAGE LOOKUP IS ONE MEMO, `_languages_of`; NO PARSE MOVES.** The output side's simplify pass folded `_languages` and its twin `_part_languages`, then each a memo, into one `lru_cache(maxsize=4096)`, `_languages_of`, keyed as they were on the word as written, the kind, the field and the lexicon's `_files`; the 2026-10-29 follow-up's Cost item counts `_languages` as the first of its two memos (the memo over `Reading` is the second), and the 2026-11-02 4i-d entry names the pair assemble's two memos. It asks `_languages` and, where that finds nothing, `_part_languages`, both now plain functions, and answers with the files and whether they are the word's parts', which the caller still drops where `_listed_whole` finds the parse's own lexicon listing the word whole, the one check the memo cannot answer. A hit stays C-level, so a word read before costs no frame; `_forms` and the memo over `Reading` are unchanged. What the pass saved per parse is in decisions/parse-cost's 2026-11-05 entry.
- **2026-11-05 #1108 (its `default = false` on `ths`, `đbqh` and `dbqh` superseded by the 2026-11-06 #1115, the pack titles entry; `ts`, `gs` and `pgs` stay the choice's, written as initials too; its accepted stacked title ending a name, given 'PGS.TS.', superseded by decisions/H5's 2026-11-06 #1116 entry, which reads it as the title; its accepted lone name word read as the family under the pack, `Anh` → family 'Anh', superseded by decisions/O5's 2026-11-06 #1111 entry: a lone word reads given under every order, the pack's included) — `locales.VI`: A PLAIN CHOICE OF `vi`, vi.toml'S `[rules]` ORDER (`name_order = "family-first-given-last"`), AND A LEXICON FRAGMENT THAT REMOVES THE READINGS OTHER FILES GIVE THE SYLLABLES VIETNAMESE NAMES ARE WRITTEN WITH: PARTICLE (`van`, `le`, `do`, `du`, `di`, `la`, `tho`, `da`, `san`), CONNECTIVE (`the`, `y`) AND CREDENTIAL (`do`, `ba`, `chi`); THE ONE PACK FRAGMENT THAT REMOVES; NO STRICT MODE; THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MOVE.** Decided in chat on 2026-11-05 ("i agree with route A"; the word list "all as recommended"; the credentials "yes, send that", and `ma` left out, confirmed in chat), over (B) a pack of the order and titles alone, documented with `strict=True`, and (C) a `strict` field on `Locale`. #1108 proposed `given` readings of `văn`/`van` and `thị`/`thi` in vi.toml, on the premise that a reading turned on also prefers. It does not: `choose` prefers only by a vocabulary reading, and a name reading never settles (decision 15), so under a plain choice `Nguyen Van Anh` read given 'Van Anh', the Dutch particle chaining `Van` into the given name, exactly as the order alone reads it. No kind a file can write would make `van` a name word, so the settling is done by the pack's fragment, whose `remove` calls apply after the choice as a caller's own do. It bends decisions 6 and 15, which bind the default and the packs, for this pack alone, on the ground that its order commits a caller to Vietnamese names: a name written without a comma that the removal could misread (`Ludwig van Beethoven`) is misread by the order first. A family comma fixes the order, and the removals still act there, a cost the pack adds on its own, accepted below. `mechanisms.md#LOCALE-PACKS-PURE-DATA`'s contract names the exception. No compiled set of the default moves (`tools/vocab/build.py` reports `_compiled.py` current), and `snapshot.jsonl` is unchanged.
  - **The measurement**, on the local corpora #1110 added (`tools/differential/local_uit.py`, UIT-ViNames; `tools/differential/local_vnassembly.py`, the National Assembly deputies), each record's parts derived from its order (the first word the family, the last the given, the words between the middle), measured 2026-11-05 and re-taken on the commit adding the credential removal, which moved none of them (default / the order alone / VI / strict VI): UIT-ViNames, 21,090 names written with diacritics, 13 / 21,065 / 21,085 / 21,089; the same names with the marks stripped (đ as d), standing in for romanized writing, 89 / 19,221 / 21,082 / 21,089 (the order to VI +1,861/−0); the deputies, 499 with the titles composed from their education, 0 / 483 / 499 / 499, stripped 0 / 404 / 499 / 499 (the order to VI +16, the stacked `PGS.TS.` and `GS.TS.`, and +95 stripped). The Vietnamese nationals of `psc-individuals`, filed in Western order, are the contrast: 1,097 of 1,494 by default and 1 under the order, the pack adding no loss beyond the order's. Per word, the records VI loses with the word left out of the fragment (UIT stripped / deputies stripped): `van` 1,221 / 74, `le` 398 / 2, `the` 163 / 3, `do` 40 / 0, `du` 15, `da` 11, `y` 10, `di` 9, `la` 3, `san` 3 (and 3 written with diacritics), `tho` 2 / 1; with diacritics only `du` 7, `di` 4, `la` 3, `san` 3, `da` 2 and `the` 1 fire. `mac`, in the first list, moved no Vietnamese-order name in any corpus (2 PSC Western-order names, losing nothing) and was dropped; `san`, the saint particle chaining a given name (`… San Hà`), was added (+3/−0 either way). Earlier and not re-taken: ORCID's Vietnam records composed family-first, 43,790 with both boxes, counting a middle the filer put in the family box as agreeing, read 89.95% under the order, 94.72% with the first fragment (`mac` in, `san` out), 95.43% strict.
  - **The credentials**, removed after the code review found `NGUYEN VAN DO` reading suffix 'DO' under the pack: removing `van`'s particle had left en.toml's credential `DO` to take the given name of a name in one case, and `BA` and `CHI` did so by default. The comparator is VI with the credential readings kept. GAINED: the removal moves a role only in a name written in capitals, the syllable read as the given name where the credential took it: UIT-ViNames stripped and in capitals 108 names (the recipe prints 109 moved, one moving only its report, `KA BA`) (`chi` 82, `do` 20, `ba` 6), in capitals with its diacritics 68 (`chi` 65, `ba` 3), the deputies in capitals 4 (`chi` 2, `ba` 2), every one a gain by the corpora's own parts, no loss; as written and stripped in mixed case it moves only the `suffix-or-name` report (69 UIT names and 4 deputies' names as written, 110 and 4 stripped). FEC's donors with a common Vietnamese surname gain too, a given name the default reads as a credential: `chi` 5 (`NGUYEN, VY CHI` → middle 'CHI'), `ba` 2. COST: among names with a common Vietnamese surname (1,200 in ctgov-officials, 3,294 in FEC), a real credential stops being a suffix in `do` 4 (`Ryan Nguyen, DO`, `Thanh D Hoang, DO`, `Thao L Nguyen, DO, FAAP`; FEC `NGUYEN, MINH QUANG LCDR DO, MPH,`) and `ba` 1 (`Phuong T Vo, BA`), `chi` none. Every ctgov one is written given-first for English-language readers, which VI misreads with the credential kept (`Ryan Nguyen, DO` → family 'Ryan') and the default reads right. Measured 2026-11-05. Recompute the gains with `uv run python tools/differential/config_diff.py diff 'expr:parser_for(Locale(code="vi", lexicon=Lexicon.empty().remove("da","di","do","du","la","le","san","tho","van", kind="particle").remove("the","y", kind="conjunction"), policy=locales.VI.policy, languages=locales.VI.languages))' plain:VI --local uit-vinames --spelling stripped,upper` (`--spelling` added for this; `upper` alone, or `--local vn-assembly`, for the other rows); the costs by the same two configurations over the ctgov and FEC names holding one of `STRONG`'s surnames (the session notes' `credcost.py`, not in a tree).
  - **Accepted for the credentials:** `DO` or `BA` written as a credential after a Vietnamese name reads as a name word, with a comma or without, dotted (reported `suffix-or-name`), in brackets or behind another credential (`Nguyen Van Anh, DO` → family 'Nguyen Van Anh', given 'DO'; `Nguyen Van Anh DO`, `Nguyen Van Anh D.O.` → given 'DO'; `Nguyen Van Anh (DO)` → nickname 'DO'; `Nguyen Van Anh MD, DO` → given 'DO'); past a second comma it stays a suffix (`Nguyen, Van Anh, DO`). `CHI` reads so without a comma (`Nguyen Van Anh CHI` → given 'CHI'); in the part right after one, the capitals shape (rules/C6) takes an unlisted word of three letters or more in capitals as a suffix behind two name words or more (C4) where the name's case contrasts, so `Nguyen Van Anh, CHI` keeps suffix 'CHI', reported, while a name written wholly in capitals gives the shape nothing to contrast with (`NGUYEN VAN ANH, CHI` → given 'CHI', unreported), and one name word before the comma leaves it nothing to spare (`Anh, CHI` → given 'CHI'); past a second comma it stays a suffix in any case, as `DO` does (rules/C2: `NGUYEN, VAN ANH, CHI` → suffix 'CHI'). (Corrected 2026-11-07: the entry said the shape takes the word whatever its readings, after any comma; it takes `CHI` only because the pack removes its credential reading, and only where the name's case contrasts, as C6 states.) Left out: `ma`, which gained no corpus name and cost one (`Linda Trinh, MA`), so `TRAN THI MA` stays a known gap (suffix 'MA'). Not removable: `VI`, a given name read as the numeral by its shape (`NGUYEN THI VI`). Declined for 3.0: keeping the credential readings and declining only the bare word ending the core of a one-case name, which would keep the comma costs away but loses FEC's family-comma gains, and needs a new `Policy` field set from vi.toml's `[rules]` (packs carry no code, and `leading_only`/`trailing_only` are reserved, unbuilt, for 3.x), amending rules/S4 (an ambiguous acronym needs words to spare) at 3.0 final.
  - **Accepted for comma names:** after a family comma, where the order reads nothing differently, the removals still act, so a particle or a credential there reads as a name word: `Beethoven, Ludwig van` → family 'Beethoven', middle 'van', where the default gives family 'van Beethoven'; `Smith, John Do` → middle 'Do'. UIT-ViNames and the deputies hold no comma names, so no corpus measures it.
  - **Recompute the four-parser table** with `local_agreement.read` over the uit-vinames and vn-assembly records under `Parser()`, `Parser(policy=Policy(name_order=FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST))`, `parser_for(locales.VI)` and `parser_for(locales.VI, strict=True)` (the session notes' `vi_measure.py`, not in a tree; `local_agreement.py --locale vi --base` scores the base under the pack too, so it cannot give the default against it); `config_diff.py diff default plain:VI --local uit-vinames`, with `--spelling stripped` for the romanized view, gives the moves, and an `expr:` fragment leaving one word out each word's.
  - **The titles.** vi.toml turns on, `default = false`, the academic titles written before a name (`ts`, `ths`, `gs`, `pgs`) and the office `đbqh` (đại biểu Quốc hội, National Assembly deputy) with its ASCII spelling `dbqh`. A dotted title is one at the head by shape already (rules/H2), so a reading is what reads the bare form (`Ts Nguyen Van Anh`) and the stacked one (`PGS.TS.`, each chunk a listed title, `PGS.TS.BS.` included, `bs` being en.toml's credential): the deputies' composed titles are ThS. 241, TS. 142, PGS.TS. 9 and GS.TS. 7, and the 16 stacked ones are exactly what the order alone misses. None of the four has a Vietnamese bearer (tools/name_usage.py, the counts on each word). `đbqh` is in none of the three corpora; CLDR 48's vi file writes it before a surname-first name (`ĐBQH Nguyễn Văn Anh`, which now reads family 'Nguyễn'). The office makes title phrases with `chief` and `deputy`, which attach to every office, so both join the choice's derived surface; neither reads differently. Accepted: a stacked title ending a name reads as the given name (`Nguyen Van Anh PGS.TS.` → given 'PGS.TS.'), where the default reads suffix 'PGS.TS.' by rules/S3's unlisted dotted suffix, which no longer takes it once `pgs` and `ts` are listed, and rules/H5 takes only single words; no local or committed corpus writes one trailing (all 16 `PGS.TS.` and `GS.TS.` lead, in vn-assembly, measured 2026-11-05), and keeping the readings to the leading position waits for `leading_only`, reserved for 3.x. Declined: `bs` (bác sĩ, physician), whose title reading, preferred under the choice, evicted en.toml's credential `BS`, so `Smith, John BS` read middle 'BS'; excluded in vi.toml with that reason. Not proposed: the military ranks, which the deputies' data writes in the position, never the name, and the monks' titles (Hòa thượng, Thượng tọa), full words found only in aliases.
  - **Accepted:** a lone name word reads as the family under the pack (`Anh` → family 'Anh'), rules/O5's convention under a family-first order, which no `Policy` field sets apart from the order (#1111 asks for one); 2 of the 6 CLDR renderings the pack loses are it (`Anh` and `Sinbad`, each in the givenFirst order; rules/O5's Accepted line), the other 4 being given-first renderings the order reverses (`Käthe Müller`, `Anh Nguyễn`). CLDR's per-pack view reads 16 of its 24 vi renderings by default and 10 with the pack.
  - **Kept: after a family comma the given part reads given-first under `FAMILY_FIRST_GIVEN_LAST`, as under every order,** so `Nguyen, Van Anh` gives given 'Van', middle 'Anh' with the pack as without it. FEC's donors with a common Vietnamese surname before the comma write the given part Western-style 69 times (`BUI, LONG VAN`) to 12 in native order (`NGUYEN, VAN HAHN`); UIT-ViNames and the deputies hold no comma names. Reopen if a corpus shows native-order comma names.

- **2026-11-06 #1115, the pack titles — THE TITLES NO NAME BEARS ARE READ BY DEFAULT.** Phase 3g wrote the Spanish and Portuguese titles `default = false` so that phase 3 moved no default parse, not for a collision, and left the default change for later, as #804 then made it for `profesor` (decisions/salutation-titles); phase 3f's Czech and Slovak doctorates and #1108's Vietnamese titles were written so on the same ground. #1115's mining of the words that open names with a period found them among the commonest, so the default now reads every one whose turning on moves no name it should not: 27 words, 36 readings (the nine doctorates in cs.toml and sk.toml both), listed in this file's Standing line dated 2026-11-06 #1115, plus nine titles the mining found unlisted, read by default from the start: es `licda`, `profr` (academic), `gral` and `tte` (military), cs `rsdr`, `thlic`, `thmgr` (academic, each with its `cased` mask) and `plk` and `npor` (military), each under 100 US Census bearers, under 5 US SSA births a year and with no Wikidata given-name statement (`tools/name_usage.py --boxes`, 2026-11-06).
  - **Kept the choice's,** each read against its moves over every local corpus: `maestra` (a schoolteacher in Spanish and Italian, and a family name, its note's reason), `doña` (a family name), `mga` (`MGA Dinusha`, `MGA Thilanka`: Sri Lankan initials, two ORCID names moved to title), `bca` (en.toml's credential), `pan`, `mgr`'s degree (the default reads Monsignor), and vi `gs`, `ts` and `pgs`, each written as initials (`NGO, TS`, `DUSSEAU, TS` and `TS BOYD, SAMUEL` in FEC, `GS Chowdhary`, `PGS Upeksha` in ORCID would lose their given names or initials to the title). Left unlisted from the mining: `mjr` (`MJR David` in ORCID, initials), `lae` (US Census 144 surname bearers, ORCID's given box 64), and `lcp` and `mvz` (three-letter capitals written as initials too, with no corpus evidence either way).
  - **Measured** with `uv run python tools/differential/config_diff.py diff --local all --committed --match '<the 36 words>' '<the tree with the 27 words' title readings removed and the nine left unlisted>' default` on 2026-11-06 against `e6a86bc9`: 312 of 8,529 matching names move, 219 of them in Mexico's DOF signatures (`Licenciado Juan Arroyo Martínez` → title 'Licenciado', given 'Juan'), 48 ClinicalTrials.gov officials (`Jan Štulik, MUDr` → title 'MUDr', given 'Jan', family 'Štulik', where the comma reversed the name), 26 ORCID, 11 cz-candidates (`JUDr. et Mgr. Zdeněk Michal` → title 'JUDr. et Mgr.', where the run of two titles read as the given name), 4 JRC, 2 PSC, 1 PatentsView and the one committed CLDR rendering `ĐBQH Nguyễn Văn Anh`, whose `title` now disagrees with CLDR's, which files `ĐBQH` as a suffix, a disagreement unchanged, while its family and given names come to agree. Read by eye, 287 of the 312 change a name field (given, middle or family), a title leaving it; the other 25 move a title from the suffix to the title field alone: 14 Czech doctorates after a comma (`Lukas Rob, Prof., MUDr., Csc.` → title 'Prof. MUDr.', suffix 'Csc.'), which moves the `credentials` view too, `Mgr. Libor Vejs, plk.v.v.`, and the 10 licentiates below. The costs: a licentiate written after a comma moves from the suffix to the title, Nordic or Latin (`Teemu Elomaa, Lic.Med`, `Erik Blennow Nordström, PhD, Lic.Psych.`, `Norberto da Costa Abi, Lic.Enf`, 10 names); a title written with its complement takes the title and leaves the complement as the given name (`Marina M Blanco-Aparicio, Doctora en Neumología` → given 'en'), the shape phase 3g left unread; a title glued to a name word by a period takes the word (`Lic.Yilian Martínez Valcárcel` → title 'Lic.Yilian'), as `Dr.` does; `Jenny Åström, lic psych` reads given 'psych'; and one name moves the other way, a credential into the family (`Dr. Fr Rinaldo DIRICCHARDI MUZGA - PhD; Lic.Theol.` → family 'Lic.Theol.', where 'PhD;' was). A family name written first is a cost too, the `graf` class (decisions/salutation-titles): ORCID's family box holds `Doctora` once and `Licenciado` once and PatentsView's holds `Lic` once (2026-11-06), none written first in its record, so none moves; a name written family first that opens with one reads it as the title.
  - **The tests** that held each word as a choice's moved: the locale rotator rows of these words became `_READ_BY_DEFAULT_SINCE_1115` in tests/v2/test_locales.py, which holds each pack reading the row as the default now does; the tests that used `lic` and `judr` as words only a choice turns on use `maestra` and `mga`, which still are, each checked to read under the pack and the default as the old word read on master.
- **2026-11-07 #1148, what a strict choice keeps of a native-script word — A STRICT CHOICE KEEPS A NATIVE-SCRIPT WORD'S READINGS, NAME READINGS INCLUDED, AS A PLAIN CHOICE OF THE SAME LANGUAGES KEEPS THEM.** SUPERSEDES: the 2026-10-30 #753, file-strict entry's "native-script vocabulary" (its item 1 reads "everything else leaves, vocabulary and name readings alike"), the 2026-10-31 #753, phase 3j head's and the 2026-11-02 #1005 head's, in part: name readings are included. No parse moves, the built filter having read this way since 3j: the strict filter (`strict_pool`'s at 3j, `_reading_index.strict_kept` since #1001) has kept every reading of a word with a letter and no Latin letter whatever file holds it, name readings among them, and `choose` then runs as under a plain choice, so a `default = false` reading stays off unless its file is chosen. Measured 2026-11-07 with `_reading_index.chosen_readings`, plain against strict: for every tag a strict choice accepts, alone and in pairs, a native-script word's readings were the same under both. Strict `hu` keeps `uk.toml`'s family `пан`, `hi.toml`'s family `बाबू` and `ar.toml`'s given `أبو`; strict `pt` reads `गुरु प्रसाद शर्मा` title `गुरु`, `ne.toml`'s given reading of it being `default = false`, and strict `ne` reads given `गुरु`.
- **2026-11-08 #1003 — THE 2.x TRANSLATION IS DELETED, AS THE 2026-11-02 #754, THE 2.x TRANSLATION'S LIFETIME ENTRY DECIDED FOR 3.1: A 2.x `Lexicon` PICKLE IS REFUSED, AND AN OLD SPELLING RAISES A FIXED `TypeError`; NO PARSE MOVES.** SUPERSEDES: item 5 of the 2026-11-02 #754, the `Lexicon` surface entry (a 2.x `Lexicon` state translated onto the nearer named base and a log), item 6's per-word translation of an old spelling and its Declined bullet's refusal of option (a), which 3.1 adopts; the 2026-11-02 #754, sub-phase 4i-b entry's translated refusals and 2.x pickle load, which built them; and the 2026-11-02 #754, a marker left behind entry, whose contract was the translation's. Carries out that entry's 3.1 bullet. `_lexicon`'s `_target_readings`, `_MARKER_READ_WITH` and `_inert_dropped`, `_edits_toward`, `_translation`, `_receiver`, `Lexicon._changed`, `_translated_2x` and the 2.x pickle's `DeprecationWarning` are gone, about 500 lines, and `_warn_at_caller` and `_normset` lose the parameters only they passed. `Lexicon.__setstate__` refuses a state holding `titles`, the field every 2.x layout has and no 3.0 one does, with `ValueError`, its remedy told by the state's layout, since a caller seldom knows which release wrote a pickle: the layout 3.0 loaded (every 2.x field but `given_names`, and no other) is told to load it under 3.0 and pickle the loaded object again, any other to build the object again. Only 2.7 and 2.8 wrote that layout. Measured 2026-11-08: `Lexicon.default()` pickled by the 2.0.0, 2.1.0, 2.2.0, 2.3.0, 2.4.0, 2.4.2, 2.5.0 and 2.6.0 wheels fails to load under 3.0.1 as missing fields, and pickled by the 2.7.0, 2.8.0 and 2.8.2 wheels loads; recipe: in a directory outside the checkout, `PYTHONSAFEPATH=1 uv run --isolated --no-project --with 'nameparser==<v>' python -c "import pickle, nameparser; assert nameparser.__version__ == '<v>'; open('lex_<v>.pickle', 'wb').write(pickle.dumps(nameparser.Lexicon.default()))"` for each version, then `pickle.loads` each file under `--with 'nameparser==3.0.1'` the same way, and under the tree, where each gets its layout's message. A `Parser` or locale pack holding one is refused at the same place, since each loads its `Lexicon` through it. `add`, `remove`, `Lexicon(...)` and `dataclasses.replace` with a set field raise `TypeError` naming the field and docs/migrate.rst's table of kinds; the list and mask refusals, which never translated, keep their code to paste. tests/v2/data/lexicon_v28.pickle stays, the refusal test's input. `tests/v2/test_snapshot.py` holds every corpus reading unchanged, no default parse reaching the code. Left standing: en.toml's six-word ceiling on `hon`'s `attaches` (decisions/H9's #999 entry), whose reason was a refusal message offering a translated list as code to paste; the clash message `_built` raises still bounds a list at six words, but shows the caller's own call, so the ceiling no longer binds and is not widened here.
